# DECISIONS.md

Decisions log of the Aron rebuild. Owner: the doc 14 author (see `docs/14-master-build-plan.md` s9.2). Date of every row below is 2026-10-04 unless the change log at the end says otherwise. The rows are the binding decisions of the plan documents `docs/14` to `docs/21`; a one-page "how to use" is at the end of this file.

Status words (exact):

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| LOCKED-BY-SPEC | CLAUDE.md or docs/01 to 13 already decide it, or hard evidence (data profile, manuals) closes it. State it, do not reopen. |
| DEFAULT | We proceed with this unless AKTCL objects. Changing it is an amendment (change log). If it differs from the current Apsis behaviour it is listed under "Deliberately changed". |
| MUST-CONFIRM (by N) | The default in the row lets the build proceed, but sub-milestone N cannot exit (or a wave cannot start) until the business answers. The proceed-with default is stated in the schedule below and in `docs/14` s4. |

Counts: 543 decisions in total (D-01 to D-269 from the plan skeleton, D-300 to D-312 raised by doc 14, 159 raised by docs 15 to 21 and copied in section M2, 54 raised by the round-2 review in section M3, 48 raised by the round-3 review in section M4): LOCKED-BY-SPEC 57, DEFAULT 313, MUST-CONFIRM 173 (101 before the copy, 50 added by M2, 12 added by M3, 10 added by M4). The "Status" column shows the single gating status (a row that is locked for one part and must be confirmed for another shows MUST-CONFIRM, with the full original status in the "Why" cell).

Evidence files that the decisions, gates and gaps cite are to be committed read-only under `/docs/evidence/manuals/` and `/docs/evidence/verification/` (D-530, gate T-0-154). They are NOT yet in the repository at the date of this file; the sponsor commits them before kickoff on 2026-10-11 and a gate that cites a file that is not there stays pending-oracle (D-554, `docs/14` s9.2b). Precedence: docs 14 to 21 and this file bind and win over docs 01 to 13 and db/schema.sql wherever they differ (D-534); the ready-to-paste text for CLAUDE.md, the README and the superseded-by banners is in `docs/14` s9.2b and is applied by the sponsor, not by this planning pass.

Contents: A to L decisions D-01 to D-269; M decisions raised while writing D-300 to D-312; M2 decisions raised by docs 15 to 21 (D-320 to D-491); M3 decisions raised by the round-2 review (D-500 to D-553); M4 decisions raised by the round-3 review (D-554 to D-601); N reserved ranges; O MUST-CONFIRM schedule; P deliberately changed from Apsis; Q question registry; R rejected register recommendations; S change log; how to use this file.

| D-02 | DEFAULT | API is Node.js (current LTS) + TypeScript + NestJS on Fastify; PostgreSQL 16; data access with Kysely (typed SQL); plain-SQL forward-only migrations with checksums (dbmate-style runner); no ORM-generated migrations | docs/13 Q2; D-qa-07 | 2026-10-04 | Q2 | 16, 20 |

## B. Data model, money, units, day state (D-15 to D-43)

| ID | Status | Decision | Why and source | Date | Answers (Q / MQ) | Docs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-15 | DEFAULT | Money is bigint milli-taka (column suffix _mtk, constant MONEY_SCALE = 1000); db/schema.sql _minor columns are renamed in migration 0001; the seed CSV loads as round(price x 1000); 23 seed price values on 20 of the 42 SKUs have three decimals (distributor 7.935 on 20 of them) so paisa cannot hold them; no floats anywhere | seed finding 1; V-money; REG s0.3 [Status as stated: DEFAULT (changes CLAUDE.md "minor units" from paisa to milli-taka)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 16 |
| D-16 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 2a) | Quantity model on every quantity-bearing row: qty_entered + unit_entered + pack_factor + qty_base; base units: stick (cigarette, bidi), piece (lighter), dozen (match); report_unit and report_factor per SKU (Lighter: Pcs on web, Box on the TSO app; Match: Dozen); STD sums qty_base only inside one SKU or one category, never across categories | V-money G-man-001; MQ-01 [Status as stated: LOCKED-BY-SPEC for cigarette and bidi (sticks: docs/22 P-04 and manual stock badge 6,500 sticks = 650 packs); MUST-CONFIRM (by 2a) for lighter box size and match entry unit] | 2026-10-04 | Q8, MQ-01 | 16, 17 |
| D-17 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 2a) | Cigarette and bidi quantity is entered, stored and displayed in sticks; the stepper step is the pack size (loose sticks allowed or not is MUST-CONFIRM); the purple pack badge is read-only qty / pack_size; prices are per stick; Match price per dozen is stored as a rational (28.00 per dozen), never as 2.33 per piece; every Match quantity cell shows a unit label. Replaces the plan assumption "SRs enter packs" | V-money G-man-001 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 2a): stepper and loose sticks (MQ-02)] | 2026-10-04 | Q8, MQ-02 | 15, 16, 17 |
| D-18 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 2a) | Memo net = gross - offer_discount - drp_discount (slide) - qc_deduction. Quantity totals are not reduced by slide or QC; the slide reward unit is a memo_line with is_free and offer_id so volume and stock include it (confirm). The CHECK is amended; both printed and on-screen lines show every non-zero component. Zero sale plus QC can make the net negative: cfg.memo.allow_negative_net default true (stored as a credit) | V-money G-man-002 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 2a): negative-net rule and the max-QC basis (MQ-03, MQ-04)] | 2026-10-04 | MQ-03, MQ-04 | 15, 16, 17 |
| D-19 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 2a) | Rounding: totals are summed from unrounded line values and rounded once (AMO fixtures 116.42 and 289.50; SR fixtures 360.50 and 161.50); per-line display rounding is never stored; round_adj_mtk stored so the printed total is reproducible; cfg.memo.rounding_mode default half_up_paisa, future-dated only | V-money G-man-001; G-sync-04; G-feat-46 [Status as stated: MUST-CONFIRM (by 2a: must equal Apsis to the paisa; gate T-2-41)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 16, 17, 20 |
| D-20 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 1a) | Every transaction stores UTC timestamps plus business_date (Asia/Dhaka, cutoff 00:00). Trusted capture time: monotonic anchor (server_time + elapsedRealtime + boot_id) is primary, the clock offset at last contact is the fallback, raw device time last; business_date and business_date_server derive from the trusted time; rows claiming a closed month on untrusted time are parked (DQ-40) | CLAUDE.md 7; D-sec-09; D-sync-13 [Status as stated: LOCKED-BY-SPEC (Dhaka business date); DEFAULT (anchor method, resolves G-fraud-02); MUST-CONFIRM (by 1a): 00:00 cutoff (Q29)] | 2026-10-04 | Q29 | 16, 17, 21 |

| D-22 | LOCKED-BY-SPEC | Synced transactional rows are immutable except server enrichment columns; every correction is a new row or event (supersedes, against, memo_void, due_collection, admin data_void); a voided client_uuid stays in ingest_registry as a tombstone so a late upload is rejected (voided_by_admin) and never resurrects a number | docs/03, docs/04; V-day G-man-086 | 2026-10-04 | - | 16, 17 |

| ID | Status | Decision | Why and source | Date | Answers (Q / MQ) | Docs |

## D. Sync, offline, battery, device and app (D-59 to D-86)

| ID | Status | Decision | Why and source | Date | Answers (Q / MQ) | Docs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-59 | LOCKED-BY-SPEC | Immediate sync (R5) is event-driven, never polled: T1 debounce 5 s after the last local write with a family hold (a visit and its children leave together; hold max 180 s); T2 app foreground; T3 validated connectivity regained (HEAD check, captive Wi-Fi counts as offline); T4 WorkManager one-off with NetworkType.CONNECTED enqueued whenever a send failed for lack of network; T5 WorkManager periodic 15 min (registered only while rows or photos are pending); T6 manual Sync; T7 Sales Submit; T8 Wi-Fi connected for the media queue. No foreground service, no socket, no AlarmManager, no timer shorter than 60 s | L-sync s2; D-sync-10 [Status as stated: LOCKED-BY-SPEC (R5, R4)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 17 |
| D-60 | DEFAULT | POST /sync/batch carries one ordered flat records[] list (type, family_uuid, rank, client_uuid, payload) with envelope fields batch_uuid, device_uuid, schema_version, device counts per type and date; the response carries accepted, rejected (reason_code, retryable), conflicts, parked, server_totals, day_states, hold_s, X-Config-Version, X-Server-Generation; requests carry X-Batch-Attempt, X-Pending-Rows and X-Last-Sync-Error. This replaces the per-type arrays in docs/09 (the new system defines its own API) | L-sync s2.4; D-sync-09; C-sre G-18/19 | 2026-10-04 | - | 17, 15 |
| D-61 | DEFAULT | Ingest is synchronous into PostgreSQL and returns authoritative accepted counts (no queue in front of the database); everything after commit (aggregation, geo re-check, plausibility, photo linking) runs from a transactional outbox worker, idempotent per dirty (business_date, route) key; aggregates are recomputed, never incremented | D-scale-1/2 | 2026-10-04 | - | 17, 18 |
| D-62 | LOCKED-BY-SPEC | Batch-level idempotency: device-generated batch_uuid; the response is persisted in the ingest transaction (table sync_batch.response) and Redis is only a cache of it; a repeat within 24 h replays the stored response; replay is keyed by (device_id, batch_uuid) so another device never receives it; the same batch_uuid with a different row set returns 409 | D-scale-4; G-scale-01; G-sre-14 [Status as stated: LOCKED-BY-SPEC (CLAUDE.md 2)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 17, 18 |
| D-63 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 7c) | Server generation: every response carries X-Server-Generation (minted at DR promotion or PITR restore); when it changes the device flips outbox rows acked within cfg.sync.resync_window_h (24) back to pending and re-sends; registry dedupe absorbs it. This is what makes DR and PITR usable | D-sre-01; G-sre-01 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 7c): business tolerance for counts rising again after failover (Q54)] | 2026-10-04 | Q54 | 17, 18 |
| D-64 | DEFAULT | Sales Submit, check-out and day_open are outbox events (they complete offline); day_submit is always the LAST record of the day's outbox sequence; the server defers the sales_submitted transition until its totals are at least the device counts claimed in the event (cfg.day.submit_settle_timeout_min 30) and shows submit_pending_rows meanwhile; submit_count_mismatch is evaluated only after settle; the rollback trigger "mismatch above 2% of routes" reads it after settle | D-sync-08; D-sre-02; G-sre-02 | 2026-10-04 | - | 17, 18, 16 |
| D-65 | DEFAULT | Poison-row isolation: ingest uses per-record savepoints; a record that throws becomes rejected(server_error) plus a quarantine row, never a 500; the client counts a family_fail_count and skips ahead after cfg.sync.family_skip_after (5) so the rest of the day uploads; sync_rejected has admin retry, fix-and-accept, discard | D-sre-03; G-sre-03 | 2026-10-04 | - | 17, 16 |

## F. Security, privacy and fraud (D-101 to D-124)

| ID | Status | Decision | Why and source | Date | Answers (Q / MQ) | Docs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-101 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 0c) | Tokens: access JWT ES256 with kid, 60 min TTL (web 15 min) with jitter plus or minus 10 min and refresh piggybacked under 5 min to expiry, 60 s grace on /sync/batch, expiry judged on corrected time; refresh token opaque 256-bit, stored hashed, rotated each use with reuse detection (60 s grace replays the stored response), sliding 30 days and absolute 90 days since the last password login; claims carry role, top scope nodes, scope_version; the server expands reach itself | D-sec-01; D-sre-04; G-sre-06 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 0c): lifetimes (Q4)] | 2026-10-04 | Q4 | 21, 17 |
| D-102 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 0c) | Passwords: Argon2id on the server; web policy at least 12 characters, mixed case and a digit, not one of the last 10, not within 24 h of the last change (docs/09, PARITY); field roles at least 8 characters with a deny-list (proposed because 8,500 reps type on shared phones); lockout 10 attempts per username in 15 min with doubling cool-down keyed by (username, device) so a bound device with proof bypasses it (no denial of service); uniform errors; temporary password for resets (24 h, forces change); no self-service reset on the web today (PARITY, reset via TSO or support); web "Remember me" cfg.auth.web_remember_me_days default 0 | docs/09; V-web G-man-091; G-fraud-12 [Status as stated: LOCKED-BY-SPEC (web policy); MUST-CONFIRM (by 0c): field-role policy (D-sec-13)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 21 |
| D-103 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 0c) | Device-binding OTP: created by the server when an unknown device attempts login; the TSO sees it in the SR Device OTP panel (view only, superset of the 8 SR-manual columns, scoped Wing to Zone filters, search, refresh) and may re-issue as an admin improvement; default 4 digits (parity), TTL cfg.auth.otp_ttl_min 120, 5 attempts then expired; stored encrypted and reversible (AES-GCM, key wrapped by Key Vault), NOT hashed, so the TSO can read it; online-only; binds user to device, needs a password-authenticated bind_required token; a bind from an unseen device model is flagged; bulk pre-issue per zone for wave days is audited | V-device G-man-021; G-feat-11 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; the "server creates it" part is inference; MUST-CONFIRM (by 0c): TTL, retries, whether the AMO app binds (MQ-15)] | 2026-10-04 | Q4, MQ-15 | 21, 19 |
| D-104 | DEFAULT | Device proof: at bind the app registers an EC P-256 key held in Android Keystore (StrongBox when present); X-Device-Proof signs refresh, bind and batch requests; every device-originated record carries an ES256 signature over canonical payload, client_uuid, captured_elapsed_ms and boot_id: record mode in the pilot, enforce before wave 1; devices without a hardware key are accepted with trust_level low | D-fraud-02/03; G-fraud-03 | 2026-10-04 | - | 21, 17 |
| D-105 | DEFAULT | Keystore attestation chain and attestationApplicationId are verified at bind: genuine AKTCL-signed build gives trust_level normal, a repack or emulator gives low with weighted scoring; never a hard block alone | G-fraud-04 | 2026-10-04 | - | 21 |
| D-106 | LOCKED-BY-SPEC | Scope is enforced twice: a request-scoped ScopeContext and scoped() repository helper (lint rule no-unscoped-query) as the primary control and PostgreSQL row-level security (SET LOCAL app.user_id) on transactional and PII tables as defence in depth, with a 10 percent p95 budget (T-4-70); the client never sends scope ids; every foreign key in a write body is resolved through ScopeContext | D-sec-04 [Status as stated: LOCKED-BY-SPEC (CLAUDE.md 4)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 21, 16 |
| D-107 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 7a entry) | PII classification: NID, TIN and trade licence are envelope-encrypted (AES-256-GCM, DEK wrapped in Key Vault, never searched, never in bundles or dw.dim_outlet); phone, owner name and address stay plaintext in app.outlet behind grants, a masked BI view, redaction in logs, list row budgets and an export log, pending legal review; docs/22 P-12 shows phone and owner are the real exposure and the NID placeholder 123 is treated as null | D-sec-06; docs/22 P-12 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 7a entry): legal opinion on sensitivity and localisation (G-sec-01, G-sec-04)] | 2026-10-04 | Q19 | 21, 16 |
| D-108 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 4c) | Role x subject PII matrix: the server returns no SR phone to an AMO view (11 asterisks parity); retailer phones stay visible on route-scoped field screens (label "name (code-phone-cluster)"); TSO web parity baseline shows Address, NID, TIN and Trade Licence columns (null where blank or the placeholder) with every Excel export logged in report_export_log; restriction possible through cfg.pii.field_roles | V-outlets G-man-062; V-web G-man-039 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 4c): whether the live Excel contains NID/TIN (ask for a real file)] | 2026-10-04 | Q30 | 21, 15 |
| D-109 | DEFAULT | Fraud model: server-side signals only (catalogue FS-01 to FS-29 in doc 21), stored in risk_signal and surfaced on AMO and TSO Exceptions and the web Exceptions report; nothing is auto-reversed or auto-deleted; supervisor review actions (reviewed, dismissed, confirmed) are idempotent events; AMO dismissals are re-sampled to the TSO; fraud thresholds (cfg.fraud.*) are owned by cfg.sec at class C2 or higher with floors and ceilings and a dead-signal watch | D-sec-08; G-fraud-10/20 | 2026-10-04 | - | 21, 19 |
| D-110 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 2d) | Geo-spoof defence beyond mock flags: collect passive radio environment at fix time (serving and neighbour cell identities, visible Wi-Fi BSSID hashes when a recent system scan exists) into geo_fix.radio_env; rules for cell/position mismatch, GNSS time anomaly, stale or synthetic fix shape, co-located users; battery cost zero (passive reads on an existing fix); privacy class same as the fix | G-fraud-01/25 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 2d): privacy sign-off (G-fraud-01)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 21, 16 |
| D-111 | DEFAULT | Outlet-location change controls: a move above 300 m on a confirmed outlet requires TSO approval; verification of a location change needs the AMO's own fix within the radius of the proposed point or the request is flagged remote_verification; a proposed point within 500 m of the requester's check-in centroid is flagged moved_to_home; placeholder-pin outlets are corrected by the AMO alone | G-fraud-06; DQ-37 | 2026-10-04 | - | 21, 19 |
| D-112 | DEFAULT | Supervisor takeover chain: a password reset plus OTP issue for the same user by the same actor within 24 h, or a bind of a device previously bound to the actor, holds the bind pending a second person and alerts DMO and security_admin; the SR is notified of a reset | G-fraud-08 | 2026-10-04 | - | 21 |
| D-113 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 6b) | Audit: append-only app.audit_log, cfg.config_change_audit, security_event and report_export_log (REVOKE plus trigger plus hash chain), exported daily to an immutable (WORM) Blob container with 7-year retention (ASSUMPTION), PII-read and export events logged; no shared admin accounts | D-sec-10 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 6b): retention (legal)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 21 |
| D-114 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 4c) | Web authentication: access token in memory and refresh in an HttpOnly SameSite=Strict cookie (BFF) with Origin checks; TOTP MFA mandatory for the admin bundles (cfg.auth.mfa_required_roles default admin; recommended admin, top, wm); Microsoft Entra ID SSO for DMO, WM, top and admin if AKTCL has it | D-sec-11 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 4c): Entra availability (G-sec-10)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 21 |
| D-115 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 2e) | Microphone: the apps request camera only and NOT RECORD_AUDIO (the Audio prompt in the manual is a camera-plugin side effect and no recording UI exists); if call recording is a real feature it needs a consent design first | V-device G-man-020; D-sec-14 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 2e): Q15, MQ-55] | 2026-10-04 | Q15, MQ-55 | 21, 17 |
| D-116 | DEFAULT | API limits: per-device and per-user rate limits in Redis (cfg.api.rl.*; WAF per-IP thresholds only as a high backstop because carrier-grade NAT puts thousands of SRs behind one IP); batch body at most 1 MiB compressed, 8 MiB decompressed with a 20:1 ratio guard, at most 500 rows, memo lines at most 60; unknown JSON keys rejected; error envelope without stack or SQL | G-scale-04/G-sec-15 | 2026-10-04 | - | 21, 18 |
| D-117 | LOCKED-BY-SPEC | Server recomputes what it can: geo verdict, prices (price_mismatch flag, never reject a printed memo), totals (reject on arithmetic), points, business date; qty multiple-of-pack and large quantity are flags; coordinates outside the Bangladesh bounding box are flagged | L-security s6.4 [Status as stated: LOCKED-BY-SPEC (CLAUDE.md 5, docs/05)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 21, 16 |
| D-118 | DEFAULT | Injection and rendering safety: export cell sanitiser against spreadsheet formulas, device- and server-side stripping of control and bidi/format characters in names reaching the printer, approval panel and dedupe, NFC normalisation and Bengali digit normalisation (G-field-10) at input and ingest | G-fraud-14 | 2026-10-04 | - | 21, 16 |
| D-119 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 7a) | Apsis credentials: treated as data to migrate or rotate, never reused against a live service; if the dump carries verifiable password hashes (algorithm and parameters known) the importer verifies then re-hashes at first login, otherwise wave day uses TSO-issued temporary passwords per SR (forced change); same usernames either way | CLAUDE.md guardrail; G-sec-03 | 2026-10-04 | Q18 | 21, 16 |
| D-120 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 7a entry) | Employee-location notice: an in-app Bangla and English notice at first login states that location is recorded only at check-in and out, outlet open, force sale and outlet capture; acceptance stored (user_consent); raw fixes are rounded to 3 decimals in dw after 730 days | L-security s4.1 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 7a entry): HR and legal text (G-sec-22)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 21 |
| D-121 | DEFAULT | PII read budgets: list endpoints mask personal columns by default with a logged reveal, hourly and daily row budgets per user, exports above a threshold need approval and carry a watermark sheet | D-fraud-10; G-fraud-13 | 2026-10-04 | - | 21 |
| D-122 | DEFAULT | Supply chain: actions pinned by SHA, OIDC only, CodeQL, Semgrep, gitleaks, Dependabot and OSV, Trivy, cosign-signed images, SBOM; APK upload key separate from the signing key; independent reproducible rebuild of the release tag before publish; release publish is a human action; the lab self-hosted runner only runs protected-tag device-lab jobs | G-fraud-16; L-security s10 | 2026-10-04 | - | 21, 20 |

## G. Scale, reliability and Azure operations (D-125 to D-140)

| ID | Status | Decision | Why and source | Date | Answers (Q / MQ) | Docs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-125 | LOCKED-BY-SPEC | Load model inputs are the data-profile values, not docs/01: peak 346,772 successful calls on one day, design point 4.5 lakh calls and 5 lakh visits per day, about 2.0 lines per call on average (design 2.5; p99 4, max 40), 11,336 routes, about 280 rows per SR-day, 2.2 million business rows per day; full table in s6 | P-01/P-02; C-sre s1.1 [Status as stated: LOCKED-BY-SPEC (docs/22)] | 2026-10-04 | - | 18 |
| D-126 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 7b) | Pre-bind day: every wave has a day T-1 in which the TSOs gather the wave's SRs at the distribution house on Wi-Fi to install, bind, log in once, pre-fetch tomorrow's bundle and test-print, so day one is a small DELTA bundle of about 25 KB, not refresh plus 304 (amended by D-557); a per-replica password-hash concurrency limiter (cfg.auth.hash_concurrency_per_replica 4, 503 plus Retry-After) and a 2 vCPU / 4 GiB wave-morning api revision protect against the Argon2id memory storm | G-sre-04; D-sre-05 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 7b): operationally feasible (Q52)] | 2026-10-04 | Q52 | 18, 20 |
| D-127 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 7c) | DR posture: RTO 4 h and RPO 15 min for a region outage (cross-region async replica plus Bicep redeploy), PITR RTO 2 h, both made safe by the server-generation re-sync (D-63); a Front Door secondary origin group in the DR region; Key Vault soft-delete recovery in the drill | D-scale-5; D-sre-01 | 2026-10-04 | Q20, Q40, Q54 | 18 |
| D-128 | DEFAULT | Read path: dashboards, reports, Excel and BI read the in-region replica with an "as of" stamp; live-state tiles (route_day, final-submit validation, device page) read the primary; Redis response cache 30 s keyed by scope hash; sync-health has a degraded mode (zone level, 120 s refresh) when replica lag exceeds 60 s | D-sre-08 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18, 16 |
| D-129 | DEFAULT | Per-user bundle snapshots are pre-generated by the job DAG (D-71), served with ETag/304 and live ?since deltas; invalidation by scope on assignment, outlet, price, offer, target or config change (debounced 60 s, capped); a pre-generation failure falls back to yesterday's snapshot plus delta (bundle_hold), not to live generation | D-scale-3; G-sre-08 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18, 17 |
| D-130 | DEFAULT | Kill-switch semantics: min_version blocks a new day's login; blocked_versions blocks new captures; neither ever blocks upload of captured rows or wipes data; the single exception is the version-scoped sync hold (cfg.ops.sync_hold_by_version, enforced at the Front Door, auto-expiring, rows stay on the device) for an upload loop; every operational switch has a mandatory duration | D-scale-9; D-sre-11 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18, 19 |
| D-131 | DEFAULT | Storage and partitions: SSD v2 has no autogrow (alert at 70 percent, first grow planned by month 6 (amended from month 12, then month 9, by doc 18 s1.9 and D-568; growth is about 2.8 GB a day on 1,024 GiB disks)); partitions created by a job with a default partition and an alert on rows landing in it; archive job writes a manifest and drops only after verified upload | G-scale-08; C-sre s1.1 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18, 16 |
| D-132 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 6c) | Blob: ZRS for bundles, apk and content; read-access geo-zone-redundant (RA-GZRS) for photos and audit-export so evidence stays readable in a region outage; lifecycle Hot to Cool 30 d to Cold 90 d to Archive 365 d; reference photos exempt; one user-delegation key cached per replica (refresh at 6 days or on 403) | G-sre-13; L-scale s2.6 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 6c): photo retention (Q21)] | 2026-10-04 | Q21 | 18, 21 |
| D-133 | DEFAULT | Pre-scale schedule is data (cfg.ops.prescale_schedule per wing, default 06:15 and 16:45 to 8 api replicas, worker 3, web 4); quotas are requested in Phase 0 (ACA cores at least 64, 128 for the full fleet; PostgreSQL at least 48 vCores; Azure Load Testing at least 10 engines) with alerts at 80 percent | G-scale-11; G-sre-25 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18 |
| D-134 | DEFAULT | Load testing is Azure Load Testing with a Locust device simulator on staging scaled to the production SKU only for the test window, plus a k6 smoke on every main build; scenarios S1 to S10 rebased on D-125; the full-fleet gate is 1.5 times the fleet | D-scale-12; G-sre-05 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18, 20 |
| D-135 | DEFAULT | SLOs (07:00 to 21:00 Dhaka): auth/sync/day availability 99.9 percent; bundle p95 at most 2 s server time; trickle ack p95 at most 1.5 s; catch-up (200 rows) p95 at most 8 s; sync success at least 99.5 percent within 3 attempts; reconciliation mismatch at most 0.1 percent of route-days after settle; aggregation lag p95 at most 60 s; dashboards p95 at most 1 s; SLIs measured at the edge as well as the API; burn-rate alerts and calendar-aware baselines (Friday, holidays) | L-scale s5.1; G-sre-19 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18 |
| D-136 | DEFAULT | Telemetry: server-side adaptive sampling (100 percent of errors, 429, 5xx, slow above 2 s; 5 percent of 2xx); device telemetry only inside the sync batch with cfg.telemetry.device_max_bytes_per_day 1024; sync summaries in a cap-exempt workspace table; the daily cap sized three times for wave days with an 80 percent alert | G-scale-13; G-sre-24 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18 |
| D-137 | DEFAULT | PgBouncer transaction-mode rules: no named prepared statements, no LISTEN on 6432, SET LOCAL only inside transactions; config cache invalidation uses Redis pub/sub with a 30 s poll fallback; graceful shutdown drains 30 s | G-sre-15 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18, 19 |
| D-138 | MUST-CONFIRM (by 7c) | Support visibility: the server records per-device observed state from request headers (X-Pending-Rows, X-Last-Sync-Error) at most once per 10 min, POST /support/ping on demand, a device-lookup workbook with an L1 read-only role; support code on every blocking screen | G-sre-18; G-field-15 [Status as stated: DEFAULT; MUST-CONFIRM (by 7c): L1 access model (Q55)] | 2026-10-04 | Q55 | 18, 20 |
| D-139 | DEFAULT | ACA api sizing and probes: 1 vCPU / 2 GiB, HTTP rule concurrentRequests 30; startup, liveness (process only) and readiness (DB only, 2 s; a Redis failure is reported as degraded, never unready, amended by D-417 because Redis fails open on field paths) probes; statement_timeout per DB role (api 15 s, worker 10 min, web 60 s); lock_timeout 3 s for api; pool 8 per api replica and 4 per worker or web | L-scale s2.4/2.5 | 2026-10-04 | - | 18 |
| D-140 | DEFAULT | Worker poll interval cfg.agg.poll_interval_s: 5 s between 06:00 and 23:00 Dhaka, 60 s otherwise; claim batch 500; claim timeout 300 s; dead items surface on sync-health with re-drive | G-sre-17; cross-lens X7 | 2026-10-04 | - | 16, 18 |

## M3. Raised by the round-2 review (D-500 to D-553)

Three skeptics reviewed documents 14 to 21 against the sponsor requirements R1 to R6 and the process requirement and raised 61 gaps (G-qa-25 to G-qa-85; skeptic gap n is G-qa-(24+n)). Each row below names the gaps it closes in the "Why and source" cell; three gaps (skeptic gaps 39, 40 and 42) were small corrections made in place and have no decision. 42 rows are DEFAULT and 12 are MUST-CONFIRM. Amendments to earlier rows are marked in those rows and listed in the change log (S).

## M4. Raised by the round-3 review (D-554 to D-601)

A third review of documents 14 to 21 raised 54 gaps (G-qa-86 to G-qa-139; review gap n is G-qa-(85+n)). Each row below names the gaps it closes in the "Why and source" cell; seven decisions close two gaps each (D-554, D-556, D-558, D-563, D-566, D-576 and D-583) and D-601 repeats gap 14 of D-576, so 48 decisions close 54 gaps. 38 rows are DEFAULT and 10 are MUST-CONFIRM (D-555, D-560, D-561, D-562, D-565, D-569, D-570, D-584, D-589, D-592; `docs/14` s4.2c). ## P. Deliberately changed from Apsis, for AKTCL sign-off

Parity first: every row below is a place where the new system differs from what the manuals or docs/13 show. The constraint named in the "Why" column is what forces it. Detail and the sign-off sub-milestone are in `docs/14` s5.

| # | What differs | Why | Decision | Flag | Sign-off by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Scope is resolved on the server | CLAUDE.md 4 | D-106 | DELIBERATE CHANGE | 0c |
| 2 | Reads come from aggregates | R1; PROJECT-CONTEXT pain point | D-61, D-128, D-190 | DELIBERATE CHANGE | 4a |
| 3 | One codebase, three flavours | least retraining; CLAUDE.md stack | D-01, D-205 | DESIGN CHANGE (invisible to users) | 1a |
| 4 | Anti-spoofing is a supervisor-visible flag | CLAUDE.md 5; docs/05 | D-96, D-109, D-110, D-123 | DELIBERATE CHANGE | 2d |
| 5 | Battery and size budgets are release gates | CLAUDE.md 3; R4 | D-73 | DELIBERATE CHANGE | 1c |
| 6 | A photo does not silently move the outlet | CLAUDE.md 5; docs/05 | D-163, D-95, D-111 | DELIBERATE CHANGE | 2d |
| 7 | 'Live' team location is the last synced fix | CLAUDE.md 3 | D-170 | DELIBERATE CHANGE | 3a |

The manual register proposed these; verification against the PDF pages or the constraints changed them. This table never overrides a decision above; it records why a recommendation was not adopted as written.

## How to use this file

1. **Read the row, not the memory.** Before building anything that touches money, units, the day state, sync, scope, geofence, KPIs or printing, find its D-id here (search the topic word) and build to the "Decision" cell. A document or a lens that says something different is wrong; raise an amendment.
2. **Status tells you how far to trust it.** LOCKED-BY-SPEC: build it. DEFAULT: build it and expect it may be amended. MUST-CONFIRM: build the default now, and know that the named sub-milestone cannot exit until the business answers; the answer date is tracked in `docs/14` s4.
3. **Log what you assume.** Every new assumption, deviation or answer gets a row (or an amendment row in the change log) with the date and the reason, before the code that depends on it is merged. A decision raised by a plan document goes in its reserved block (section N); a decision raised by a build session goes to the doc 14 author with the same columns.
4. **Never edit a decision silently.** Status or text changes are made only by the doc 14 author, in the same commit as a change-log row. Rows are never deleted; a superseded decision keeps its row and the change log says what replaced it.
5. **Cite by id.** Code comments, commit messages, tests and gate evidence cite D-ids, F-ids, G-ids and T-ids exactly as written here; `rtm-check` (`docs/20` s8) fails the build when a cited id does not exist.
6. **Questions.** A business answer that arrives (Q, MQ) is recorded as an amendment of the decision that carries the default, with the answer, the date and who gave it; the confirmation schedule in `docs/14` s4 is updated in the same commit.
7. **Guardrails stay outside this file.** No credentials, keys or personal data are recorded here. Apsis credentials found in the dump are data to migrate or rotate (D-119); the Apsis backend and app are never called (D-312).

A missing secret fails startup with the variable name only. | docs/24 s6.4 names Key Vault references with managed identity; no per-request Key Vault dependency, fewer libraries. |

No in-app OTel SDK. | docs/24 s13.1; the agent auto-instruments Ktor/Netty/JDBC; avoids a second telemetry pipeline. |

The rotation key is derived from the JWT signing key (one Key Vault secret). The access token in a grace replay is freshly minted with the same claims. | s8.1 "gets the same replacement" without storing plaintext tokens. |

## 2026-10-07 — Owner decisions: Maven mirror, git governance, repository visibility
- **Maven mirror allowed** (owner, in the lead chat): `settings.gradle.kts` tries Google's official Maven Central mirror first, except on GitHub Actions (CI always resolves from real Central, the independent check) or when `ARON_NO_MIRROR` is set. Reason: lane containers share one IP that Central rate-limits with HTTP 429. Later hardening: Gradle dependency verification (checksums generated on CI from real Central), Day 6.
- **Git governance approved** (owner): `tools/github-governance.ps1` run by the laptop operator: `main` created and protected, baseline tag, `azure-stage` and `azure-prod` environments (names changed from staging and prod to match the deploy workflows, 2026-10-07); the lead opens a daily gate pull request INT to `main` (docs/30 s2 and s3).
- **Repository stays public until the product is complete** (owner): the owner makes it private afterwards. Before that, set a GitHub Actions spending limit (private repositories meter CI minutes). CodeQL and secret scanning are usable while it is public; CI gates must not depend on them (gitleaks, OSV-scanner, Semgrep).

## backend-reports lane (2026-10-07)
- D-REP-01 Aggregates are rebuilt per route-day from the transaction tables (pure function of source rows, idempotent), not incremented from event payloads; the outbox only says which route-days are dirty. Reason: late, voided and superseded records need no special case; running twice gives the same numbers.
- D-REP-02 Outbox reading is gap-safe: ids not seen are re-read until they commit or 60 s pass (rolled back). Reason: identity ids commit out of order (checker finding).
- D-REP-03 geo_valid uses the server verdict, falling back to the phone's verdict while the server re-check has not run. A user-day is suspicious when its open/confirmed risk-signal scores sum to at least cfg.geo.suspicious_score_threshold (default 50, s11.4).
- D-REP-04 `agg_daily_outlet` is one row per (date, outlet) across all routes; `route_id` is the outlet's own route.

## android-geo-dpc lane (2026-10-07)
- GD-11 `android/dpc` and `android/core-geo` may depend on `:shared:contract` (wire DTOs are the single truth); amends the docs/24 s2.1 module table (lead ruling 2026-10-07). Local mirrors of `EnrolDeviceResponse` and `DevicePolicy` are deleted once shared announces them.

## Design (2026-10-07)
- **Outdoor-first overrides glass** (owner): the owner wants the Apple-like glass look but said that where it could hurt legibility for salesmen in sunlight, the lead's recommendation applies, while keeping the apps fancy and of a clear standard. Decision: content surfaces in the field apps are solid, glass is limited to chrome, light theme is the field default, contrast is computed from tokens (7:1 key figures, 35 percent glare proxy), one-tap Sunlight mode (opaque, higher contrast and weight, larger type, thicker lines), web and admin keep full glass. Specified in `docs/32` section 2a; tested by a contrast unit test and screenshot tests; the owner's outdoor check is D-UI-01. Reason: a field app that cannot be read in the sun fails the core promise of no disruption to a selling day.

- 2026-10-07 D-REP-05: `memo-number-gaps` does not require a block to be full once a later block or overflow number exists (a lost block tail stays undetected). A user with several phone blocks may legitimately use block 2 before finishing block 1, so inferring a full block would raise false gaps. Revisit when the device-block binding history is available in dw. The report now computes gaps over the whole user-day series and filters by the caller's scope (a user-day with at least one in-scope number), and counts admin-voided numbers as used.
## android-sys lane (2026-10-07, accepted by the lead)
- AS-01 `media_meta` is sent after the blob upload succeeds (contract `MediaMetaPayload`: "sent after the blob upload succeeded"); the contract wins over the order in docs/24 s4.11 (record, then media_meta, then SAS and PUT). The referencing record still always syncs first.
- AS-02 AMO logout behaves like SR (keeps data, keeps uploading); only the TSO wipes, and only a fully reconciled device (docs/24 names SR and TSO only).
- AS-03 Rejected and quarantined outbox rows count as unsent for the TSO wipe: they are the phone's only copy until support has them.
- AS-04 The media queue is stored as atomic files (`files/media/u<id>/<uuid>.jpg` plus a JSON sidecar) instead of a Room table, so core-database stays android-core's; idempotent by media uuid, kill-safe.
- D-GEO-BG-01 (2026-10-07, lead ruling on docs/requests/android-geo-dpc-breadcrumb-background.md) `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` is declared only in the app-sr and app-amo manifests, for the optional breadcrumbs (N-035), never in app-tso or a library manifest. Reason: N-035's acceptance (8-hour day, screen mostly off) cannot pass without it, and docs/24 s10.2 is the specific binding text; F-SYS-023's acceptance names only the microphone. Conditions: the app never requests it at runtime unless `cfg.geo.breadcrumbs_enabled`; the DPC grants it only while that key is true and pins it denied otherwise; breadcrumbs stay off by default; core-system's `ManifestPermissionAuditTest` allows exactly those two manifests and fails anywhere else (microphone rule unchanged).
- D-DB-PART-01 (2026-10-07, lead ruling on db-partitioning-ruling.md): the nine high-volume capture tables stay unpartitioned through the pilot. Partitioning ships as its own forward-only migration batch in a scheduled window after the SR slice runs end to end on dev and before the staging deployment in the final account, owned by db with backend-core/reports/admin adjusting queries in the same window. Reason: the final account's database runs every migration from empty before any fleet data exists, so partitioning before staging rewrites nothing live; doing it now costs about a lane-day of four lanes mid-critical-path (docs/28 pilot size, docs/11 no-hiccup cutover). The held batch is listed in docs/status/db.md so it cannot be forgotten at the staging gate.

## backend-core lane, session 4 (2026-10-07)
- BC-41 F-SYS-012: the server re-check judges a visit at `max(captured_at, received_at - cfg.sys.config_accept_window_h)` (D-431): a config change made after the capture and inside the window is ignored, an older one applies. The outlet location is the latest `outlet_location_history` row valid by then, else the outlet row (seed and import rows have no history). Radius, accuracy and policy use the bundle's chain and clamps (role, wing, division, territory, geo class, zone, the outlet's current route, outlet), so phone and server agree on unchanged config. The re-check never refuses a visit; `GEO_DEVICE_SERVER_MISMATCH` is left to the risk-signal worker (F-SYS-057).
- BC-42 N-037 `POST /v1/admin/notifications`: senders are web ADMIN, SUPERADMIN, TOP, WM, DMO and TSO (the spec names none; AMO assigns tasks with their own push). A geography target must lie wholly in the caller's reach (403), a user or device target must be a user in reach (404). An announcement's title and body travel in the data message (there is no announcement table to pull them from); nothing else does. Without an FCM service account the send is accepted and audited but nothing leaves.
- BC-43 AUD-REL-01/02: config and generation caches refresh single-flight with a 2..30 s back-off after a failure; `/v1/health*` read cached values only (nil generation when cold). Transient database failures answer 503 ERR_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE with Retry-After 5..30. Not done here: the in-request retry for idempotent transactions and the 25 s request timeout (docs/18 s4.3).
- BC-44 AUD-SEC-02: lockout doubling is capped at 2 h in both stores (docs/21 s2.4 and D-102 win over the old 24 h cap; `cfg.auth.lockout_min` above 120 is therefore capped too). Only a phone the server knows hashes in the phones' Argon2 pool; web logins and unknown phones (non-PROD only) hash in a small pool (1 slot, 8 queued per replica). Web logins are limited per caller IP class (30 per minute) and by a replica backstop (300 per minute); unknown phones by their own backstop, never per IP (carrier NAT). Refresh: 20 per hour per phone, counted against the device the token's family is bound to after its proof verified, plus 600 per minute per caller IP class. Idle unlocked `auth_lockout` rows older than a day are purged, 20 per failure (doubling restarts after a quiet day). Open: a bound phone with a valid proof is still locked like any key; web lockout and the per-IP web limit see the BFF's address until the BFF sends a signed client IP (`docs/requests/backend-core-bff-client-ip.md`); the refresh window is fixed (no burst 5).
- BC-45 (2026-10-07, session 5) AUD-PERF-02: one platform interceptor (`RequestIsolation`, installed by `aronApi` after admission control) runs every call except `/v1/health` and `/v1/health/ready` on `Dispatchers.IO.limitedParallelism(write pool - 1)` (one connection stays free for the header lookups and readiness ping on IO; the Opus checker's nested-borrow deadlock does not occur because JDBI 3.55 reuses the thread's handle for a nested withHandle, proven with a pool of one in `BatchAcceptanceTest`) under a 25 s timeout (503 ERR_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, Retry-After 5..30). Chosen over wrapping each JDBI call in `Database.blocking{}`: it covers every lane's routes without editing them and a new route cannot forget it; the "arch test" is `RequestIsolationTest.everyRouteRunsOnTheRequestDispatcherAndProbesDoNot`. The per-request config/generation header lookups run on IO; readiness pings on IO. SQLSTATE 57014 (statement timeout) is now 503 as the audit row asks (BC-43 had kept it 500), logged at ERROR with the stack so slow queries stay visible. The in-flight limiter stays N-056 admission control. Honest bound: cancellation acts at suspension points, so a handler inside JDBC answers when its statement ends (25 s plus one statement, 15 s); if it then responds before suspending, the client gets that real answer.
- BC-46 (2026-10-07, session 5) AUD-REL-07: `apiServer` sets shutdownGracePeriod 3 s and shutdownTimeout 25 s; `Drain` counts non-probe calls, turns readiness 503 on ApplicationStopPreparing and blocks the stop up to 15 s until no call is in flight (worst stop about 15 s plus Netty's 3 x 3 s quiet periods, inside the 30 s default termination grace), and responses sent while draining carry `Connection: close`; the pools close on ApplicationStopped in `apiServer` only (tests share one Database across applications). The worker gets the same hook when it has a job loop with in-flight work to wait for (not built).
- BC-47 (2026-10-07, session 5) AUD-SEC-08 / AUD-TP-3: `RouteInventoryTest` walks the production routing tree. The public allow-list is the contract: an operation with `security: []` or `{}` among its alternatives may be served outside `authenticated {}` (this admits the device-proof routes `/devices/nonce`, `/devices/me/policy`, `/devices/me/status`). Every guarded route must answer 401 without a token, and must have a line in `backend/app/src/test/resources/scope-cases.txt`: a scope test case, an exemption with a reason, or `gap: <lane>`. After the Opus checker (7 wrong cases reclassified): 33 cases, 23 exemptions, 68 gaps (47 backend-admin, 17 backend-reports, 4 backend-core), the gap set frozen in `scope-gaps-baseline.txt` (may only shrink); a case must name the route's path in its test class. Also pinned: the public set (literal, equal to the contract's), the four widened guards (change-password, bind-device, logout, sync/batch; the selector now carries the guard's settings), device-proof routes answer 401 without a proof, and the walker fails on non-method leaves and unknown selectors. Interim role check not built: an SR token must get 403 on every /admin route (needs a live database in the inventory test). Not built: the per-route role-denial matrix, since the contract carries no per-operation roles (needs `x-roles` in the contract first). `ARON_SCOPE_SEEDS` widens `ScopeLeakTest`.
- BC-48 (2026-10-07, session 5) AUD-SEC-07: only `ARON_ROLE=api` requires the JWT signing key; worker and migrate start without it (the worker neither signs nor verifies). `docs/requests/infra-worker-no-signing-key.md` asks infra to drop the secret from the worker container. JWKS publishing both keys during a rotation is tested in `SettingsTest` (the T-7-71 drill can reuse it). The refresh derivation key stays derived from the signing key (optional split not built).
- BC-49 (2026-10-07, session 5) AUD-SEC-03: `SecurityEvents` port in platform, best effort (`safely`, never fails the caller); sink today is one `aron.security` JSON log line (user and device hashed, username as `username_hash`), table requested (`backend-core-security-event-table.md`). Emitted: login_failure (deduped per username hash, device and minute) and lockout (once, when the lock is set) in `LoginService`; refresh_reuse exactly once in `RefreshService` when the family is revoked; password_change on success; device_proof_invalid, device_state_refused and scope_changed in the platform's problem handler for every route. force_logout waits for the admin force-logout endpoint (not built here).
- BC-50 (2026-10-07, session 5) AUD-SEC-01, backend-core part: the guard's gate (`ScopeVersionLookup.deviceStatus`, `JdbiUserStore`, same 10 s cache) reads the status of the token's device (`did`) and refuses an `aron-api` token from a suspended (ERR_DEVICE_SUSPENDED) or revoked/replaced (ERR_DEVICE_REVOKED) phone; a token whose `did` row no longer exists is refused on every route (Opus checker: a path-prefix rule could be dodged by path encoding). Only where the scope-version check runs: sync/batch and logout (upload grant) keep working so captured sales still arrive. The post-revoke upload-grant cap in refresh waits for the device-revoke endpoint (F-ADM-009/F-API-086); the scope_version triggers are the db lane's part of the row.
- BC-51 (2026-10-07, session 5) AUD-REL-08 (logging part): logback's built-in `JsonEncoder` (one JSON object per line, stack trace as one field, no new dependency). The `aron.request` line logs the route template (`/v1/admin/users/{id}`, from Ktor's RoutingCallStarted; `(unmatched)` otherwise), never the raw path or query; `user`/`device` are 16-hex pseudonyms (`logPseudonym`, D-136), plus `app_version` (pattern-checked) and `revision` (build). `RequestLogTest` is the PII-scrub test. Metrics, worker heartbeat and the log-workspace split stay with N-062 and the worker row; item 8 of `infra-backend-runtime.md` is answered when the metric names exist.
- BC-52 (2026-10-07, session 5) Opus checker on SEC-01/03/07, fixed: `refresh_reuse` is written only by the replay whose revoke changed the row (`RefreshStore.revokeFamily` returns Boolean), so concurrent replays write one event; IPv6 `ipClass` expands the literal before taking /48 (no DNS lookup). Open, logged not fixed: (a) rotating the JWT signing key changes `derivedSecret`, so refresh children and sealed bind OTPs differ across replicas mid-rollout; separate Key Vault secrets for the refresh and OTP keys are a precondition of any rotation (T-7-71); (b) `username_hash` is unsalted SHA-256 (64 bits), a pseudonym not an anonymiser; an HMAC needs a separate log key (infra secret); (c) phones without a device row (`cfg.device.require_enrolled=false`) carry no `did`, so the device gate has nothing to check; the pilot should run with require_enrolled on once enrolment works; (d) change-password failures write no `login_failure` (lockout only).
- BC-53 (2026-10-07, session 5) F-SYS-072 server side (android-core-backend-record-signature-mode.md, lead priority 1): ingest step 6 honours `cfg.sec.record_signature_mode`, read once per batch: `off` skips, `record` accepts a header record with a missing or bad `sig` and raises one DEVICE_INTEGRITY_FAIL per phone and business date (evidence `reason` = record_signature_missing/invalid), `enforce` quarantines `device_integrity_failed` as before. A missing or unknown value is `record`, so no sale is dropped by default (the key is not in the registry yet: `backend-core-record-signature-mode-key.md`). The registry hash leaves `sig` out (a stored hash with `sig` still matches), so a row first sent unsigned and later signed is a duplicate, not payload_conflict; the batch fingerprint keeps the whole envelope. Opus checker (4 findings, 3 fixed): a registry row keyed by the old full-envelope hash is re-keyed on its first resend (parked rows then turn accepted, never a later 23505 schema_invalid); a `device_integrity_failed` quarantine is released when the mode is not enforce (the resend is processed like a parked row and its open sync_quarantine row is marked accepted); the flag runs in its own savepoint. (1) **Decode:** `POST /v1/devices/me/status` with `play_integrity` is decoded by Google before the transaction (`notify/GooglePlayIntegrityDecoder`, a Google service-account client next to FCM; `ARON_PLAY_INTEGRITY_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`, else the FCM account, else no decoder). In the transaction the nonce is spent (issued to this device, purpose `play_integrity`, unexpired, unused) and `platform/PlayIntegrityCheck` binds the payload: `requestPackageName` = the device's `app_package`, `requestHash` = hex SHA-256(nonce + device_uuid) (R12), `timestampMillis` within 15 min (5 min future skew). Only then does `deviceRecognitionVerdict` decide `pass` (contains `MEETS_DEVICE_INTEGRITY`) or `fail`; `appRecognitionVerdict` is recorded in the flag only (D24-18). A refused decode, a spent/foreign nonce, another package or a stale token is `unevaluated` and **does not move** the device verdict: a replayed report never fails a phone and a replayed pass never passes one. A `fail` raises DEVICE_INTEGRITY_FAIL (severity 4, weight 30) on the device for the Dhaka date. The token is never stored or logged. (2) **Gate** (IngestService step 5b, after scope, before the record signature): gated types are `attendance_event`, `memo`, `memo_void`, `due_collection` (children of a held parent are held with it). *Not enrolled* = the device row has no `enrolment_token_id` or no usable key (the dev seed phone). With `cfg.device.require_enrolled` on: gated records quarantined `device_not_enrolled`, and because a keyless phone cannot prove its batch, **every non-telemetry record of a keyless phone** is held too; `POST /sync/batch` no longer refuses a keyless phone under the gate (it would retry forever). With it off: accepted and flagged. With `cfg.device.require_integrity` on, an enrolled phone whose verdict is not `pass` (fail, unevaluated, stale) has its gated records quarantined `device_integrity_failed`; off, only a genuine `fail` is flagged (an unevaluated dev phone is not). Flags: one DEVICE_INTEGRITY_FAIL per device and date, upgraded to severity 4 / score 30 with `evidence.gate` (`device_not_enrolled`, `play_integrity_<verdict>`) and `held`. (3) **Release:** server side, a `device_not_enrolled` or `device_integrity_failed` quarantine with an open review item is processed again on every resend (the earlier signature-only release generalised; under enforce a bad signature is re-quarantined with the same outcome); accepted, it closes the item and the registry row becomes `accepted` under the same uuid, user and device (the lead's digest note). **But today's phone treats a quarantine as terminal** (it re-sends only `device_integrity_failed`, not under enforce, at most 7 daily rounds), so in practice a held row is released by the reviewer's acceptor (backend-reports, on hold) or by the phone change requested in `docs/requests/backend-core-gate-resend-android-core.md`. (4) **Opus checker FAIL, fixed:** the token must name our app (`appIntegrity.packageName` = the device's `app_package` and `certificateSha256Digest` contains its enrolled `app_signing_cert_sha256`), else a re-signed copy on a genuine phone could relay a pass to a rooted one; verdicts apply in token order (`integrity_checked_at` = the token's timestamp, an older token never overwrites); a missing `deviceIntegrity` block is `unevaluated`, a present block without a verdict list is `fail` (Google omits the list for a device that meets nothing); the test fixes. Logged, not fixed: a signature quarantine resent under enforce with a newly valid `sig` is now accepted (the sig is the device key's proof, so this is a release, not a hole); a release re-runs scope and may re-code the item `scope_out_of_reach`; a released row that parks under enforce loses its release (the `parked` branch is unchanged); `evidence.held` is the first gate flag's of the day; non-Play-certified budget phones always `fail` (a pilot device check must confirm the field models pass). Open: a `pass` never goes stale (no `integrity_refresh_h` expiry yet; an old pass keeps a phone ungated); the offline `device_status` record path does not decode (its nonce would have expired); credentials and the Play Console link are infra's and the owner's (`docs/requests/backend-core-play-integrity-secret.md`).

- BC-81 (2026-10-08, session 10) F-API-007 **POST /v1/media/upload** multipart fallback (`media/MediaUpload.kt`, `MediaUploadTest`, ledger `app.media_upload` V0061). **One uuid = one purpose** (db asked us to choose: `blob_path` is UNIQUE without the purpose): a second upload of the same `media_uuid` under the other purpose, or with other bytes, is 400 `ERR_VALIDATION` (the contract has no 409 on this operation). The file is checked before anything is written: lowercase UUID v4, purpose `feedback`/`support`, sha256 equals the bytes, JPEG magic and `image/jpeg` part type (else 415), at most 307 200 bytes (the reader cuts at one byte over, then 413). Under a per-uuid advisory lock in one transaction: read the ledger (same purpose and bytes: `replayed: true`, nothing written), else write the blob (overwrite: nothing was acknowledged yet, so a crash between the write and the insert is repaired by the retry), then INSERT ... Before this an MFA role (ADMIN, SUPERADMIN, SUPPORT) could not finish a web login. **Trust on first use:** an MFA-role user with no confirmed enrolment enrols with the login's `mfa_token` (it has no full session before TOTP); an unconfirmed enrolment is replaced by a later enrol; the first good TOTP code confirms it (`mfa_enabled`). Once confirmed, enrol is 409 and only an ADMIN's `reset_mfa` starts over. Enrol is for the MFA roles only (403 otherwise: other roles never reach the TOTP step that confirms). TOTP RFC 6238 (SHA-1, 6 digits, 30 s, one step either side, never a step at or below the last used one, under a row lock); ten recovery codes `XXXX-XXXX`, HMAC verifiers only, usable after confirmation, single use, case-insensitive. Wrong codes count per user (`mfa|<id>`, `cfg.auth.lockout_attempts`; admin `unlock` and `reset_mfa` now clear it). A scope_version change spends outstanding mfa_tokens. Key ring: `ARON_MFA_KEY` (+ `_PREVIOUS`) from Key Vault (`docs/requests/backend-core-mfa-key.md`), then the signing-key derivation; a secret no key opens is 503 without a lockout count. Audit rows `mfa.enrol`, `mfa.confirm`, `mfa.recovery_spent`; security-event kinds wait on `docs/requests/backend-core-mfa-security-events.md`. Open: `amr` ["pwd","mfa"] is not carried by refreshed tokens (the refresh family does not store it; matters once a step-up check reads `amr`); the 5-minute mfa_token is reusable (each use needs a fresh code); web needs an enrolment screen (QR from `otpauth_uri`, recovery codes shown once) reached from the MFA step.

The handler now reads the body itself (at most one byte over the limit: 413) and parses it with a small strict in-memory reader (`Multipart` in `MediaUpload.kt`: RFC 7578 parts after CRLF-anchored delimiters, quoted boundary, transport padding, at most 8 parts, fields at most 200 bytes, the file cut one byte over so `store()` still answers 413); anything malformed is 400, a malformed Content-Type 415. Ktor's own parser class is internal API, so it is not used. Sonnet checker: PASS with low interop notes (unquoted `name=`, LF-only line ends are 400; no real client sends them).
