- BA-30 (2026-10-15, backend-admin) **Outlet code rule (F-ADM-037)**: the Apsis rule is unknown (docs/15). Approving a new-outlet request gives the code `<ZONE CODE>-<NNN>`, NNN = highest numeric suffix of that zone's codes + 1 (three digits at least), under a per-zone advisory lock; an explicit `outlet_code` on the approval wins; `code` is not patchable, so an approved outlet never changes code. Zone codes outside the outlet-code form are cleaned (`-` for other characters, 27 characters at most). Direct admin creates without a code keep `AD-<id>`.
- BA-31 (2026-10-15, backend-admin) **Web entry (F-API-050/052) and unlocks (F-API-049)**: roles per docs/24 s8.5: TSO (own zones), ADMIN, SUPERADMIN write web entry; the warehouse QC and its reason need ADMIN or SUPERADMIN; unlocks are granted by `cfg.web.entry_unlock_roles` plus SUPERADMIN. A date older than `cfg.web.entry_backdate_days` (0 = only today) needs an active unlock of the route or its zone (409 `ERR_ENTRY_WINDOW_CLOSED`); no future dates; Final Submit refuses (409). Policy `cfg.web.entry_app_overlap_policy` is treated as `exclusive_flag` whatever its value (stored and flagged, never added). A web entry and a data void of the route-day serialise on one advisory lock key. The Astha outlet-SKU entry is trimmed (docs/27).
- BA-32 (2026-10-15, backend-admin) **Location history rows for every basis change**: an outlet created without a pin writes a `none` row, a set pin `master`, a cleared pin `none`; the contract read lists only rows with coordinates. **User scope `valid_to`** is an exclusive end (R20), edited by a later PUT; `valid_to` must be after `valid_from`.
- UI-SR-1 (2026-10-17, ui-calm-glass, SR shell and Home) **Home day state from local facts only** (screens-sr s2 dock table; `HomeDayModel.dayState`): checked in = the attendance flow has a check-in today; stock issued = any `stock_movement` on the business date; route done = the KPI strip's planned outlets > 0 and non-visit = 0 (a skipped outlet is not a visit, so a route with skips never reads as done: the 17:00 rule then offers Sales submit); after check-out time = the flow's `checkOutEnabled` (checked in, not out, `cfg.day.checkout_earliest_time` reached on trusted Dhaka time) or already checked out; submitted = a `day_submit` row today. Precedence: not checked in, then complete (submitted and checked out), then submitted after check-out time (dock Check out), then submitted before it (**no dock and no success chip**: the table names no step; a new state `SUBMITTED`), then route done or after the time (Sales submit), then no stock, then in the field.
- UI-SR-2 (2026-10-17, ui-calm-glass) **Sync chip without a sync-state flow**: core-sync exposes no "syncing" flow and SystemShell's network read is a one-shot, so the chip is derived from the outbox (`countInState(in_flight)` > 0 = Syncing, `pending` > 0 = Waiting(n), newest `acked_at` = Synced at HH:mm Dhaka, else Synced) and one `NetworkCapabilities` read (no validated internet = Offline, which wins over the counts). Read on resume, on every screen change, after a check-in/out and after "sync now"; never polled. A live chip needs a `StateFlow` from `SyncScheduler` (follow-up for core-sync).
- UI-SR-3 (2026-10-17, ui-calm-glass) **Sync sheet (screens-sr s19) is a Material3 `ModalBottomSheet`** coloured from the tokens (the kit has no `AronBottomSheet` yet): the chip in full, the sun switch, the pending count, the newest ack, "Sync now" (`SyncTrigger.MANUAL`, disabled offline with the reason) and the support shortcut. Per-entity counts and the storage line wait on a counter API; the sun switch moved off the sale screens' chrome into Home and this sheet (s0 thumb map).
- UI-SR-4 (2026-10-17, ui-calm-glass) **Hero skeleton has no 150 ms delay**: `HomeContent(loading = true)` shows the hero skeleton as soon as Home composes and the hero replaces it when the Room pass (`SrHomeReads.snapshot`, `Dispatchers.IO`, keyed on the day data, attendance and the resume tick) lands; the tiles never wait. Route kind in the header is AKTCL's English wording (`Daily`, `3F`, `2F`, non-translatable) as the design asks; the old localized `hm_route_*` strings stay for callers that pass no label.
- UI-SR-5 (2026-10-17, ui-calm-glass) **Robolectric SDK 36 tests need Java 21+**: the feature-home and app-sr compose tests (`@Config(sdk = [36])`) cannot start on the laptop's JDK 17; they run green with `-Dorg.gradle.java.home="C:\Program Files\Android\Android Studio\jbr"` (JDK 25) and `--no-build-cache` (the cache packer fails on Windows file modes). CI must pin JDK 21.
- UI-SR-6 (2026-10-17, ui-calm-glass, review fixes) **Home and frame after review**: the frame's back button dispatches the system Back (`rememberSystemBack`), so a feature's own `BackHandler` (Memo detail, Sales Submit cancel) always wins over the parent mapping, exactly like the gesture; a dock whose tile is disabled by an expired or missing bundle is disabled too and shows `hm_blocked_by_bundle` as its reason; the device line is caption text only (the warning word `hm_health_warn_word`, BN "সতর্কতা", a new word pending the native reviewer, plus the warning icon speaking `hm_health_warning`), the storage-under-200-MB danger banner is the one card; stale, expired and missing bundles are `Warning` banners (danger is kept for storage); the header prints the two s2 caption lines (route name over `<ISO date> · <route kind>`, `HomeHeader.routeName`/`isoDate` with defaults); switch rows carry `Role.Switch` and their `ToggleableState`; the meter speaks `hm_meter_spoken` as the hero's state description; the compact variant (window under 700 dp) drops the hero meta line and the device line; the stock card shrinks a quantity to a 12 sp floor and stacks label over "ইস্যু … · বর্তমান স্টক …" from font scale 1.3; the dock cross-fades 150 ms (120 ms linear under reduced motion); the EN tile label is "Tasks" (BN unchanged). Known gaps left to the kit: `AronTopBar` has no title/subtitle modifier slots (`HomeScreenTags.SUBTITLE` is reached by text) and no collapsing header.
- BA-33 (2026-10-15, backend-admin) **Dues adjustments (F-ADM-036) rulings**: ADMIN proposes, SUPERADMIN decides (docs/24 s8.5), never the maker. A data void is refused (409 `ERR_DAY_DATA_VOID_NOT_ALLOWED`) while an approved dues correction is attached to one of the route-day's memos, and an approval for an already voided memo is 409; reversing the correction through a ledger row is a later row (lead ruling 2026-10-15). `cfg.sec.fraud.adjust_approval_mtk` stays deferred: every correction needs a SUPERADMIN, which is today's behaviour (the key is not among the 172 seeded). A negative correction has no balance bound until the owner answers; the approval audit row carries `balance_after_mtk` and `balance_below_zero` so the screen can flag it. Amount cap per adjustment 10^13 mtk (a db CHECK is routed to the db lane).
- BA-34 (2026-10-15, backend-admin) **Data void and the dues ledger**: a tombstoned memo is netted whole (its due, closures, and the collections and late-collection give-backs that are tombstoned with it). A collection from a day the void does not touch, and the give-back it caused, is real cash and stays as a credit; this makes the balance independent of whether that collection synced before or after the void, and it is reversed on its own if its own day is voided later (one row of its own derived source that nets the collection and its give-back together). Known edge, not changed: a memo that is both SR-voided and later data-voided, with a late collection from another day, keeps the give-back net (backend-core `DuesLedger` does not treat `voided_at` as a closure).

- 2026-10-15 D-REP-06: Export jobs (F-SYS-064) produce xlsx; `pdf` stays unproduced until the PDF library and Bangla font decision (docs/requests/backend-reports-pdf-export.md). The API still accepts `pdf`, queues it, and the job ends `failed` with a clear reason. Export files go to the `exports` container (ARON_BLOB_CONTAINER_EXPORTS) of the storage account; the download link is a 15-minute read-only user-delegation URL, the file lives 24 h. The job re-derives the requester's reach on the server when it runs; the stored `filters` hold the query only.
- 2026-10-15 D-REP-07: PII budgets (F-SYS-058): the hourly budget counts the unmasked personal rows of JSON reads in `app.pii_read_budget` (a read that would pass the limit is refused with 429 and is not counted; the TSO limit 5000 comes from its role value in `app.cfg_value`); the daily export budget sums `app.report_export.row_count` of the user's personal-column exports since the Dhaka midnight. Masked reads are free.

- 2026-10-15 D-REP-08: Export checker follow-ups. Fixed: late workers can no longer fail or change a job whose lease was swept (finish/fail UPDATEs check `status = 'running' AND claimed_by`), the owner sees only a safe refusal text or the exception class, the resolved period is stored with the job, rows stream into the workbook instead of a list, the daily PII export budget is reserved atomically (per-user advisory lock around check + insert, `ReportEngine.beginExport`), the role limit respects effective-dated rows. Not done, noted: (a) no attempt counter, so a job that kills the worker is retried after each lease (needs a `report_export.attempts` column: db lane); (b) the job's pii flag is the token claim at request time (no server-side source to re-check); (c) the daily export budget ignores role-scoped values; (d) `/report-exports` numbers rows with row_number() over the table (needs a keyset cursor when the table is large); (e) the audit row and the job row are separate transactions on the async path.

- 2026-10-15 D-REP-09: Zone-wide AMO bundle (F-AMO-044, `SupervisorBundle.kt`, `BundleService`). The AMO owns no route, so the zone's active routes (at most 2,000) ride in `routes[]` as `assignment_kind: cover`, `planned_today: false`, `target_outlets: 0`, empty `open_memos` (the contract has no other carrier, and the existing outlet paging reads `routes[].outlets`); the AMO's own target and Login/Submit % never include them (route-day state is untouched: the zone routes are not in `plans`, so nothing marks them `logged_in`). `route_ids` in the `supervisor` section lets the app tell zone routes from its own. TSO gets the `supervisor` section only (team, pending requests), no zone outlets (several zones; a web job). `supervisor` = zone_ids and routes from `Reach` (token reach, never client ids), team = users assigned to those routes that day (phone masked `017*****678`), pending requests = status `pending` in reach, risk signals (AMO only) = `open` in reach, `supervisor_day`. Pages are served from a per-user, per-scope_version cache of the full bundle for 5 minutes (32 versions at most), so a sale landing between page 1 and page 2 no longer 410s the download; a new `bundle()` call still gets the new version.

- 2026-10-15 D-REP-10: Bundle pre-generation (F-SYS-067, `BundlePreGeneration.kt`, worker). The 22:00 pass builds tomorrow and the user's next working day (Thursday night also covers Saturday) for every active SR, AMO and TSO through `BundleService.bundle(prebuild = true)`: like a pre-fetch it creates the day's rows and the snapshot but never freezes a target, marks a login or records a download, so the 03:30 refresh and 04:30 coverage check (which build today's date) are safe too. The worker builds as the user's lowest active phone binding (same `bind_ordinal` and device-scoped config as that phone's digest; a user with several phones gets the first phone's version). 03:30 rebuilds users with a `bundle_user` dirty key and deletes only the keys whose build succeeded; 04:30 counts today's coverage and builds the missing ones below 99 %. A tick marks a phase done only when it ran (a lock held by another replica or an exception is retried next minute). Paced at `cfg.bundle.regen_max_per_s` (default 20), advisory lock per phase and date (one pooled connection held for the pass). Stored bodies and serving from them are NOT built: no producer marks `bundle_user` dirty, and a stale stored bundle would ship old prices to the field; the API keeps building on request (ETag and 304 unchanged). Needs: db producers and infra `bundles` container (docs/requests/backend-reports-bundle-serve-snapshot.md).
- 2026-10-15 D-REP-11: F-AMO-044 checker fixes. Zone routes carry `target_outlets` 0, no `acting_for_user_id` and a synthetic `not_started` day state (never the rep's live state, so a rep's sale does not move the AMO's snapshot); zone outlets are chosen by the outlet's own zone (`o.zone_id` in the reach), and a route is listed when its zone or any of its active outlets is in reach (route zone changes do not re-stamp outlets, V0042); the TSO section lists the reach's routes and team and an empty `risk_signals` array (never null); the page cache holds at most two versions per user and about 150,000 paged rows in all, oldest out first.

- 2026-10-17 D-UI-COLOUR-01: Owner asked for a vibrant, colourful UI with at least ten colours, colours only; the three Android apps matter most, the web portal may stay calmer; yes is green, no is red. Done as token and style changes only. Android: aurora page gradient (sky, lavender, blush; deep indigo to violet in dark; flat in sunlight) now painted behind every screen by `aronPageBackground()`; electric indigo accent; emerald, orange, crimson and teal status colours with tinted containers; ten tile hues taken in turn by the home grid; `AronYesButton` (green) and `AronNoButton` (red) used by `AronConfirmDialog` and the print yes/no dialogs (core-printing now depends on core-ui). Web: same palette in `globals.css`, ten chart hues cycled per bar, utility washes richer, good/bad buttons green/red. Guardrails kept: content stays on solid cards; the outdoor gates of docs/32 s2a still hold and are asserted in `TokenContrastTest` (new tests for yes/no labels at 7:1 and 3:1 under glare, ten tile hues at 7:1) and in the web contrast tests; Sunlight mode keeps flat solid surfaces. Android golden screenshots re-recorded and eyeballed. Assumption: "No" means any cancel or dismiss in the kit confirm dialog, red even when the confirm action is destructive; revisit if the owner wants the destructive action itself in red.

- 2026-10-17 D-UI-COLOUR-02: Two UI lines had diverged and the cloud lead had not seen the second. The laptop session's Calm Glass work (kit with the AronIcons set, icon tiles, SR and AMO/TSO homes, 36 goldens; branches `lane/ui-calm-glass` and the hardening branch `claude/amazing-kilby-fdc9ab`) never reached INT, so the colour pass D-UI-COLOUR-01 was built and promoted on the old plain kit (INT ef056f26) and the owner, who installed that APK, lost the icon UI. Fix: `lane/lead-colors-calm` = `lane/ui-calm-glass` 0c386d4e + INT merged (every UI conflict resolved to the Calm Glass side, message catalogues and this file keep both) + the vibrant palette re-applied on the Calm Glass kit: new `yesFill` and `noFill` roles with `AronYesButton` and `AronNoButton`, ten `AronTokens.Hues` for the icon wells (`LocalTileHue`, set by `AronTileGrid`), aurora gradient values, status hues; 36 goldens re-recorded and eyeballed; TokenContrastTest 16 tests green. The web theme stays the Calm Glass one (owner: web may be calmer); the INT web palette of D-UI-COLOUR-01 is superseded by it. Rule from now on: the lead's lane check also fetches every non-lane branch that moves (`claude/*`) and reports a line that is not on INT; the hardening branch still has to merge INT after this lands (it already contains `lane/ui-calm-glass`).
- 2026-10-18 D-AUTH-TSO-NOBIND: Owner decision: the TSO app logs in with no bind OTP. Reason: the Device OTP panel and issue endpoint cover SR and AMO users only (`DeviceOtps.kt`), so the OTP that `LoginService.complete` created for a TSO's first login on a phone could never be read; the TSO test phone was stuck at "bind required". Change: `LoginService.complete` skips the bind step when `user.role == TSO` (`cfg.auth.bind_otp_required` still applies to SR and AMO). The enrolment gate (`cfg.device.require_enrolled`) is unchanged, so an unknown phone is still refused. A TSO phone gets no `device_binding` row, so the login answer carries `bind_ordinal` null (allowed by LoginDevice). Open: the bundle falls back to ordinal 0 for an unbound phone; no TSO memo numbering uses it. Needs a deploy to dev before the phone sees it.


- 2026-10-17 D-HARD-GATE-01: Merging INT into the hardening branch (`claude/amazing-kilby-fdc9ab`, owner request "merge it and fix its build") showed how the migration gate judges `V0084`..`V0087`. (1) The red Android job on that branch was one golden (`home_bn_light_font13`) recorded on Windows; INT's Linux-recorded goldens replace it, so the merge alone fixes it (merge `da5f3382`, ci #857 green, pushed without force). (2) `tools/ci/migrations-check.sh` compares a push with the merge-base of the previous tip (`BEFORE`) and, on a first push, a dispatch or a force-push, with INT. A migration that already existed at that base counts as shipped: it is not linted again and cannot be edited. So `V0087` (no `SET lock_timeout` before `CREATE FUNCTION`) passes on every push to the hardening branch but fails squawk the first time it is judged against INT (the promotion path), and an edit to it on a branch that already holds it fails the checksum rule (lane/lead-hardening ci #856: "was shipped at da5f338 and changed since"). (3) Resolution: the fix (`SET lock_timeout = '5s';` in `V0087`, commit `18542ace`) is applied only on a candidate pushed as a NEW branch (`lane/lead-hardening-int`, run by workflow_dispatch because a new branch on commits GitHub already knows starts no push run), which is judged against INT; `V0087` has not been applied on dev (INT stops at `V0083`), so editing it is allowed. The hardening branch keeps the old `V0087` text; when it later takes INT back it must do so on a new branch name, or the checksum rule fires once on that one line. (4) Promotion of the hardening work to INT waits for the owner: it brings SCALE-02/03, DB-01/04, DD-01/02 and OPS-02/03 into INT and dev.

- 2026-10-17 D-HARD-HOLD-01: Owner decision "hold hardening": the laptop session's hardening work (`claude/amazing-kilby-fdc9ab` da5f3382 and the migration-fixed candidate `lane/lead-hardening-int` 18542ace) is NOT promoted to INT or dev for now; the owner expects many feature and functionality improvements to land first. The integrator and the lead's lane check treat both heads as held. The merge of INT into the hardening branch (colours) stays done. Revisit when the owner says `promote hardening`.

- 2026-10-17 D-AMO-SURVEY-01 (android-amo): The AMO Survey (F-AMO-043/010) is the SR POSM survey rules (`PosmSurvey`) behind the Control Call menu, no points. `CaptureRepository.recordSurveyResponse` now accepts visits of kind `sr_call` and `amo_control_call` (never joint call or TSO); content views stay SR-only. The bundle's first survey is used (as the SR does): `SurveyDef` has no audience field, so if POSM and an AMO survey are both published the AMO would get the lowest id. Open for backend/contract: an audience or kind field. Known, same as the SR: a back-press between the survey write and the photo claim can leave a photo unclaimed.
- 2026-10-17 D-AMO-PRICE-01 (android-amo, ASSUMED, G-feat-01/D-334): Price compliance (F-AMO-035) is per pack: reference = outlet list price (`price_type` of the open outlet) x `base_per_pack` / `per_base_qty`, rounded half up to a milli-taka; the AMO types the shelf price in taka (max 3 decimals, parsed without floats). Compliant when |observed - reference| <= reference x tolerance, integer basis points, default 100 (1%), read from `cfg.amo.price_tolerance_bp` if the registry ever has it. One `price_compliance_check` (rank 2, family = the visit) per typed SKU, saved all or nothing, once per visit.

- 2026-10-17 D-AMO-SALE-01 (android-amo, decides the F-AMO-006 wiring): the SR sale stack is reused by copying `SrSaleKit`, `RoomSaleReads`, the sale hosts, `PrintConfig`, `StockSlips` and the print/stock parts of `SrDay` into `app-amo` (`AmoSaleKit`, `AmoSaleHosts`, `AmoDay`); the feature modules (`feature-sale`, `-memo`, `-stock`, `-dayclose`) are shared, not copied. Extraction into a shared module is deferred: it would move the `sr_*` resources and touch the SR lanes' files. The AMO sale is the SR sale route without start prompt, AV, KV or SR survey, inside the geo-gated Control Call (there is no un-gated AMO sale).
- 2026-10-17 D-AMO-CLOSE-01 (android-amo): a Control Call closes `sold` when a memo exists for the visit, `zero_sale_stock_ok` when it produced only audit records (assessment, price check, survey), and `abandoned` (not counted as a visit) when it produced nothing.
- 2026-10-17 D-AMO-SALE-02 (android-amo, from the checker): one sale per Control Call (a second tap on Sale says the sale exists; changes go through Memo edit); a memo with no lines (zero sale) closes the call as `zero_sale_stock_ok`; closing a call is serialised. Memo edit keeps the SR rule (a fresh in-range fix), so a memo sold under Manual Override cannot be edited later from elsewhere: open question for the sponsor. Memo numbering needs a valid username and the login's bind ordinal (as for the SR); a null numbering fails the first commit loudly, it does not number.
- 2026-10-17 D-SIZE-BASELINE-02: The APK size gate (tools/ci/apk-size-gate.py, fail at +15 % over the baseline, absolute 30 MB per ABI) failed the first real AMO build: amo-release armeabi-v7a 4.15 MB against a baseline of 3.58 MB, which was the size of the placeholder AMO shell (identical to the TSO shell). Wave 2 turns both shells into full apps on the SR's shared feature modules, so growth to the SR's size is expected, not a regression. Ruling (lead, infra-owned file): the `amo-release` and `tso-release` baselines are set to the `sr-release` baseline (SR is the realistic reference: same modules), which gives AMO and TSO the same +5 % warn and +15 % fail headroom the SR has. Absolute budgets are unchanged and far away (4 to 6 MB against 30 MB). Re-baseline each app once its wave 2 rows are done and the real sizes are known (`tools/ci/apk-size-gate.py --write-baseline` on a green INT build); the same rule as the CameraX and Maps resets of 2026-10-07.

- 2026-10-18 D-AMO-VERIFY-01 (android-amo): Verify (F-AMO-022/023/024) reads the pending list from the bundle's `supervisor.pending_outlet_requests` and the request details from `GET /v1/outlet-requests/{uuid}` through `AmoApi` (last answer kept per user, so the screen works offline from the summary). Save queues an `outlet_request_verification` record (verified) with a fresh `outlet_verification` fix; a missing or mock fix queues nothing. Cancel = back with no record (acceptance F-AMO-022); the `discarded` decision is a separate press-and-hold "reject" (docs/07 calls Cancel a reject: owner to confirm). Gaps logged: the verification payload has no route or cluster member, so route/cluster are shown read-only, not editable; the optional re-captured photo is not offered yet (payload supports `photo_uuid`).

- 2026-10-18 D-AMO-BASE-01 (android-amo): Update Base (F-AMO-028) is a `location` outlet request from the Outlet hub: confirm, photo with the AMO's own fix, then the exact point. The N-053 map is lite (a still image, no gestures), so the point is the AMO's fix moved in 10 m steps (N/S/E/W) within `cfg.geo.update_base_max_distance_m` (default 100) and shown with its distance from the shop's current point; no map is loaded on this screen. `OutletRequests` refuses a chosen point beyond the limit (POINT_TOO_FAR) or taken on a mocked fix (MOCK_FIX); the app also refuses a move beyond `cfg.geo.update_base_max_move_m` (1000) from the shop's current point. **Server side the guards are open**: the backend only checks lat/lng range for a `location` request (it flags `mocked_fix` and has the TSO move check); the fix-to-point distance, the move limit and the monthly cap per outlet are not enforced there, so a modified client could bypass them (request filed: docs/requests/android-amo-update-base-server-guards.md). docs/24 s12.2 says a location request takes lat/lng 'from the request's own fix'; the app now sends a point within the limit of it (the contract allows any in-range pair). Offline it works from the current fix. Owner may later ask for a draggable map.

- 2026-10-18 D-AMO-REPORT-01 (android-amo): the AMO Report tile reads `POST /v1/reports/{key}/query` (json, 500 rows a page, up to 5 pages, scope from the token). STD Memo Report uses `std-memo` (the only STD report the AMO role may run; `route-std` by category is web-only), so the per-route view is memos, gross and net from the memo rows; asking backend to open `route-std` to the AMO would give the per-category STD of docs/07. The kept copy of a report is a file in the cache folder, 3 days, 600 KB at most.

- 2026-10-18 D-AMO-VERIFY-02 (android-amo, lead ruling): Verify follows docs/07: Cancel rejects the request. It asks for a confirmation and then queues the `discarded` decision (the payload has it); leaving with Back queues nothing. This replaces the press-and-hold reject of D-AMO-VERIFY-01 and departs from the F-AMO-022 acceptance wording "Cancel makes no server call" (the decision is queued and sent with the next sync). Route and cluster stay read-only in Verify (the verification payload has no member for them).

- 2026-10-18 D-TSO-027-SCOPE (lead ruling, android-amo doing the TSO dashboard block): F-TSO-027 is re-scoped. The TSO bundle stays outlet-free (data budget, docs/04), so there is no "about 2,500 outlets under 1 MB gzipped" login snapshot. The TSO app reads the token-scoped dashboard aggregates on demand (Refresh, no timer) with kept copies and an "as of" stamp; zone filter from the summary's children; outlet pickers, when a TSO row needs them, use the contract's online outlet search or the cache of the dashboard reads, with an offline empty state. BACKEND-PENDING: by_segment (F-TSO-005 shows its empty state until it arrives), SR name in the route lists, the 500 for a cover route outside the TSO's zones (lead-backend works docs/requests/android-amo-tso-dashboard-backend.md). DEVICE-PENDING on the Galaxy A06 for all cards.
- 2026-10-19 D-AMO-HOME-01 (android-amo): the AMO Home is eight hub tiles in two groups plus a Settings gear, not the manual's 17-tile grid (hubs: Sale = Stock/Sale/Memo/Summary, Team = Team Location/Performance/Task Delegation/Live Dashboard/SR Stock/Exceptions, Outlet = verification and own requests). Reason: 17 tiles do not fit a 360 dp phone at 130% font scale, and the hubs keep every destination one tap deeper. Retraining impact: AMOs find Stock, Memo and Summary under "বিক্রয়" and the team screens under "টিম". Joint Call now sits before Control Call as in the manual. The owner can ask for the full grid.
- 2026-10-19 D-AMO-REPLAY-01 (android-amo): `StockLoad.save` writes its re-save guard BEFORE the commit and `commitFailed` gives it back, so a kill between the commit and `committed` can no longer let the same increments post twice after a relaunch (shared with SR). `recordNumberedSale` returns the stored memo number of a memo that is still in the database and reserves nothing.

- 2026-10-17 D-CONTRACT-WIDEN-01 (lead approval, session_01MbUQSxrP7AB9tbyANjUTPS): contract 1.3.2 widens `CodeItem.attrs` to scalars or arrays of scalars and `ConfigValueJson` role-map values to objects or arrays of objects (`ConfigValueRecord`), to match what shipped migrations V0006, V0012, V0027 and V0049 already store and the server already sends; oasdiff reports it as breaking, approved by version bump and docs/requests/contract-conformance-widening.md. Reason: no running client sees anything new, and recoding stored config would be riskier. Follow-up 1.3.3 (same approval, same request file): `ConfigKeyDef.default_value` may be null (V0006 `cfg.ops.maintenance_banner`, no banner).
- 2026-10-17 D-CONTRACT-OPENBASE-01 (lead-contract, N-014 section D): a schema used as an `allOf` base carries no closing keyword; the outermost schema closes with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Opened: Outlet, TargetRow, ContentItem, TutorialItem, SurveyDef, RubricDef, RouteAssignment, ConfigChangeItem. Cost: where a base is used on its own (outlet pages and writes, route-assignment answers, bundle targets, surveys, rubrics, content and tutorials, TutorialList, ConfigChangeRequest items) a stray extra member is no longer refused by the schema (the server's strict decoding still refuses unknown request members). Reason: an open `*Core` base wrapped by a closed schema of the old name is what oasdiff (no allOf flattening) reports as breaking on every use.

- 2026-10-18 D-MIG-RANGE-01 (lead, session_01MbUQSxrP7AB9tbyANjUTPS): the lead's earlier reservation of `V0084`..`V0087` for the held hardening branch is lifted. INT stops at `V0083` and the hardening migrations are applied nowhere (D-HARD-GATE-01), but `tools/ci/migrations-check.sh` requires every new migration to be above the highest shipped version and Flyway has no out-of-order mode, so lane work that needs a migration cannot wait for a promotion the owner is holding. Lanes take the next free numbers on INT, numbers assigned by the lead so two heads never collide: `V0084` lead-backend (cfg key `cfg.support.upload_replay_max_h`, the PDA stored-upload age limit), `V0085` backend-authz (only if the C3 keys of docs/requests/backend-authz-c3-keys.md go by option A; option B needs none). The hardening branch renumbers its `V0084`..`V0087` to the next free numbers when the owner says "promote hardening" (file names, `RestoreDrillReconciliationTest`, `infra/scripts` and docs references; allowed because they are applied nowhere), on a new candidate branch as in D-HARD-GATE-01. Reason: holding hardening must not hold the authorisation and replay fixes; the renumbering is mechanical and costs one commit at promotion time.

- 2026-10-18 D-AUTHZ-QA-Q1..Q6 (lead, session_01MbUQSxrP7AB9tbyANjUTPS, answering qa's six open audit questions, none of which fails a test): (Q1, Q2) `cfg.auth.mfa_required_roles` and `cfg.price.max_change_pct` stay C2 as keys; D-AUTHZ-Q3Q4 (option B, no migration) makes a change C3 when it loosens the value (drops an MFA role, raises the price cap) or carries an end date, so one ADMIN can no longer switch either rail off; built by backend-authz as its own push. (Q3) `TokenSubject.pii` never set at login: the PII reveal flow of docs/21 s4.5 (re-auth within `cfg.pii.reauth_min`) is IN scope and not built yet; until it is, no role sees phone/owner columns on the admin API, which is the safe default; backend-authz owns it next after listAudit and editor_permission, with the design in docs/requests/backend-authz-pii-reveal.md (re-auth key to be registered then, a migration number from the lead). (Q4) `cfg_key.editor_permission`: ENFORCE per request through `app.role_grant_map` (the D-AUTHZ-Q5 mechanism), with the default seed granting every edit permission to ADMIN and SUPERADMIN so no current behaviour changes and a grant can be revoked; the column is not dropped. (Q5, Q6) DMO and WM are refused `GET /v1/admin/enrolment-tokens`, which docs/24 s8.5 gives them (R): the matrix stands (match current behaviour first), so the read is opened, limited to the caller's reach, listing token ids, status and expiry only, never a secret (a token secret is shown once at mint); small item for backend-authz after editor_permission. Order for backend-authz after Q3/Q4 option B: listAudit, editor_permission, enrolment-token read for DMO/WM, PII reveal.

- 2026-10-18 D-MIG-ASSIGN-02 (lead, session_01MbUQSxrP7AB9tbyANjUTPS): migration numbers for backend-authz session 2, assigned so two heads never collide: `V0085` the `app.role_grant_map` seed that grants every `cfg.edit.*` permission to ADMIN and SUPERADMIN when `cfg_key.editor_permission` is enforced (D-AUTHZ-QA-Q4); `V0086` the PII reveal re-auth key (`cfg.pii.reauth_min`) and anything else the reveal flow needs (D-AUTHZ-QA-Q3), only if that item is built. Option B of Q3/Q4 needs no migration (72776516). The held hardening branch renumbers its four migrations to the next free numbers after these when the owner says "promote hardening" (D-MIG-RANGE-01), so a lane that needs one more number asks the lead rather than taking one.

- 2026-10-18 D-AUDIT-PII-01 (lead, session_01MbUQSxrP7AB9tbyANjUTPS, on backend-authz #2's finding with listAudit 73bc56f): audit before/after images store `owner_name`, `full_name`, `address`, `pda_contact_no`, `dep_name` and `lat`/`lng` in clear (`AdminCtx.audit` redacts only phone and email). `listAudit` masks all of them for every role until the PII reveal flow exists (D-AUTHZ-QA-Q3), so nothing leaks through the new route, and the stored rows are unchanged on purpose: the audit chain hashes the stored image, so redacting at write time changes what is proved and needs its own ruling. Decision: keep reads masked now; backend-authz folds a proposal (redact at write for new rows only, keep old rows readable only through the reveal flow, hash-chain impact spelled out) into the PII reveal item and does not change write-time behaviour before the lead rules on it.
- 2026-10-18 D-AUDIT-PII-01 RULING (lead, on the backend-authz session 3 proposal in DECISIONS D-AUTHZ-PII/D-AUDIT-PII-01; 21:10 UTC): APPROVED with three changes. (1) New audit rows only: the platform writer `AuditLog.write` replaces the value of every personal-text member named in `AuditMask.PII` (full_name, phone, email, contact_number, owner_name, address, pda_contact_no, dep_name, nid, tin, trade_license, provisional_* text) with `[redacted]` before the insert, at any depth, as `AdminCtx.audit` already does for phone/email. Old rows are never rewritten (V0010 trigger, hash chain, WORM copy) and stay readable in clear only through listAudit under the reveal (ADMIN/SUPERADMIN, step-up, audited per request). (2) lat/lng are NOT redacted when they are an OUTLET pin: an outlet coordinate is a business location, not personal data, and the before/after image of a pin move is the forensic record for pin tampering and spoofing (docs/05, outlet pin history). Any other lat/lng in an audit image (a user, device or visit position, if a write site carries one) IS redacted; the lane lists those write sites in DECISIONS. The listAudit READ masking of lat/lng stays as built (default-deny for ANALYST/SUPPORT, unmasked only under the reveal). (3) No keyed fingerprint now: it needs a new Key Vault secret in the test account; `[redacted]` only. A fingerprint (new rows only) can be added later if investigators need equality comparison. Direction note: tightening later (more members, a fingerprint) is possible, loosening is not (a redacted value is gone), so the lane errs to the narrower member list and logs every member it leaves in clear with the reason. Tests (T1, Opus checker): redaction at every depth and in arrays; `app.audit_verify()` passes on a mix of old clear rows and new redacted rows; listAudit under the reveal shows old rows in clear and new rows `[redacted]`; a test that no audit write bypasses the platform writer (the ~60 sites); a pin-move row keeps its outlet lat/lng. Owner-visible trade-off (report in the morning summary): after this change the audit log can no longer show what a name, phone or address was changed FROM for new rows; the entity tables keep only the current value. Sequence: after PII reveal (24f56a23) is on INT and deployed; one push, own green run, 'ready <sha>'. AuthzAuditTest NOTE 'pii-claim': test and authz-audit-baseline.txt stay untouched (qa archived; the note is recorded unconditionally and is answered by D-AUTHZ-PII).
- 2026-10-18 D-TSO-BUNDLE-OUTLETS (lead rulings to lead-backend, 2026-10-17 23:24 and 2026-10-18 01:04 UTC): the TSO bundle carries the zone-wide routes and outlets of its reach (the AMO builder, F-AMO-044; commit 3f3184bd), superseding the "TSO bundle stays outlet-free" part of D-TSO-027-SCOPE. TSO bundle outlet fields = AMO fields (owner_name, contact_number unmasked, open_due_mtk), reach-bounded. Fleet-size territories are a final-account item (docs/requests/backend-tso-bundle-fleet-scale.md); no cap at pilot size.
- 2026-10-18 D-OUTLET-REQ-REPLAY (lead, 2026-10-18 01:41 UTC, from the Opus checker on 09071414): a replayed POST of an outlet request answers the caller's own record with its current state (status, trail with verifier/approver ids and notes, decision_reason), also after the outlet left the caller's reach (GET then answers 404). It is the caller's own record; revisit if the trail should be trimmed to creation-time fields.
- 2026-10-18 D-AUTHZ-Q2 (backend-authz, lead queue item Q2): the `/v1/admin/config/*` reads (keys, values, changes, versions, reach, blast-radius, whatif, density, calibration) follow docs/24 s8.5 "Config changes": ADMIN and SUPERADMIN only; DMO, WM, TOP, ANALYST and SUPPORT get 403 (they read nothing there before s8.5 either). One exception, because a built screen needs it: the TSO web entry page resolves `cfg.web.entry_classes` for its zone, so `GET /v1/admin/config/resolve` stays open to the TSO, reach-scoped (lead-backend's ConfigScope: every node of the chain inside the reach, else 403 ERR_OUT_OF_SCOPE). The TSO radius proposal writes through `POST /v1/admin/config/changes` and reads nothing. The web admin config pages (ADMIN_PORTAL_ROLES) still list SUPPORT; web-dashboard is told. With only national readers left, the change, version and reach lists need no reach filter (Q1 rest closed by this rule).
- 2026-10-18 D-AUTHZ-Q5 (backend-authz; lead rulings 2026-10-18): admin permissions (`finance.backdate` first) are per role in `app.role_grant_map`, read per request through the user store's 10 s cache and added by the bearer guard to every API principal; tokens carry none, so a grant and a revoke hold within 10 s. A price batch's back-date takes effect at the approval, so `decidePriceBatch` judges the APPROVER's `finance.backdate` at approval time (a submit-time flag no longer carries it; a batch that came due while pending needs it too); replays answer first. Follow-up, its own item after the rebuild lands (not bundled): grants are SQL rows without an admin route or a record of a revoke; build the write through a C3 config change and the audit log.
- 2026-10-18 D-AMO-REPORT-02 (lead ruling to lead-backend, 2026-10-18 04:23 UTC; supersedes the "route-std is web-only" part of D-AMO-REPORT-01): the AMO role runs exactly these reports: std-memo, sales-summary, route-memo and route-std, zone-scoped by the token (docs/requests/android-amo-report-access.md); every other report key is refused (403). qa's AuthzAuditTest models the same set.
- 2026-10-18 D-AUTHZ-Q2 amendment (backend-authz, lead 2026-10-18): the resolve exceptions are a per-role allowlist (`RESOLVE_GRANTS` in ConfigApi): TSO -> `cfg.web.entry_classes` at a zone in its reach; SUPPORT -> `cfg.calendar.weekend_days` at global (the calendar page). Each: current value only (no `at`), no keys, values, history or writes. The Releases page needs no config read for SUPPORT (it reads GET /v1/admin/releases/policy; only the APK size hint comes from config, web hides it for SUPPORT).
- 2026-10-18 D-AUTHZ-Q3Q4 (backend-authz; lead option B 2026-10-18, V0085 released): `cfg.auth.mfa_required_roles` and `cfg.price.max_change_pct` are C2 for an open-ended tightening or no change, and C3 (a SUPERADMIN requests, a second approves) when the change loosens the value now or at any boundary of the rows it overrides, or carries an end (`effective_to`): a window's tail falls back to the default and could be judged against rows that start only during the C2 delay (Opus checkers B1, B3). While a change of either key is scheduled or pending, no other change of it is accepted (409 ERR_REQUEST_STATE; B2). docs/24 s9.5 rows read "C2; C3 when loosened or windowed". Open (non-blocking): editor_permission (cfg.edit.security, cfg.edit.finance) is still not checked on config changes.
- 2026-10-18 D-18-FIXED-KEY (lead ruling to lead-backend, 2026-10-18 04:58 UTC): `cfg.memo.allow_negative_net` is fixed by D-18 and is not a runtime switch: a negative net is allowed only for a zero-gross sale with QC (a credit); gross > 0 with net < 0 is always quarantined. Neither the server nor the phones read the key (both apply D-18 directly). The key stays in the V0006 catalogue for now, documented as fixed; a later migration (after the V0084-V0087 range held by lead-hardening lands) marks it read-only or removes it from the catalogue and seed.
- 2026-10-18 D-PDA-REPLAY-AGE (lead ruling 2026-10-18 04:58 UTC; V0084 by D-MIG-RANGE-01, lead 05:17 UTC on lane/lead-docs-night 2e962851: lead-hardening renumbers its V0084-V0087 at promotion): a stored PDA to Support upload (POST /v1/support/pda-upload, same upload_uuid, user, size and hash) is replayed for a fresh write SAS only while it is younger than `cfg.support.upload_replay_max_h` (default 168 h = 7 days, the sync backdate window; 1..720). An older replay answers 400 replay_expired (the phone marks the job refused, last error http_400; the rep sends again, which mints a new upload_uuid); nothing is stored or applied. It is a diagnostic file, never money or counts.
- 2026-10-18 D-AUTHZ-AUDIT (backend-authz session 2, lead queue item 1): `GET /v1/admin/audit` (listAudit) is built in backend/masterdata `AuditRead.kt`. Readers ANALYST, SUPPORT, ADMIN, SUPERADMIN, national (docs/24 s8.5); every other role 403. The read is MFA-gated per request like the config and enrolment-token reads (AZ-1; Opus checker B1: the log carries committed config values). Newest first, keyset on `id` (the cursor is the last id; ids of concurrent writers can interleave by design of V0010, so a row committed late behind a cursor is seen on a fresh first page, never twice), limit 1..500 (default 100), filters entity, entity_id, actor_user_id, action, from/to business date (at most 92 days). Masking (`AuditMask`): never shown to anyone: `prefix` (the first six characters of an enrolment-token secret), `token_id`, a defensive list of secret names and any member whose name looks like secret material, at any depth, and the value of a config row whose key name looks secret. Personal data (owner_name, full_name, phone, email, address, lat/lng, ..., matched case-insensitively with name patterns) is shown only to a token carrying the PII grant (docs/21 s4.5; none does until the reveal flow, D-AUTHZ-QA-Q3), so ADMIN/SUPERADMIN see every other value, free text included. ANALYST and SUPPORT: default deny: only changed members are listed, a value only when the entity's allowlist names it and it is a scalar; any other member, a new key included, shows `[masked]`, and the free-text `reason` is masked. Nested images are shown to ADMIN as their masked JSON text (the contract allows scalars only). No contract change: `row_hash` is the chain field the contract carries; `chain_seq` and `prev_hash` stay server-side (`app.audit_verify`). Found by the write-site audit, not fixed here (outside this item): `AdminCtx.audit` redacts phone/email but stores `owner_name`, `full_name`, `address`, `pda_contact_no`, `dep_name` and outlet lat/lng in clear, and direct writers redact nothing; the read masks them.
- 2026-10-18 D-AUTHZ-EDITOR (backend-authz session 2, lead D-AUTHZ-QA-Q4, migration V0085 by D-MIG-ASSIGN-02): `cfg_key.editor_permission` is enforced per request. A config change set (create, a revert, a role-permission change, the adoption of a TSO proposal, break-glass included) needs every item key's `editor_permission` among the caller's role grants in `app.role_grant_map`, read inside the request (no cache: a revoke holds at once); the second SUPERADMIN approving a C3 change needs them too (the approval is where it takes effect, as D-AUTHZ-Q5). Missing: 403 ERR_FORBIDDEN naming the keys and permissions. V0085 seeds every `cfg.edit.*` the registry names to ADMIN and SUPERADMIN, so nothing changes today; EditorPermissionTest fails when a later migration adds a new `cfg.edit.*` without seeding its grants. Not covered (logged): the TSO radius proposal keeps its own rule (`cfg.geo.tso_radius_mode`, cfg.geo.radius_m only), a scheduled C2 change applies by the job even if the requester's grant is revoked during the delay (cancel it instead), and grants are still SQL rows with no admin route or revoke audit (the Q5 follow-up). An adopt is judged on the adopter's grants before the TSO proposal is cancelled (checker B2). Visible side effect: `GET /v1/me` `permissions` of ADMIN and SUPERADMIN now lists the six `cfg.edit.*` grants (additive, contract-valid). Governed elsewhere: AdminPrices `bumpListVersion` writes `cfg.price.list_version` (editor cfg.edit.finance) as a derived marker of a price publish, which keeps the price-batch rules (separation of duties, finance.backdate), not this grant.
- 2026-10-18 D-AUTHZ-ENROL-READ (backend-authz session 2, lead D-AUTHZ-QA-Q5/Q6, docs/24 s8.5 R): `GET /v1/admin/enrolment-tokens` answers DMO and WM too, limited to tokens whose `zone_id` is in the caller's reach (a token with no zone is national and shown to a national reach only), and stripped to ids, status and expiry: `token_prefix` (the first six characters of the secret) is answered as `------` (the contract requires six characters; a contract change to make it optional would be breaking), `note` (free text) is null. Minting and revoking stay ADMIN, SUPERADMIN, SUPPORT; the TSO stays refused (its s8.5 cell is the OTP panel). DMO and WM are not in `cfg.auth.mfa_required_roles` by default, so this read needs no second factor for them.
- 2026-10-18 D-AUTHZ-PII (backend-authz session 3, lead D-AUTHZ-QA-Q3, docs/21 s4.5; migration V0086 by D-MIG-ASSIGN-02; contract additive): the PII reveal is built. `POST /v1/auth/reauth` (web API sessions only) takes the password and, for an MFA user, a fresh 6-digit TOTP code (no recovery codes, no reused step); the role must hold `pii.reveal` in `app.role_grant_map` (403 before any hashing otherwise). Wrong answers share one 401 `ERR_AUTH_INVALID_CREDENTIALS` and lock the step-up only (`reauth|user` key, `cfg.auth.lockout_*`, lockout security event). Success writes the step-up audit row first (entity `pii_reveal`, action `step_up`, `pii_until` only), then mints a web access token with the session's `amr` plus claims `pii` and `pii_until` = now + `cfg.pii.reauth_min` (V0086: default 15, 1..15, global, C2, restrictive downwards, `cfg.edit.security`; docs/19 said 1..120 and G ROLE: narrowed because the web token lives 15 minutes and a refresh mints a token without the reveal, so a longer value would be a lie). The refresh token is unchanged. Server-side check (AuthGuard): the verifier honours `pii` only inside `pii_until`; the guard then requires a web API token, the role's live `pii.reveal` grant (10 s gate cache, so a revoke closes an open window) and a written audit row for the request (entity `pii_reveal`, action `read`, method, path without the query string, `pii_until`; never a personal value); if any fails the request is served masked, never refused. `GET /v1/me` reports `pii` and the new `pii_until` and writes no row. V0086 seeds `pii.reveal` to ADMIN and SUPERADMIN only. What a reveal unmasks is what each endpoint already gated on `pii` (user phone, outlet contact number, report `pii` columns, outlet-request radio/contact); in `listAudit` it unmasks for ADMIN/SUPERADMIN only the members named in `AuditMask.PII`, never a member that merely matches the personal-name pattern, never NEVER_SHOW; ANALYST and SUPPORT keep the default-deny view whatever their token says (docs/24 s8.5 gives them R on the audit log, nothing on PII). Opus checker B1/B2 (fixed): another user's export with personal columns (`GET /v1/report-exports/{id}`, ADMIN/SUPERADMIN viewers) gets a download link only while the viewer's own reveal holds (so the guard audited that request); the owner keeps the link of their own export. Every read a reveal serves unmasked charges `cfg.pii.list_rows_per_hour` (`PiiReadBudget.chargeRevealed`: admin users and outlets lists and single reads, the outlet detail, `GET /v1/outlets`, `GET /v1/outlets/nearby`, an outlet request, an ADMIN/SUPERADMIN `listAudit` page; 429 beyond; round-2 B3/B4), as the reports already did; masked reads cost nothing. The reveal row is written after the rate limit (N2). Not covered (logged): phone tokens never reveal (the field roles' clear-view of docs/21 s4.5 is not built, they stay masked as today); a queued export keeps the `pii_included` decided when it was requested inside the window, and its owner keeps the 24 h link after the window or a revoke; the read row carries the route, not the row count (the budget table holds the count per user and hour); single-record responses of audited writes (create, patch, outlet-request decisions) are not charged; an admin fetching another user's PII export inside a reveal is audited but not charged (the exporter paid `cfg.pii.export_rows_per_day`); module test guards grant `pii.reveal` to every role, the real per-role grant is proved by PiiRevealTest (production wiring). Synchronous print/xlsx report exports with personal columns stay capped by `cfg.pii.export_rows_per_day` (not the hourly budget), as before. A revealed audit page or nearby answer charges every row served, personal member or not (the safe direction; the web should say why a long browse can answer 429). Opus checker: PASS in round 3 (B1 to B4 closed).
- 2026-10-18 D-AUDIT-PII-01 proposal (backend-authz session 3, for the lead's ruling; nothing changed at write time): (1) New rows only: `AuditLog.write` (the one platform writer every module goes through) replaces the value of every member named in `AuditMask.PII` (full_name, phone, email, contact_number, owner_name, address, pda_contact_no, dep_name, nid, tin, trade_license, lat, lng, provisional_*), at any depth, with `[redacted]` before the insert, as `AdminCtx.audit` already does for phone/email; "changed or not" stays visible because the member stays listed. Optional variant: a keyed fingerprint (`hmac:` + 16 hex, HMAC-SHA256 with a Key Vault key) instead of `[redacted]`, so equal values stay comparable without the value. Cost: the audit log can no longer show what a name or coordinate was changed from; the entity tables keep only the current value. (2) Old rows are never rewritten: the V0010 trigger refuses UPDATE/DELETE, and each `row_hash` covers `prev_hash` plus the stored image, so rewriting one row would change every later hash and disagree with the WORM export. Old rows stay readable in clear only through `listAudit` under the reveal (ADMIN/SUPERADMIN, step-up, audited per request), masked for everyone else. (3) Hash-chain impact: none for verification. Redaction happens before the insert, so the chain proves the redacted image exactly as it proves any image today; `app.audit_verify()` is unchanged. The cut-over point is the first row after the deploy (no marker column needed; a reader cannot tell a redacted value from a person typing `[redacted]`, which is harmless). Recommendation: (1) with `[redacted]`, lat/lng included.

- 2026-10-18 D-AUDIT-PII-01 implemented (backend-authz session 4, on the ruling above). `AuditLog.write` (backend/platform) runs `AuditLog.redact(entity, image)` on before and after before the insert, so the chain trigger hashes the redacted image and `app.audit_verify()` is unchanged. Members redacted, exact names, case-insensitive, at any depth and inside arrays, a whole object/array value replaced: `AuditLog.PII` = full_name, phone, email, contact_number, owner_name, address, pda_contact_no, dep_name, nid, tin, trade_license; `AuditLog.GEO` = lat, lng, provisional_lat, provisional_lng, redacted unless the entity is in `AuditLog.OUTLET_PIN` = {outlet}. A JSON null stays null. Old rows are never touched. `AuditMask.PII` (listAudit read side) must equal PII + GEO (test). `AdminCtx.audit`'s older `redactPii` stays (it also drops password members; now redundant for the PII names).
  - lat/lng write sites (2026-10-18 sweep of every audit call site in backend/*/src/main, about 70 including multi-line and generic ones): only entity `outlet` carries coordinates (AdminCrud create/update of outlets incl. pin moves and clears, `location_history` of the outlet image). No audit image carries a user, device or visit position today (device enrol/status/directive, tasks, tracking actions, quarantine, notifications, MFA, PII reveal: none has lat/lng). GEO outside an outlet is therefore a guard for future writers. `provisional_lat/lng` appear in no image today; outlet images keep them (they are proposed outlet pins), other entities lose them.
  - Members left in clear at write, with the reason (the read mask still hides them from everyone without the reveal and from ANALYST/SUPPORT always): look-alike names that only `AuditMask.PII_LIKE` catches (`owner_phone`, `home_lat`, `Owner_Phone`, a `*_bn` name variant): narrow list per the ruling, none is written by any site today; the business `name`/`name_bn` of outlets, routes and geography nodes (business data, not a person); `username` and `employee_code` (system identifiers used in every report); free text (task title/description/resolution note, tracking note, notification title_en, reason column, report filters): typed by staff, may mention a person, but redacting it would make the trail useless; `entity_id` (ids only).
  - Readers of audit images that drive behaviour (checked before redacting): `tracking_action` replay (route_id, business_date, note, notified_user_ids), `outlet_kind_batch` replay (fingerprint, updated, unchanged), `paper_backfill` existence check (no other reader in backend main or db/migrations besides `audit_verify`). None reads a redacted member, so no behaviour changes. No undo, replay or approval reads a personal member.
  - Known effect (owner-visible, as the ruling says): for new rows the trail shows that a personal member was in the image (and, in AdminCrud updates which list only changed members, that it changed) but not its old or new value. listAudit's ANALYST/SUPPORT "changed members" view compares stored values, so a personal member changed in a new row shows to them as unchanged (both sides `[redacted]`); ADMIN/SUPERADMIN see the member listed. Read masking kept as built per the ruling; if the lead wants the 'changed' bit back for ANALYST/SUPPORT, unequal values could be written as a distinct marker (new rows only), not done (Opus checker N1).
  - Tests: AuditLogTest (redaction at every depth/array/case, null kept, outlet keeps coordinates at depth, look-alikes left; chain verifies over old clear + new redacted rows), AuditReadTest (reveal shows an old row in clear and a new row `[redacted]`, chain verifies), AdminOutletsTest (create row: owner_name/contact_number redacted, lat/lng kept; first pin and a move of an existing pin keep old and new lat/lng), AuditWriteRedactionTest (no `INSERT INTO`/`COPY` into audit_log in backend main sources, db/migrations, db/seed, db/src/main, infra, tools or scripts except AuditLog.kt; a regex scan, so SQL split across concatenation or dynamic EXECUTE is not caught (none today); write and read lists agree).
- 2026-10-18 D-UI-REF-01 (lead, on the OWNER's message of 2026-10-18 ~22:10 UTC: "why the dashboard section of web portal is so different from the existing one ... why there is no packshot ... why you didn't follow the design of the existing one from the screenshots I provided ... in the morning I want the apps look alike of the existing Aron app, web dashboard"): the owner's reference screenshots are the visual spec for the SR, AMO and TSO apps and the web dashboard and portal; Calm Glass (docs/32, owner-approved 2026-10-07) no longer defines the look. Cause, stated plainly: the lanes were told to build on docs/32 and the lead ruled the web shell "Calm Glass, not the crimson sidebar" (web-dashboard self-review ruling 3), so the screenshots were used for function and not for the look; that was the lead's error, now reversed. Spec and acceptance: docs/ui-reference/PARITY-SPEC.md; CLAUDE.md rule 14a. Lanes started the same night: core-ui kit and icons, SR screens (two lanes), AMO, TSO, web shell and dashboard, packshot data path. Known gaps stated up front: no AMO or TSO screenshots exist in git (layouts from the manuals in the same visual language until the owner sends screenshots); the pack pictures must come from AKTCL (admin portal upload); until then dev builds use crops of the owner's own screenshots for the five SKUs they show. Everything else in the standing rules is unchanged (offline-first, units, deferred programmes, perf budgets).
- 2026-10-18 D-PACKSHOT-01 (lane ui-packshots; PARITY-SPEC s2.6; lead rulings 2026-10-18 on proposal B; T1, Opus checker): pack pictures reach the phone as the stable `Sku.image_asset_id` in the bundle, never a signed URL (the bundle is fingerprinted and pre-generated; a read SAS expires after a day). The API streams the bytes from `GET /v1/sku-images/{asset_id}` (no SAS leaves it; immutable cache headers, ETag = asset id; a bare 404 unless it is a bound sku_image of at most 300 KB). Deviation from the lead's ruling, told to the lead: the bundle carries the asset id and the phone builds the path on its own origin, rather than a baked `<api origin>/...` image_url, because the server has no public-origin setting. The phone caches the bytes under the asset id (a new upload is a new id), preloads after each sync run (mobile data allowed), and the screens read disk only. Dev builds (`-Paron.devPackshots=true`, the dev-origin CI release job only) carry three crops of `docs/ui-reference/sr/stock.webp` for the six Maxim seed SKUs; production never does. The real art comes from AKTCL (docs/runbooks/sku-pack-pictures.md). Room v8; no Postgres migration (V0087 released).

## 2026-10-19 — Aron logo and pack-picture tooling, re-applied on origin 0675990b (lead; streams J1 to J5)

- 2026-10-19 Reading note on ids (lead): the first version of this work (2026-10-18, local commit 0b15ed92 on the unpushed branch `backup/brand-first-design`) used the ids D-BRAND-01, D-PACK-01, D-PACK-02 and D-BP-*. They were never in this file. Comments, READMEs, tests and the loader runbook written in the re-apply still cite D-BRAND-01 and D-PACK-01: read D-BRAND-01 as D-BRAND-02, and D-PACK-01 as D-PACK-03 (the chain) plus D-PACK-04 (the public repository) below. The one rule of the old D-PACK-01 that is NOT carried over is "show pack pictures Fit (letterbox), never Crop": the kit crops, see D-PACK-03 point (c). Detail, verification and open items are in `docs/status/brand-packshots.md`.
- 2026-10-19 D-BRAND-02 (lead, on the owner's ask of 2026-10-18 "find the 'aron' logo attached to use everywhere it required"; streams J1 to J4): the Aron logo is in the product wherever the current UI draws a brand mark, and deliberately nowhere else. Provenance: the owner supplied the artwork on 2026-10-18 (the manifest says "supplied by the owner"; AKTCL owns the Aron IP per CLAUDE.md); that answers Q-UI-13 for the logo only, and the owner may confirm ownership in one line. Master `assets/brand/aron-logo-original.png` (500x499 RGBA, transparent), committed byte for byte, sha256 in `assets/brand/manifest.json`; every derived raster comes from `tools/brand/build_brand_assets.py` (Pillow: crop, LANCZOS resize and placement; the one derivative that is not a plain resize is the Android 13 themed-icon layer, a tone map of the same pixels), verified by its `--check`. Palette unchanged: the logo is artwork, not a theme source, and no token or accent changes. Where it is. (1) Launcher: ONE adaptive icon per app (minSdk 26), the shared foreground and monochrome layers in core-ui (WebP at xhdpi, xxhdpi, xxxhdpi) and a per-app plate colour that keeps F-SYS-033 (the three apps stay tellable side by side): SR `#0B5D3B`, AMO `#1F4E8C`, TSO `#8C1D18`; each manifest points `android:icon` and `android:roundIcon` at `@mipmap/ic_launcher`; labels and package ids are unchanged; the three placeholder vector icons are deleted. The glyph is 60 dp tall in the 108 dp layer, and its farthest pixel that is at least 16/255 opaque lies 32.70 to 32.93 dp from the centre, inside the 33 dp radius of the 66 dp safe zone (`assets/README.md`). (2) In the apps: `AronBrandMark` and `AronBrandPlate` (core-ui `AronBrandMark.kt`), a bundled 256 px resource on a solid plate whose fill is a parameter defaulting to the look's `card` colour (solid white in the standard and Sunlight looks); decorative (`contentDescription = null`, so no string), with no blur, gradient, alpha or animation. The only brand-mark slot in the current UI spec is `BrandBlock` in feature-auth `AuthKit.kt` (login, enrolment and OTP of all three apps): a 72 dp plate carrying a 64 dp logo replaces the drawn peak glyph. (3) Web: one `BrandMark` (`web/src/components/brand-mark.tsx`, a plain decorative `img` with a 1x/2x/3x srcset on a round white plate that uses the `bg-on-rail` token) in the sidebar rail and drawer (36 px plate), the mobile top bar (32 px plate, replacing the dashboard glyph) and the sign-in card (44 px plate); `web/public/brand/aron-mark-{64,128,256}.png`, `icon.png` (192), `apple-icon.png` (180) and `favicon.ico` through the Next file conventions. The proxy matcher lets a signed-out visitor load exactly those files (`robots.txt` and `favicon.ico` are now `$`-anchored, a small tightening: `/favicon.ico/x` and `/robots.txt.bak` are gated now), and `next.config.ts` gives each of those files its own Cache-Control rule, never immutable because the names are not content-hashed. (4) The printed report header: `ReportOutputs.printHtml` embeds the 64 px mark (`backend/analytics/src/main/resources/brand/aron-mark-64.png`, read once) as a `data:` URI, 28 px, empty alt, left of the title; a missing or oversized resource prints the report without the mark (one warning, never an exception); the xlsx export is unbranded. Deliberately NOT branded: the printed 58 mm memo and every slip (they match the current Aron; whether to print the logo there is an owner yes or no, default no), the push small icon (stays the bell: a status-bar icon must be a silhouette and there is no vector master), dpc, every dense working screen (sale, stock, review, memo, picker, visit, attendance, summary, submit, tasks), the default top bar, the blank Restoring state, and, because neither the parity spec nor the lanes' status files give them a mark slot, the first-bundle download screen, the update page and the Settings version row. Tests: `AppIconTest` in each app, `AronBrandMarkTest`, `web/tests/brand-assets.test.ts`, the smoke spec, `ReportPrintMarkTest`. Not checked on a device: the launcher icons on a real launcher (round, squircle, Android 13 themed), the small marks on a low-density field phone (device check D-UI-04), and whether the three inherited plate colours stay tellable for a red-green colour-blind rep (the label ARON SR / AMO / TSO is the other difference).
- 2026-10-19 D-PACK-03 (lead; streams J1 and J5; replaces the pack-picture half of the first design of 2026-10-18): pack pictures follow the ui-packshots lane's design, D-PACKSHOT-01, and only that. The chain as this tree's code has it: admin asset store (purpose `sku_image`) -> `app.sku.image_asset_id` -> the sync bundle's `Sku.image_asset_id` (never a link) -> each phone downloads a picture once from `GET /v1/sku-images/{asset_id}` (the API streams the bytes; there is no read link for pack pictures) -> the phone's disk cache under the asset id -> the Stock and Sale rows (the kit's `PackCard` and `PackshotImage`) draw it from disk. `PackshotPreload` runs after each completed sync run (at most 100 pictures per run, mobile data allowed). Room version 8 and its `sku.image_asset_id` column are the lane's; we add no Room version, no migration and no schema file. Our first design was built and reviewed on 2026-10-18 and DROPPED on 2026-10-19, when origin shipped the lane's version. It was a signed `Sku.image_url` in the bundle (an Azure user-delegation SAS with a timer-renewed key, a bundle version computed on the path form, a failed-link marker), a Room `sku.image_url` column with `ForgetBundleCursor7To8`, a picture port (`ImageCache.fetchResult`, `SkuThumbnails`, `PackImageSource`, `AronSkuThumb`, a post-sync job), `SaleSku.imageUrl`, AMO wiring, a thumbnail column in the web admin SKU list, a contract redaction for `image_url`, and brand marks on the first-bundle, update and Settings rows. The lane's design is simpler for the reason D-PACKSHOT-01 gives (an asset id is stable, a signed link expires, and the bundle is pre-generated and fingerprinted) and was already shipped, so keeping ours would have meant two designs for one feature. The first design stays, unpushed, in the local branch `backup/brand-first-design` (commit 0b15ed92), is read with `git show`, and is never merged or cherry-picked; none of it is in the tree. What we add on top and keep: the bulk loader `tools/data/load_packshots.py` with `prepare_packshots.py`, their tests and the runbook `docs/runbooks/load-packshots.md`, which put the owner's 42 pictures through the same audited admin API as the lane's one-SKU runbook (`docs/runbooks/sku-pack-pictures.md`); the processed set (long edge at most 320 px, never upscaled, JPEG q82 4:4:4, no metadata, at most 300 KB, content-addressed asset ids; 42 files, 854 KB, 37 distinct pictures; git-ignored); the hash manifest `assets/packshots/manifest.csv`; the two READMEs; and the repo hygiene of D-PACK-04. The loader reads each bound picture back twice, from the blob link and from `GET /v1/sku-images/{asset_id}` (the route the phones use), and a phone-path failure right after a fresh bind stops the run. Findings, read from the code and not run on a phone: (a) a sync run does not bring a phone its changed bundle, because the sync answer carries `bundle_version_current = null` (`IngestService.kt`): an SR phone takes it at app start (force-stop and reopen), on coming to the front after a gate of 30 to 240 minutes, or on the next business day, and the picture download follows the next completed sync run; the lane's runbook says "the next sync", which is not so; the AMO and TSO apps have no start-up or foreground bundle download in this tree, so a phone check starts on SR; (b) a failed preload download is remembered until the app process restarts (`SkuPackshots.failed`), where the lane's runbook says it is retried after every sync; (c) the kit draws the picture with `ContentScale.Crop` in a 56 x 90 dp slot, so Aster (a landscape 320 x 175 group shot) loses its sides; the first design's Fit (letterbox) is NOT adopted here and the choice is the kit lane's and the lead's; (d) the admin SKU list and PATCH answer carry `image_url` but not `image_asset_id`, which the contract's `Sku` declares, so the loader reads the binding from either. Not done: the loader has never touched a real environment, and a run needs the owner's yes in chat for that environment. Four Black Diamond SKUs are suspect (`BDA-10s`, `BDDF-10s`, `BDA-20s`, `BDDF-20s`: the two 20s files are byte-identical and the two 10s files show the same art); the owner decides between loading all 42 and `--skip-suspect` first.
- 2026-10-19 D-PACK-04 (lead; public repository; replaces D-PACK-02 of the first design): the repository is public until the product is complete (CLAUDE.md rule 13) and git history cannot be retracted, so no pixel of the 42 pack pictures the owner supplied on 2026-10-18, or of anything made from them, goes into any tracked or to-be-tracked file: not the originals, not the processed copies, not thumbnails or crops, not web mock fixtures, not Roborazzi or Playwright goldens, not test resources, not docs. Tests and fixtures draw synthetic pictures in code. What is committed is `assets/packshots/manifest.csv` (sku_code, source file, sizes, sha256, duplicate group, note; no pixels) and `assets/packshots/README.md`. The root `.gitignore` is an allowlist: `assets/packshots/*` is ignored (any file type in any case, and the whole `processed/` folder) except those two files. That is all it guarantees: it does not stop `git add -f` or a picture copied to another folder (`web/public`, an Android `res` folder, `docs`); the guard for the first is proposed in `docs/requests/brand-infra-ci-gates.md` and does not exist yet. Existing exceptions, untouched and not these files: the owner's reference screenshots of the current Aron under `docs/ui-reference` (`sr/stock.webp` shows MAXIM packs) and the three dev-only stand-in crops cut from one of them under `android/dev-packshots` (the ui-packshots lane's). The logo is the opposite case: AKTCL artwork supplied for this purpose and committed on purpose (D-BRAND-02). If the owner decides to commit the pictures after all (a written yes in chat, never an agent's call): (1) only after the repository is private, because a public push is permanent; (2) delete the `assets/packshots/*` block from `.gitignore` and commit `processed/` (854 KB), not the originals (five originals carried EXIF, XMP or comment segments, two of them with an author name; the processed copies carry none); (3) the loader, the runbook and the manifest need no change; (4) the web mock, the goldens and the unit-test fixtures stay synthetic unless the owner says otherwise in the same message; (5) update `assets/README.md`, `assets/packshots/README.md` and this entry, and retire the CI guard.
- 2026-10-19 D-BP-A-apks (lead): `.gitignore` ignores `/apks/`, anchored to the repository root: the operator's local drop folder for CI-built dev APKs with their `SHA256SUMS`, `release-manifest.json` and apksigner `.idsig` files. Release builds come from CI, never from git, and the rule keeps a `git add -A` from putting that folder's signed APKs into a public repository. The same block also ignores `/tools/checks/*/apk/` and `/tools/checks/*/transcript.log`, the output of the device-check scripts (signed dev APKs, and PowerShell transcripts that name the laptop); found by the final review, before it was committed. It is not brand work; it rides in the same commit because `.gitignore` is committed whole, and the owner may drop it. The original author of the rule is unconfirmed.
- 2026-10-19 D-BP-A6 (lead): `.gitattributes` has six rules: `*.png`, `*.webp`, `*.jpg`, `*.jpeg` and `*.ico` are binary, and `assets/**/manifest.*` is pinned to LF so the manifests read the same on the CRLF laptop and on the Linux runner. There is no global `text=auto` on purpose: the laptop runs `core.autocrlf=true`, and a global rule would show phantom diffs on files that are already committed.
