Additive; no existing operation changes.

### 2026-10-22 lane mdm (D-MDM-01)
- **D-MDM-GRANTS (interim):** until backend-authz delivers the `modules` token claim, the `mdm` grant is by role: SUPERADMIN and ADMIN write, others none. One seam (`MdmGrants`), request `docs/requests/mdm-authz-module-grant.md`.
- **D-MDM-CMD:** commands are rows (`device_command`, client UUID, `seq` order); signed ES256 `aron-command-v1` like directives, bound to the device; pushed by an FCM data message `kind=mdm_nudge` (no payload) and pulled by the DPC on every sync; the phone acks `acked|failed` with a result of at most 2 KB. A factory reset is created `pending_approval` and has no signature until a different person approves; it expires after the TTL (24 h default).
- **D-MDM-POLICY:** the MDM policy (allowed apps, weekday/time block windows in Dhaka time, kiosk, location sampling) is delivered as an `apply_policy` command `{policy_id, version, policy}` (and `{policy_id:null}` to clear), not through `DevicePolicy` (strict contract, owned by android-geo-dpc); the DPC applies it additively next to the attendance blocking.
- **D-MDM-FLAGS:** compliance flags are derived at read time from the device row and its latest status report (no flag table): rooted, mock_location, outdated_app, developer_options, adb_enabled, not_device_owner, location_off, auto_time_off, policy_drift, stale_contact (48 h). Network shows `unknown` until the report carries `network_type`.
- Backend is behind `ARON_MDM_ENABLED=1` until db lands `docs/requests/db-mdm-device-command.md`; tests apply the same DDL from `mdm_ddl.sql`.
- 2026-10-22 (mdm #2): db landed V0102/V0103; the DDL shim is gone. `DbGrants` reads `app.user_module_grant` for the `mdm` gate until the `modules` claim exists.
- 2026-10-22 D-AUTHZ-ROLES-01 (backend-authz session 5, wave 4 row 1, on D-ROLES-02; contract 1.4.0 by docs/requests/contract-portal-levels.md, lead approval pending; migration requested in docs/requests/db-backend-authz-roles.md): `Role` gains `NATIONAL` and `BRAND`; `TOP` stays in the enum and in `app.role_def` as retired (audit rows name it), and the requested migration moves every TOP user to NATIONAL (scope_version bumped, TOP's role-scoped config values, `menu_by_role` entry and `role_grant_map` rows copied; a CHECK then forbids TOP on `app_user`); the admin users API refuses a new TOP (400 `retired`) and a role code whose migration has not landed (400 `not_available`). Portal levels are enforced in the guard (`PortalLevels`, before any handler, so the handlers' older deny-lists such as `Role.entries - SR - SUPPORT` cannot let a new role through): no web token is ever minted for SR or AMO: `LoginService.complete` (every login, change-password, MFA verify and bind path) answers 403 `ERR_AUTH_PORTAL_NOT_ALLOWED` (new ProblemCode) only after the password matched (a wrong password stays the common 401, so the code never tells an outsider which usernames are reps) and a web refresh family of theirs never refreshes; NATIONAL and TOP read whatever the handlers allow TOP today (NATIONAL added beside TOP in every reader set) and write nothing but their own session (`/v1/auth/**`), `POST /v1/client-errors` and `POST /v1/reports/{key}/query` (403 otherwise; visible change: a TOP user can no longer send notifications, which the owner's level 3 does not include); BRAND has national geography in the reach resolver but may call only `/v1/me` and its own session until the brand-filtered reads of row 3 open. Paths are judged after the router's normalisation (decoded segments, empty ones dropped; a dot segment or an encoded slash is refused). Feature flag: none needed for row 1 (the code is correct before and after the migration: TOP and NATIONAL behave alike, no BRAND user can exist before); test databases get the requested SQL from `backend/platform/src/testFixtures/resources/pending/authz-wave4.sql` (applied by FreshDb only while a piece is missing; deleted when db's migrations land). Tests: PortalLevelsTest (role x method x path table, crafted paths), PortalLevelLoginTest (SR/AMO web login, refresh and token refused, app login unchanged, NATIONAL/BRAND/TOP web login), PendingAuthzSchemaTest (TOP migration run twice ends in the same state; values, grants, menus copied; TOP refused afterwards; a brand scope must name a brand; house scope rows accepted).
  - Opus checker (row 1, round 1): B1 fixed: a phone token serves only its flavour's field role (sr SR, amo AMO, tso TSO): the guard refuses any other role on a phone API token (403), a phone family's full grant does not refresh after a role change, `complete()` (every path into a session: login, change-password, MFA verify, bind) refuses a web session for SR/AMO and a phone session for another role, and a role change in the admin users API revokes the user's full refresh families (`admin_force_logout`). The upload grant survives a role change as it survives a disable (captured rows always reach the server; ingest judges each record by the uploader's assignment). N3 fixed: the own-session allowlist names the four `/v1/auth` routes (logout, change-password, mfa/enrol, reauth), not the prefix. N4 fixed in the requested SQL: TOP rows already superseded are not copied; a role-list value in effect that names TOP (any `*_roles` key, `cfg.auth.mfa_required_roles` above all, so MFA never loosens) is closed at the migration and replaced by the same list plus NATIONAL; the ADMIN module seed inserts only into an empty table, so a re-run never restores a narrowed grant. Left as logged: a role-list value scheduled to start in the future that names TOP cannot be closed (none in the test account); NATIONAL reads are judged by HTTP method (no GET with side effects was found; no inventory test asserts it).
  - Opus checker (row 1, round 2, session 6): B1-bis fixed: the phone upload grant (exempt from the flavour check so captured rows always arrive, D24-57) still minted a token with the user's NEW role after a role change (TSO made NATIONAL or BRAND), and sync/batch judged its records with that role's national reach (a `risk_review` record could change any risk signal in the country). Now the upload refresh mints the token with the phone flavour's field role (rows still arrive; ingest judges them by the old field role's assignment, so they are accepted in reach or quarantined out of reach, never national), and the guard refuses any phone token, upload included, whose role is not its flavour's field role (an upload token minted before the fix waits for its next refresh). N2 fixed: `POST /v1/auth/reauth` refuses SR/AMO like every web mint. N3 fixed in the requested SQL: A2b puts NATIONAL in place of TOP in `*_roles` lists and A4 moves (copies, then deletes) TOP's `role_grant_map` rows, so a re-run never re-adds what a SUPERADMIN removed. Not changed: N1 (an SR/AMO web token is refused at issuance, not in the guard: module test tokens mint SR/AMO as web), N4/N5 pre-existing (logged), N6 BRAND's national geographic reach stays until row 3 opens brand-filtered reads (BRAND_ROUTES is still /v1/me + own session). Tests: PortalLevelsTest (upload token of every non-field role refused), PortalLevelLoginTest (upload refresh after a role change carries the field role), PendingAuthzSchemaTest (no TOP grant left; MFA list names NATIONAL instead of TOP).
- 2026-10-22 D-AUTHZ-MODULES-01 (backend-authz session 6, wave 4 row 2, on D-ROLES-02): ADMIN module grants. `AdminModules` (backend/platform) maps every guarded route to one of the 15 modules of docs/34 s1 (first match, credentials before users, daily-tracking before dashboards), to the caller's own session (`/v1/me`, `/v1/tutorials`, `/v1/app/update-check` and PortalLevels.OWN_SESSION), or to the routes no ADMIN web token serves (`NOT_ADMIN`: sync, devices, phone auth steps); RouteInventoryTest fails on a guarded route in none of them. A web token of an ADMIN carries the claim `modules` `{module: "r"|"w"}` and of a BRAND the claim `brands` [brand node ids], both read by TokenIssuer at every mint (after the user row, so a grant change in between leaves a stale `sv` and the guard asks for a refresh). The guard, after the scope-version check, judges ADMIN web API calls: no claim -> 401 ERR_SCOPE_CHANGED; unmapped route -> 403; module not granted -> 403; a non-read (anything but GET/HEAD/OPTIONS and the read POSTs report query and price preview) on an `r` module -> 403. SUPERADMIN holds every module; other roles are judged by their handlers as before. Feature flag: the grants are live once `app.user_module_grant` and `app.user_brand_scope` exist (probed once per process); before that no claim is minted and ADMIN keeps today's reach. A user made ADMIN (new or by role change) holds no module until a SUPERADMIN grants them (fail closed); migration B seeds `w` on every module for the admins that exist when it runs; the dev seed `db/seed/07_admin_module_grants.sql` does the same for the seeded pilot admins. `Me.modules` (ADMIN its grants, SUPERADMIN all `w`, else null) and `Me.brands` (BRAND) for the portal menu (contract 1.4.0 part 2, additive). The grants API is lane backend-admin's `GET/PUT /v1/admin/users/{id}/access` (72c80eec), reviewed and adopted (module `users`). Tests: AdminModulesTest (role x module x access matrix over a real route per module, read POSTs, credentials vs users, encoded and dot paths, not live, no claim), TokenVerifierTest (claims minted only for web ADMIN/BRAND once live; malformed claims 401), ModuleGrantsTest (production wiring: read-only module refuses a write, ungranted module 403, widened grant -> 401 ERR_SCOPE_CHANGED then the new token works; SUPERADMIN Me lists 15 modules; BRAND token and Me carry its brand), RouteInventoryTest.everyGuardedRouteHasAnAdminModule. Open: MDM's own `MdmGrants` wiring waits for lane mdm's API on INT (the route gate already applies to /v1/admin/mdm/**).

Now an ADMIN may not create, change into or out of, or reset the credentials of NATIONAL, BRAND, ANALYST, SUPPORT (and retired TOP) users: they are the Super Admin's (docs/34 s1), and `POST /v1/admin/users` also needs `credentials:w` (it answers a temporary password; `AdminModules.ALSO_NEEDS`). Not done (proposal for the lead): limiting each geographic role to scope rows at or below its level (a `national` row on a TSO would give nothing); it changes the qa scope-leak oracle, which treats any national row as national reach for every role. N4: risk-signal reviews moved to `daily_tracking`, field notifications and PDA uploads to `mdm`. N5: see B1. N6: an upload token whose role is not its flavour's answers 401 ERR_SCOPE_CHANGED (the app refreshes the upload grant at once) instead of 403 (which stopped sync). N7 (10 s gate cache after a grant change; backend-admin's PUT does not invalidate it) accepted and logged. Tests: AdminModulesTest (create needs credentials, preview is a write, module placements), FieldRoleReachTest, AdminUsersRoutesTest (ADMIN refused on national-reach accounts, SUPERADMIN allowed), PortalLevelsTest (upload mismatch 401).

Options: (a) accept and document that users:w + credentials:w is a people-admin grant equal to the reach of the people it manages (the portal warns when a SUPERADMIN grants both); (b) ADMIN user create / password reset of a portal role also needs `reports` and `daily_tracking` read; (c) only a SUPERADMIN creates portal-role logins. Also logged: a reviewer's release of a quarantined record re-judges it with the uploader's current role (reviewer-initiated, accepted).

Creating, re-roling or resetting an ADMIN or SUPERADMIN login is SUPERADMIN-only (as before). This supersedes the part of the row-2 round-1 B1 fix that made NATIONAL and BRAND SUPERADMIN-only; ANALYST and SUPPORT (internal service roles) stay SUPERADMIN-only (lead 15:07: the ruling's list was the owner's seven levels plus SR/AMO). Every create is audited with the new role, every credential reset with `target_role`, with actor and reason. The portal warns a SUPERADMIN who grants users:w together with credentials:w (web-admin, filed by the lead). The refusal answers 403 ERR_FORBIDDEN with stable detail strings for the cutover (lead 15:07); `ERR_AUTHZ_ROLE_ABOVE_GRANT` is queued in docs/requests/contract-v1.7-queue.md for the lead's batched 1.7.0.

It lists only active houses and only zones whose house is active (merged duplicates stay inactive), returns strictly the caller's reach for every portal level (SUPERADMIN all); backend-reports adds the active filter with a test, backend-authz a scope-leak style test with row 3. The reach resolver reads `house` scope nodes (zones whose `zone.house_id` is the node); the `house` node_type in UserScopeNode/UserScopeWrite waits for contract 1.7 (queue line 2), so until then house nodes are written only by seed or migration.

Ruling: accept and document (the owner gave Admins per-module grants on purpose, docs/34 s1) with two hardenings: an ADMIN may create, re-role or reset credentials only for roles below ADMIN (TSO, DMO, WM, NATIONAL, BRAND, SR, AMO); doing so for an ADMIN or SUPERADMIN login is SUPERADMIN-only (`ERR_AUTHZ_ROLE_ABOVE_GRANT`, 403); every such create or reset is audited with actor, target role and reason and listed under the `users` audit view; web-admin warns a SUPERADMIN who grants `users:w` together with `credentials:w`. Rejected: implicit module coupling (creates needing reports read) and SUPERADMIN-only creation of portal logins.

- D-MDM-BREADCRUMB-01 (2026-10-22, lead instruction to mdm #2): lane mdm owns the breadcrumb install (N-035) for SR and AMO, because no Android lane is active and the MDM location mode (docs/34 s5) only means something once breadcrumbs run. Wired through `DeviceRuntime.breadcrumbs` (controller, state store, `geo_breadcrumb` sink into the outbox via `CaptureRepository.recordBreadcrumb`), check-in/out calls from the SR and AMO attendance committers, and `reconcile` at app start, each config refresh and each MDM policy change. Default OFF: breadcrumbs run only when `cfg.geo.breadcrumbs_enabled` is true or the MDM policy sets `interval`; MDM `off` always wins; `on_demand` leaves the config. Floor 5 minutes (BreadcrumbSettings clamp 5..120). Fused provider at balanced power, batched (`maxUpdateDelay` = interval), no new service, no alarm. TSO is left out on purpose: D-GEO-BG-01 allows background location only in app-sr and app-amo (TSO has no check-in); the shared DeviceRuntime never starts a request there because nothing calls `onAttendanceCommitted`. Sale and check-in fixes do not depend on the policy.
- D-MDM-BREADCRUMB-01 amendment (2026-10-22, after the Opus T1 check): (1) the controller is installed synchronously in `DeviceRuntime.start` (Application.onCreate) and the receiver no longer stops the request when the controller is missing; (2) a window belongs to the user and the Dhaka business date of the check-in (`BreadcrumbOwner`): another day or another signed-in user ends it and points are written only for the owner; (3) MDM `interval` can switch breadcrumbs on only where `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` is held (the DPC grants it only while the device policy allows breadcrumbs, D-GEO-BG-01), otherwise it is ignored: asked the lead whether the DPC grant should also follow MDM `interval`; (4) `captured_offline` comes from connectivity; `stop()` is called only for a request this process started (plus once per process).
  - Lead ruling on (3), 2026-10-22: D-GEO-BG-01 stays. The DPC device policy's `breadcrumbsEnabled` is the single switch for the background-location grant; an MDM `interval` is honoured only where that grant is held and is otherwise ignored, never half-on. Reason: one switch for a battery-sensitive permission, no implicit coupling between two policies. If the owner later wants MDM to drive it, the MDM policy sets `breadcrumbsEnabled` explicitly through the same switch; the grant does not follow the interval.
- 2026-10-22 D-AUTHZ-BRAND-01 (backend-authz session 7, row 3 of docs/lanes/backend-authz.md, within D-ROLES-02 / docs/34 s1): a BRAND caller's brand scope on the national dashboard is read from `app.user_brand_scope` on every request (Wiring.brandScope -> backend-reports' BrandScopeResolver), not from the token's `brands` claim, so a revoked brand stops at the next call without waiting for the scope-version check or the token's expiry (one indexed primary-key read per card). Fail closed: a BRAND user with no brand row, or before the table is deployed, gets 403 on every dashboard card; every other level gets no product restriction (null). ## 2026-10-22 backend-core session 14 (docs/requests/backend-core-qa-1.md; answer in backend-core-qa-1-answer.md)
- **D-BC-MEMONO-01 (backend-core, 2026-10-22).** The server's memo number rule (docs/24 s7.5) accepts a sequence in any of the user's four ordinal blocks (`1 .. 4 x cfg.memo.seq_block_size`) or overflow ranges (`5001..8999` by ordinal), not only the uploading phone's current ordinal: a rebound phone still uploads numbers printed under its old slot, and a paper backfill has no phone; tying it to one binding would refuse printed sales for good. Login = the uploader (capturing user), date = the record's business date. Block size = the current config value (the key is future-dated, new binds only); a lowered value would refuse old high numbers (open item). Children of a memo refused `memo_no_invalid` are refused with it (final), not parked.
- **D-BC-PACK-01 (backend-core, 2026-10-22; lead ruling of the same day).** A `memo_line` or `stock_movement` entered in packs whose `pack_factor` is not `app.sku.base_per_pack` is parked `qty_invalid` and acked `retryable: true` (the ack's `retryable` is authoritative for a parked record, overriding the catalogue default `false` of `qty_invalid`); the memo and its other children in the segment are parked with it, so nothing of the memo is booked. A line held after its memo was stored flags the memo `pack_factor_mismatch`. Comparison is with the current `base_per_pack`: `app.sku` has no pack-size history (the lead's "as of the price-list date" needs a db history table if wanted).
- **D-BC-PREVIEW-01 (backend-core, 2026-10-22; lead ruling).** The Final Submit preview lists every active route (F-TSO-010) but its three warnings count only routes due that day (`route_day.planned` true or the route has data); a route with no route-day row still counts as due (a planner failure never hides a missing login). Showing an unplanned route as "not due" needs an additive `planned` member on the preview route (proposed, contract change, not done).

- 2026-10-23 D-WEB-ADMIN-MENU-02 (web-admin session 6; closes the gap recorded in D-WEB-ADMIN-MENU-01): the write controls of the non-generator pages (calendar, config console, geofence, flags, app-block, config history and changes, permissions, quarantine, day control, data entry, entry unlocks, dues, supervisor targets, print templates, devices, device replace, enrolment, releases, device OTP) follow the ADMIN module grant. One helper, `loadOpGate(token, role)` in `lib/admin/access.ts`, answers per operation: the role rule AND the grant for the module that owns the operation's API path (`moduleOfApiPath`) is `w`. A read-only ADMIN sees the page without its forms and the module read-only note; the API still answers 403. A test (`tests/op-gate.test.ts`) fails if an operation path belongs to no module (it would escape the gate). No claim (grant tables not deployed), SUPERADMIN and other roles keep the role rule only.
## 2026-10-23 backend-reports session 5 (lead rulings of 04:00 UTC)
- **D-REPORTS-VOID-01 (backend-reports, 2026-10-23; lead ruling).** A route-day data void (`POST` data void, masterdata `DataVoidApi`) tombstones the route's transactional records of that business date (visits, memos and their lines, stock movements, due collections, geo fixes, route-day events, and the attendance events that carry that `route_id`) and writes the barrier; it does not erase attendance in general. A check-in is a user-day attendance event: one with no `route_id` is not touched, so the national Successful Login card keeps counting it. By design, not a defect; no masterdata change. An "attendance void" would be a separate admin action if the owner ever asks for one.
- **D-REPORTS-LOGIN-01 (backend-reports, 2026-10-23; lead ruling, owner brief s3.4, db V0108 `checked_in_at`).** One meaning of "Successful Login" everywhere: the SR logged in AND downloaded the bundle AND checked in (`DashboardSummaries.successfulLoginSql`). It now drives the national Login/Submit card, `dw.agg_daily_zone.logged_in_routes` (hence the dashboard KPIs `logged_in_routes`/`login_pct` and the Ops sync-health `login_pct`), the dashboard route children, Daily Tracking (bucket `not_logged_in` and the same-time-yesterday comparator, where each stamp must be at or before the instant), the Ops login/submit lists (not logged in / logged in not submitted) and the reports `daily-tracking` and `final-submit-status` ("Not logged in"). Before the summary columns exist (db V0093 to V0108) the older rule (any day state past `not_started`) holds. A submit counts only on a successful login (`sales_submitted_routes`, the dashboard children and the Ops lists), as the national card already did, so the submit share of the logged-in routes never exceeds 100 %; the `final-submit-status` report keeps counting the submit states as recorded. The check-in of a route-day's acting (stand-in) SR counts for the route even when the event names no route (Opus checker finding). Honest note: counts can be lower than a pure login count (an SR who logged in and sold but never checked in is "not logged in" here); the owner can object in the morning report. The TSO app's own team view (`TeamApi`) still counts by day state and is not changed. The Daily Tracking `state` field and the `data-entry-log` login timestamp stay as recorded. The help text is the i18n key `nat.login.successful.help` (en, bn).
## 2026-10-23 db lane, session 10 (wave 4 db decisions, copied from docs/status/db.md sessions 7 to 10)
- **D-DB-W4-01 (db, 2026-10-22).** The route classification column is `app.route.route_class` (normal, manual, dummy_br, dummy_institutional; V0094/V0095, also on `dw.dim_geo`), not `route.kind`: that name was already taken.
- **D-DB-W4-02 (db, 2026-10-22).** The calendar (`dw.v_calendar`, `v_calendar_month`, `v_working_day`; V0097) is views over `dim_date` + `calendar_holiday`, national holidays only; no second calendar table.
- **D-DB-W4-03 (db, 2026-10-22).** Seed 06 (Gulshan/Banani dev geography) is gated to the dev profile by the absence of seed 04's config version, because about 250 backend assertions key on the Mirpur test fixture. Confirmed on dev by train 11's smoke (route GUL-SR-2).
- **D-DB-W4-04 (db, 2026-10-22).** Lighter catalogue pack size is 25 (owner's "box of 25", V0104); lighter prices stay per piece.
- **D-DB-W4-05 (db, 2026-10-22).** Route targets live in a new `app.route_monthly_target` (+ `route_target_batch`, V0096); the docs/27 `app.target` hook stays empty (targets programme deferred, standing rule 2).
- **D-DB-HOUSE-01 (db, 2026-10-22; lead ruling).** V0093 keeps no unique active-house index: duplicate active houses (same territory and name) are allowed; `house.is_active`, the one-off dedupe and the dim_geo house columns stay. Readers de-duplicate.
- **D-DB-MDM-01 (db, 2026-10-22).** `app.device_command` is guarded in the database (trigger, V0102): states move forward only, request columns are fixed, approval, delivery and result are write-once, a factory reset needs a requester, and a pending reset may expire without breaking a CHECK. Who may approve (the approver role) is enforced by the API only.
- **D-DB-V0108-01 (db, 2026-10-22).** `dw.agg_daily_route.checked_in_at` was backfilled for the last seven days in SQL inside V0108 (backend-reports' rule: the event's route, else a user assigned to the route that day), not by marking dirty keys, so the backfill cannot be lost before the consumer ships.
- **D-DB-V0110-01 (db, 2026-10-23).** `dw.agg_daily_outlet.sold_qty_base` (mixed units) leaves in two steps: V0109 adds the four per-category columns and drops it from `dw.v_daily_outlet`; V0110 drops the table column only after the Aggregator stops naming it (train 15) and rides last in a later train, so no deployed writer ever names a missing column.

- **D-CONTRACT-1-7-01 (lead, 2026-10-23 09:35 UTC).** Contract 1.7.0 applied as one lead head per D-CONTRACT-BATCH-01: rows 1 to 4 of `docs/requests/contract-v1.7-queue.md` (`ERR_AUTHZ_ROLE_ABOVE_GRANT`; `house` in the user-scope `node_type` enums and in `ReachLevel`; `DeviceStatusReport.network_type`; `FinalSubmitPreview.routes[].planned`), with the slices, `WireDtos.kt`, `ContractCatalogues.kt`, the web types and bn/en messages and docs/24 s3.4, s10.3, s14a R21 in the same head. The three response-enum additions are breaking for an old reader (oasdiff) and are approved by the version bump plus the changed queue file, as the CI gate requires. Wiring goes to the lanes: backend-authz (code swap in `AdminUsers.kt`, `house` in `SCOPE_TYPES` and `SCOPE_TABLES`), backend-core (`planned`), mdm (`network_type` on the server DTO and in `DeviceStatusReporter`), web-admin (`house` in the user-scope editor and `scope-rules.ts`; "not due" greying on the final-submit view: row 4's text says web-dashboard, but the view lives under web/src/components/admin/config, inherited from the dormant web-config lane, so web-admin owns it) and android-tso ("not due" greying). Also regenerated: backend/sync `PayloadShapes.kt` (its drift test caught the batch locally; every generator in the repo runs on a contract batch, checklist in the v1.7 queue file). One mechanical compile fix in another lane's file, told to web-dashboard: `web/mock/dash.ts` child-level map gained `house: "zone"` because `ReachLevel` now carries `house` (web typecheck covers `mock/`). Reason: one bump for four queued rows instead of four spurious versions; server before apps as R18.
- **D-WEB-ADMIN-HOUSE-NOTDUE-01 (web-admin session 7, 2026-10-23).** (1) User scope editor: `house` joins the level list from `GEO_LEVELS` (`lib/admin/geo.ts`, the same order the geo entities use) and `NodeType`; the house list loads like the other levels; labels `scope.house` bn/en. Role rule in `scope-rules.ts`: only TSO may hold a house node (TSO: territory or house); backend-authz's own rule was not yet written in DECISIONS, so this is the minimum the lead named (NationalScopeLeakTest) and is one line to widen. A 400 on save shows the server's `detail` sentence (unknown_node until the server accepts house). (2) Final Submit view: a route with `planned === false` and `had_data === false` is "not due" (`fs.state.not_due`): greyed row (`DataTable` gained an optional `rowProps`), status text "not due", pending cell "—". The view itself shows no failed/pending totals (the warnings are the server's and already count due routes only, D-BC-PREVIEW-01), so nothing needed excluding. `planned` null/true/absent and `planned false` with data keep the ordinary row.
- **D-BC-MEMONO-02 (backend-core, 2026-10-23; closes session 14 open item (b)).** The memo number rule's block size is the largest `cfg.memo.seq_block_size` ever stored at global scope (`app.cfg_value` keeps every row), never below the current value, clamped 100..999. A phone keeps the block size it bound with (docs/17: a change applies to new binds only), so judging by the current value alone would refuse, for good, numbers already printed after the key is lowered. The four ordinal blocks always span `1 .. 4 x size`, so the largest size covers every smaller one; the cost is a wider accepted range for the user's own numbers only (login and date are still checked). Test: `MemoNumberPackSizeTest.aLoweredBlockSizeStillAcceptsNumbersPrintedUnderTheOldOne`.
- **D-AUTHZ-1-7-WIRING-01 (backend-authz session 8, 2026-10-23).** Server wiring of contract 1.7.0 rows 1 and 2. (1) Every refusal of D-AUTHZ-ADMIN-CREDS-01 now answers 403 `ERR_AUTHZ_ROLE_ABOVE_GRANT` with its stable detail unchanged: create and PATCH ("only a SUPERADMIN manages ADMIN and SUPERADMIN users", "... manages ANALYST and SUPPORT users"), credential actions ("only a SUPERADMIN acts on ADMIN and SUPERADMIN users", "... acts on ANALYST and SUPPORT users", as the contract's credentials description says), PUT scope, and GET access of another ADMIN (`UserAccessApi.kt`; this one goes beyond the getUserAccess text, which names no code: lead, add it to that description in the next batch or say otherwise). PUT /v1/admin/users/{id}/scope also refuses an ADMIN re-scoping an ANALYST, SUPPORT (or retired TOP) login, which the 1.7.0 description of that operation states and the code had not enforced (a tightening; SUPERADMIN unchanged). Other 403s (TSO limits, own role, "only a SUPERADMIN gives national scope", D-ROLES-02) stay `ERR_FORBIDDEN`; the audit rows and the web text are unchanged. (2) `house` is in `SCOPE_TYPES` and `SCOPE_TABLES` (`app.house`, keyed by `id`); only an **active** house is accepted (an inactive or merged house is `400 unknown_node`, as the scope tree never offers it, D-SCOPE-TREE-01); a non-national caller would need the house to overlap its reach, as for every node type (moot today: only ADMIN and SUPERADMIN write scope, both national). The resolver already reads it (zones whose `zone.house_id` is the node), and the Me/LoginResponse reach summary emits `type: house`. **Role-node rule for the portal (hand-off: web-admin adds it to `web/src/lib/admin/scope-rules.ts`, which has no `house` yet):** `house` may be given to **TSO and AMO** only (docs/24 s8.4 defines both reaches as "the zones in user_scope"; a house is a named group of zones inside one territory, so a TSO may hold a territory or a house, as NationalScopeLeakTest's house TSO does, and an AMO a zone or a house); DMO keeps a division, WM a wing, the national levels national. The server, as for every other node type, does not enforce the role-node pairing (it enforces existence, reach and the senior/service-role gates), so a DMO with a house node resolves correctly (tested) but the portal does not offer it. A house set inactive keeps reaching its zones for users who already hold it (only writes check status); a house merge (`HouseDedup.kt`) moves zones but not `user_scope` house nodes, so such a user drops to zero reach (fails closed): follow-up for backend-admin, move the nodes to the survivor and bump `scope_version` (Opus checker note). Open for backend-reports (lead's 1.7.0 hand-off note): `DashboardApi` maps no house top node yet, so a house-only TSO gets 403 on the dashboard summary until it does.
- **D-WEB-ADMIN-HOUSE-AMO-01 (web-admin session 7, 2026-10-23).** Per backend-authz D-AUTHZ-1-7-WIRING-01 the `house` scope node may be given to TSO and AMO only; `scope-rules.ts` AMO is now zone or house (TSO unchanged). Supersedes the TSO-only line of D-WEB-ADMIN-HOUSE-NOTDUE-01.
- **D-TSO-PARITY-SHARED-01 (android-tso, 2026-10-23; lead ruling).** The shared Settings and Logout strings in `feature-home` take the SR manual wording for all three roles (SR, AMO, TSO): `set_title` "সেটিং", `set_logout` "লগ আউট", logout dialog title "আপনি কি নিশ্চিত যে লগআউট করতে চান?", buttons "হ্যাঁ" (`set_logout_confirm`) and "না" (`set_cancel`), colours kept (green Yes, red No). The unsent-data body `set_logout_message` stays (the G-01 guard is deliberate). Source: docs/evidence/manuals/manual-sr.md s77-79, manual-amo.md p77-79 (same wording in the AMO manual). The lead's ruling text quoted the old values; the manual wording was applied since the ruling says "screenshot wording". Open question: split per role only if an AMO or TSO screenshot later differs. No string id of these is referenced in the print modules.
- **D-TSO-PARITY-O3-01 (android-tso, 2026-10-23; lead ruling).** The outlet-request capture button has its own string id `out_req_capture_geo_photo` ("GEO এবং ছবি ধারণ করুন" / "Capture GEO and photo"), passed to the shared `GeoPhotoBlock` as `takeLabel`; the force-sale photo button keeps `out_take_photo` "দোকানের ছবি তুলুন". The retake label is unchanged. The outlet-request shutter already reads one location fix at that tap (`GeoPhotoCapture.shutter`: photo, then `fixes.readFix(purpose)`; on demand, no continuous GPS), so no follow-up slice is needed.
- **D-TSO-PARITY-SPELLING-01 (android-tso, 2026-10-23; lead ruling).** The old app's misspelling "কারন" is not reproduced: the edit-reason title reads "দয়া করে বিক্রয় এডিট করার কারণ লিখুন" (standard spelling, as our other bn strings use). Wording, layout and order otherwise stay verbatim to the screenshot; the owner can object in the evening report.
- **D-PRINT-03 (android-print session 5, 2026-10-24; owner P0 "the app crashes on Print while the printer is connected").** The print path never lets an exception reach the process: `MemoPrinting`'s print calls and every `SaveAndPrint` step catch any Throwable but cancellation (Errors included: out of memory, a class missing from the R8 build) and turn it into `PrintFailure.INTERNAL` (new value), with the sale saved and reprintable. `PrinterManager`'s background coroutines (reader, connect, close, write watchdog) catch whatever an OEM Bluetooth stack throws, and a `CoroutineExceptionHandler` on its scope is the last line. `BluetoothSppTransport` maps RuntimeExceptions from stream calls to IOException. Catching Errors is deliberate here and only at these edges: a print is never worth ending a selling day. Reason: no stack trace yet; these were the ways the code could end the process on a real phone that the SimPrinter tests could not show (commit 669d1a59).
- **D-PRINT-04 (android-print session 5, 2026-10-24).** Rendering (font load, shaping, raster) runs on `Dispatchers.Default` inside `MemoPrinting` (`renderContext`), never on the caller's thread. Before, the sale and memo screens called from Main, so a cold render (about 320 ms on a fast JVM, seconds on a 2 GB phone) froze the screen and could raise Android's "app not responding" dialog. Unit tests that drive virtual time pass `EmptyCoroutineContext`; production keeps the default.
- **D-PRINT-05 (android-print session 5, 2026-10-24).** `core-printing/diag/CrashLog` keeps the last crash (thread, build versionName+versionCode, UTC time, stack) and the last caught print error in `noBackupFilesDir/print-diag`, plus Android's exit reasons (API 30+, with the main-thread stack for an ANR), appended to the Settings → Printer diagnostics export. Text is scrubbed (phone numbers, long digit runs, Bangla text). It complements, not replaces, F-SYS-032's `ErrorReporter`, which uploads and then deletes its files. It is installed by one line in the SR and AMO `Application` after `errorReporter.start` (TSO does not print). The build is identified by version, not git sha: no sha BuildConfig field exists, and the CI run number maps to the sha.
- **D-PRINT-06 (android-print session 5, 2026-10-24).** Print failure dialogs name the cause, Bangla first (`ui_print_why_*`: no printer, unreachable, link dropped, paper out, app error). The stock slip, reprint and summary dialog (`PrintAttemptDialogs`) uses a document-neutral first line (`ui_print_failed_doc`) instead of "the sale is saved".
- **D-TSO-PRINT-GUARD-01 (android-tso session 4, 2026-10-24; lead ruling 06:37 from android-print's P0 review).** The stock-slip and day-summary print steps of the SR and AMO day flows run through `guardedPrint` (`feature-sale/domain/PrintGuard.kt`): any exception other than cancellation becomes `PrintAttempt.Failed(PrintFailure.INTERNAL)` (the "app error" dialog of D-PRINT-06) instead of reaching the process (summary) or vanishing without a dialog (stock slip). One helper serves both apps so one test (`PrintGuardTest`) covers both; cancellation is rethrown. The earlier `runCatching` on the stock slip is replaced, not kept.
- **D-SALE-ZERO-PRICE-01 (android-tso, 2026-10-24; lead ruling from the owner's Sale screenshot).** A SKU with no positive unit price for the outlet's price type is never offered on the Sale screen (SR and AMO share the list) and so can never become a sale line: `SaleSku.isOfferable` (`unitPriceMtk > 0`) is applied in `RoomSaleReads.catalog` of both apps (the Sale list source, not a composable); the Stock screen reads the stock tracker and still shows the SKU. db #10 confirmed three real zero-price SKUs (MaxDB-10S_10HL, SupSty-10S, SupSty-20S). Side effect: QC entry draws on the same catalog, so a QC line for a zero-price SKU is not offered either (its value would be zero anyway). The sales-plan filtering is unchanged (owner's question in the morning report); the server-side rejection of a zero-price line is backend-core's.
- **D-BC-SMOKE-MEMONO-01 (backend-core session 16, 2026-10-24; lead P1).** The dev deploy's SR slice smoke numbered its memo `seq` 9000..9999 (`infra/scripts/slice-smoke.py`), which the s7.5 rule (`MemoNumbers.inRanges`: `1 .. 4 x block`, or 5001..5999, 6001..6999, 7001..7999, 8001..8999) has refused since 5f4fe84; the first selling-day run (2026-10-24, GUL-SR-2) was `memo_no_invalid`. The rule stays as it is: a phone's `seqOf` stays inside it, it never consults the user's `bind_ordinal`, and the block size falls back to the registry default 500 with no global row. The smoke now takes `MEMO_SEQ_RANGE = (8001, 8999)` (ordinal 3's overflow): valid at every block size 100..999 and reached by a phone only after 500+ memos in a day on ordinal 3. Test: `MemoNumberPackSizeTest.theSliceSmokesMemoRangeIsValid` reads the script's range (fails on 9000..9999); `check_infra` SliceSmoke pins `8\d{3}`.
- **D-BC-SMOKE-RED-01 (backend-core session 16, 2026-10-24; lead P1; infra-owned file, through the lead).** `infra/deploy.sh` exits 1 with `::error::` when the slice smoke result is FAILED, after the summary table is written (everything is already deployed; the EXIT trap releases the deploy lock). SKIPPED (not a selling day) and "not run" stay warnings. The deploy job then reads red instead of "success" over a FAILED table.
- **D-BC-ZERO-PRICE-01 (backend-core session 16, 2026-10-24; implements lead ruling D-SALE-ZERO-PRICE-01).** A `memo_line` of `line_kind` `sale` whose `base_price_mtk` is zero or missing is a final per-record `schema_invalid` (detail `zero_price_line: ...`; the existing code, a dedicated `price_invalid` is queued in `docs/requests/contract-v1.8-queue.md`), judged at ingest step 8a, after the money check, so the line is arithmetically consistent and its gross is zero. The line alone is refused: the memo and its priced lines are stored (the gross is unchanged) and the memo gets `server_flags` `zero_price_line` (its stored lines are one short of `line_count`). A resend stays refused (registry `rejected`). Not refused: a line the phone priced above zero for an SKU whose server price is zero or missing; that money was collected and is in the memo's gross, so refusing the line would leave the gross without it. It keeps the existing `price_mismatch` flag (`MemoChecks.afterChildStored`). Free-issue kinds (`promo_free`, `free_sample`, `drp_reward`) are not judged; they belong to the deferred programmes. Bundle unchanged: it ships every SKU (`BundleService`, no price filter), so an SKU can reach the phone with no price row for its price type; the app filters (android-tso #3). A memo whose only line was zero-priced is stored with gross 0 and still counts as `line_count > 0` in the dashboard call counts until review. Tests: `ZeroPriceLineTest` (seeded property case `ARON_ZERO_PRICE_SEED`).
- **D-BC-SMOKE-MEMONO-02 (backend-core session 16, 2026-10-24; lead follow-up, refines D-BC-SMOKE-MEMONO-01).** The slice smoke numbers its memo from the overflow range of the slot the server bound its device to (login `device.bind_ordinal` o: `5000 + o x 1000 + 1 .. + 999`; seed 05 binds the dev device to sr1001 at ordinal 0, so 5001..5999), which no other phone of the user numbers from (ordinals are unique among a user's active bindings) and the smoke device is no real phone. Without an ordinal in the login it falls back to `MEMO_SEQ_RANGE` (8001..8999). `MemoNumberPackSizeTest.inRangesAcceptsExactlyTheFourBlocksAndTheFourOverflowRanges` pins the oracle (-1..10000 at block sizes 100, 500, 999); `theSliceSmokesMemoRangeIsValid` checks the formula and the fallback; the check_infra stub login now returns the device (ordinal 0) and pins `5\d{3}`.
- **D-SALE-ZERO-PRICE-01 server block (lead confirmation, 2026-10-24 07:35 UTC; backend-core session 16).** Both sides of D-BC-ZERO-PRICE-01 are confirmed: (1) a sale line whose stated `base_price_mtk` is ≤ 0 is refused final `schema_invalid`, the memo stored and flagged `zero_price_line`, the priced lines kept; (2) a line the phone priced above zero for an SKU the server has at zero or no price (a stale bundle) stays stored with the existing `price_mismatch` flag, never quarantined: the retailer paid that money and it is in the memo's gross, and the flag makes it visible for review. docs/24 lists no memo server flags, so no docs/24 row was added.
- **D-BC-ZERO-PRICE-02 (backend-core session 16, 2026-10-24; Opus checker on D-BC-ZERO-PRICE-01: 1 blocking + notes, all fixed or recorded).** (1) Blocking, fixed: the `zero_price_line` flag was set only when the memo was already stored, so a zero line sent before its memo, or refused while its memo was held (device gate, signature enforce, a review, a handler), left the memo stored one line short and unflagged. A memo now checks, when stored, for a zero-price refusal of its own line by its uploader on its business date (`MemoChecks.refusedZeroLine`, `sync_rejected` detail `zero_price_line:`) and is flagged then; test `ZeroPriceLineTest.aZeroLineSentBeforeItsMemoStillFlagsTheMemo` (fails without it). (2) The flag is set only on a memo of the line's own uploader (`flagZeroPrice` checks `user_id`): a line naming another user's memo uuid cannot mark it. (3) Recorded, not changed: once a zero line is refused, the memo's stored lines never reach `line_count`, so the split-family re-check in `afterChildStored` (and a review's `familyMismatches`) no longer runs for that memo; its money was verified once at ingest (step 8 forces the zero line's gross to 0), and the flag sends it to review. The goods of the refused line are missing from server `sold_qty` (a stock gap at day close, visible). Device count == server count holds (rejected rows count on both sides, `Reconciliation`). (4) Correction to D-BC-ZERO-PRICE-01: `base_price_mtk` is required and non-negative in the contract, so only an exact 0 reaches step 8a; a missing one fails earlier. A paper entry with a zero line is rolled back whole (`ingestManual`), and `PaperBackfill` prices from `sku_price`. (5) Test: `committed_at` per family is distinct, so no seed of `ARON_ZERO_PRICE_SEED` can make two families a `content_duplicate`.
- **D-BC-SMOKE-MEMONO-03 (backend-core session 16, 2026-10-24; checker notes on D-BC-SMOKE-MEMONO-01/02).** (1) Correction: a phone on ordinal 3 reaches 8001 after `block + 1` memos in a day, at least 101 (block size 100), not "500+"; it also needs the same login. Moot for the bound-ordinal range. (2) The smoke's seq is no longer random: `memo_seq` maps the second of the Dhaka day onto the range (`lo + secs x size // 86400`), so two smoke runs at least 87 s apart on one day never draw the same number (deploys are serialised and take minutes). Two equal draws would have been `memo_no_duplicate` (the unique index covers voided memos), and that turns the deploy red since D-BC-SMOKE-RED-01. (3) The check_infra stub pins the number numerically (5001..5999), no longer by a regex that admits 5000.
- **D-BC-ZERO-PRICE-03 (backend-core session 16, 2026-10-24; Opus re-check of D-BC-ZERO-PRICE-02: PASS, notes).** (1) `refusedZeroLine` no longer requires the line's business date to equal the memo's (a line keyed under another date still flags its memo; the `user_id` prefix of the index serves it). (2) Step 8a takes the memo's step-2 advisory lock (`hashtextextended(memo_client_uuid, 0)`) before flagging, so a memo stored in a concurrent batch either is seen by the line or sees the line's refusal; the only new lock wait is the same zero line in two batches at once (a deadlock Postgres breaks; the batch is retried as transient). (3) Correction to D-BC-ZERO-PRICE-02 item (3): when the zero line is refused before its memo's family is complete on the server (line first, or memo held), `familyMismatches` skips the family (counts differ), so that memo's gross is never re-checked against its stored lines; only the `zero_price_line` flag shows it. Left as is (a review flag, the phone computed the gross with the shared `MemoMath`). (4) A `matchBackfill` of such a phone memo is refused `ERR_CONFLICT` (one line short of the paper twin); nothing is lost. (5) Untested, recorded: the held-memo release path and the `user_id` guard of `flagZeroPrice`. (6) The slice smoke reads the business date and the memo slot from one clock.
- **D-TSO-PARITY-X3-01 (android-tso session 4, 2026-10-24; audit row X3, screenshot/manual wins by rule 14a).** The SR survey step shows the manual's info card "আপনার (*) মার্ক করা প্রশ্নের উত্তর দিতে হবে" (`sr_survey_required_info`, Info banner, only when a visible question is required), marks each required question "(*)", and the submit button reads "সাবমিট →" in Bangla (manual wording as written; English stays "Submit"). A1 and A4 were found already implemented (user card via `userTitle`; prompt centred above the buttons), and X2 already matches: no change.
- **D-TSO-PARITY-O5-01 (android-tso session 4, 2026-10-24; audit row O5, manual wording).** The outlet-request confirm dialog reads "আপনি কি নিশ্চিত?" with a kind-specific body: permanent close "এই দোকানটি স্থায়ীভাবে বন্ধ হতে যাচ্ছে" (`out_req_confirm_close`), every other kind "এই পরিবর্তন সংরক্ষণ করা হবে" (manual text, no question mark). English: "Confirm" / "Save this change?" / "This shop will be permanently closed. Continue?". The close banner stays. Yes/No keep their green/red.
- **D-FLAGS-SURFACE-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 08:55 UTC; from backend-core session 16's finding under D-BC-ZERO-PRICE-02).** `app.memo.server_flags` (zero_price_line, price_mismatch, arithmetic_mismatch, pack_factor_mismatch and the other flags MemoChecks/IngestService write) had no reader: no API, report, `dw` view or web page. Rule 5 (integrity findings are surfaced) makes this a P2 gap to close before the cutover, in this order: (1) db adds the stable view `dw.v_memo_flags` (one row per flagged memo with its scope keys, gross, flags, stored_at, voided_at); (2) the lead applies contract 1.8 as one batch (queue rows 1, 2 and the new row 3: operation `GET /v1/dashboards/flagged-memos` under the caller's reach, flags as documented strings, not an enum, so later flags stay additive; `server_flags` as an optional member on the memo read item); (3) backend-core implements row 3 in backend/analytics over the view, with scope and paging tests, and the `price_invalid` wiring from row 2; (4) web-admin builds the "Flagged memos" dashboard page. - **D-DB-MEMO-FLAGS-VIEW-01 (db, 2026-10-24; lead request from backend-core #16).** V0111 adds the stable view `dw.v_memo_flags`: one row per memo whose `app.memo.server_flags` is non-empty (zero_price_line, price_mismatch, arithmetic_mismatch, pack_factor_mismatch and the others MemoChecks/IngestService write), with memo id, client UUID, family UUID (groups a memo with its edits), memo_no, business date, status, user_id, outlet id/code/name, route, zone, territory, division and wing ids, gross_mtk, flags, stored_at (= received_at) and voided_at. Voided and superseded memos stay in with their status; counts use `status = 'active' AND voided_at IS NULL`. Scope keys are frozen at the business date: the memo's own route and stamped zone (V0042); a memo without a route takes the outlet's placement on its business date (`outlet_placement_history`) and that route's zone on the date, and only then the outlet's current route and zone; territory, division and wing through the current hierarchy. **No user names or logins** in the view (deviation from the request's `user_code`/`user_name`): `dw` carries no names (`dim_user`), and `web_ro`/`bi_reader` read every `dw` relation, so the endpoint resolves names from `app.app_user` under its own role. **No index** at pilot size; a partial index on `server_flags <> '{}'` is the first fix if the view gets slow at fleet size. No `app` table or contract change.

The bn/en text of `price_invalid` is in backend/sync `ReasonTexts.kt` (the bundle's `reason_texts`, one entry per code, enforced by `BundleUnitTest`); no web bn/en message was added because the web has no `RecordOutcomeCode` text catalogue (the quarantine page shows the code as it is). The response-enum addition is breaking for an old reader (oasdiff) and is approved by the version bump plus the changed queue file. Server behaviour unchanged: ingest still answers `schema_invalid` with detail `zero_price_line: ...`. Wiring goes to the lanes: backend-core (price_invalid code swap; the flagged-memos endpoint over dw.v_memo_flags; server_flags on the memo read), web-admin (the Flagged memos dashboard page), db (dw.v_memo_flags landed in V0111; `price_invalid` is not in the `app.sync_rejected.code` CHECK list of V0008, so db adds it before backend-core's swap).

- **D-REPO-PRIVATE-01 (owner, 2026-10-24 about 13:00 UTC; supersedes the "public until complete" part of CLAUDE.md rule 13).** The owner made the repository private today, before the product is complete, so the 42 real pack pictures (D-PACK-04) may now sit in git and be bundled in the dev build, and anything else held back only because the repository was public is unblocked. Consequences the lead handles: (1) GitHub Actions minutes and artifact storage now count against the owner's plan (a public repository had them free); the owner sets the Actions spending limit (rule 13) and the lead cuts the minutes (superseded lane runs cancelled, artifact retention trimmed, dependency-review step guarded) on a lead hotfix branch with an Opus check; (2) GitHub Advanced Security features (secret-scanning push protection, code scanning, dependency review on pull requests, custom Dependabot alert rules) are off on a private repository without GHAS; the repository's own gates already cover them (gitleaks over the whole history, OSV-Scanner, Semgrep, Trivy, SBOM, pinned tools), and Dependabot alerts, security updates and version-update pull requests stay free once the dependency graph is enabled in the repository settings; (3) existing forks, if any, stay public and detached: the owner checks the fork list; (4) the lanes, the Claude GitHub App and CI are unaffected. The pictures still have to come from the owner's laptop (the only copy): either the loader run by the laptop operator session (server path, the final design) or the processed WebP files committed to a lead branch for bundling in the dev APK.
- **D-REPO-PUBLIC-02 (owner, 2026-10-24 about 13:35 UTC; reverses D-REPO-PRIVATE-01 and restores CLAUDE.md rule 13).** The owner made the repository public again the same afternoon: GitHub Actions minutes and artifact storage are free again and the free security scanning (secret-scanning push protection, CodeQL, dependency review) returns; the repository stays public until the product is complete and goes private at go-live, with the Actions spending limit set first (rule 13). The owner accepts that anyone can read the code, the specs, the pilot seed and the pack pictures if they are committed. Consequences: the lead CI-cost branch (lane/lead-ci-private: superseded lane runs cancelled, artifact retention trimmed, dependency-review and CodeQL guarded on a private repository) still lands, because the first two save queue time and storage anyway and the guards do nothing while the repository is public. The pack pictures keep the two paths of owner row L: the loader (server path, the final design, pictures never in git) is preferred; committing the processed WebP files for the dev APK is accepted on the owner's word about visibility. Nothing changes for the lanes, the integrator or the deploy.

Only that code: the V0008 split is deliberate, `sync_rejected` takes the codes the contract marks rejected (plus the server-only `retry_exhausted`) and `app.sync_quarantine` the 15 it marks quarantined, so the CHECK keeps a quarantined money record from landing in `sync_rejected` instead of the admin queue (Opus checker caught a first draft that widened it to the whole enum). A later contract code needs a migration like this one, in the table its status names; backend-core wires a new code only after its db change is on INT.
- **D-PRINT-07 (android-print, 2026-10-24; root cause of D-PRINT-02/03, owner screenshot "The app could not prepare the paper").** Every print on the phone failed because `PrintTemplate.TOKEN` (`\{(@?)([^{}]{1,60})}`) ended in a bare `}`: Android's `java.util.regex` is ICU, which rejects it (U_REGEX_RULE_SYNTAX), while the desktop JVM, and so every JVM and Robolectric test, accepts it. Every template-based document (memo, stock slip, day summary, void slip, due receipt) failed before a byte reached the printer; only the diagnostics test pages (plain bitmaps) could print. Proven by compiling the pattern with ICU 74's C library (`uregex_open`); every other regex literal, the shaper's runtime patterns and the interpolated ones pass ICU. Fix 72ff0a37: braces escaped (same matches on both engines). Rule from now on: a brace in a regex is escaped unless it is a `{n,m}` quantifier; `AndroidRegexBracesTest` (core-printing) applies ICU's brace rule to every regex literal in `android/` and `shared/` and fails the build otherwise. Device proof pending: the owner prints a stock slip and a sale memo on the MP-58N with the next signed build.

A resend of a line refused under the old code keeps its old answer (registry `rejected`, step 2). Tests: `ZeroPriceLineTest` expects `price_invalid`.

Ruling: fail-closed stays (never a leak), but every TSO the owner's levels allow must see their dashboards: `resolveScopedNode` resolves a `house` node like the other geo levels, and a route-only TSO's default node set is the zones of their assigned routes; same reach rules as `GET /v1/memos`; scope-leak tests cover house and route-only cases. Built by backend-core session 17 as its first row (request `docs/requests/backend-core-17-house-scope-dashboards.md`); not a cutover blocker, before the device lab. (2) Node zones come from `dw.dim_geo` (route-keyed), so a zone with no routes is absent from node-scoped dashboards: an under-report of zero sales, never a leak; accepted until the db lane keys `dim_geo` zones independently of routes (db #11 note, low). - **D-BC-DASH-HOUSE-01 (backend-core session 17, 2026-10-24; D-DASH-TSO-HOUSE-SCOPE-01 point 1).** A node-scoped dashboard read without `level` and `node_id` resolves a caller's `house` top node to the reach's zones (`app.zone.house_id`, the same source as `GET /v1/memos`), in `resolveScopedNode` (sync-health, login-submit, daily-tracking, flagged-memos) and in `DashboardService` (summary, geo-validation; children are the house's zones). Not `dw.dim_geo.house_id`: the projector folds same-name houses of a territory into the lowest id (V0093 duplicates), so the dimension's zones of either house differ from the reach's and the `containsAll` guard would 403 both TSOs (Opus checker note 2). With other top nodes as well (house + territory) both resolvers read the whole reach, labelled by the first node. An explicit `level=house` stays 400 `ERR_VALIDATION`, as the contract's GeoLevelQuery states (no contract change); a house TSO drills by its zones. Tests: `ScopeLeakAcrossEndpointsTest.aHouseScopedTsoReadsItsOwnHouseByDefaultAndNeverTheSiblingHouse` (own house = the national view of its zone; the sibling house H-SIB has H-MIR's name in other case, so the dimension folds them; H-SIB's zone, the territory and the other division 403, the probe finds no leak).
- **D-BC-DASH-ROUTE-ONLY-01 (backend-core session 17, 2026-10-24; refines D-DASH-TSO-HOUSE-SCOPE-01/02; lead rulings 17:33 and 17:50 UTC, D-DASH-TSO-HOUSE-SCOPE-03).** Route-only default stays 403 until route-keyed aggregates. The default call answers 403 with each endpoint's existing code; explicit `node_id` keeps the reach check (a route-only TSO reaches no zone, so 403). Change against before: on sync-health, daily-tracking and flagged-memos a route-only TSO with two or more routes used to get 200 with an empty node set (the multi-node branch with no zones); it now gets 403 like the one-route case (summary, geo-validation and login-submit were already 403). Test: `ScopeLeakAcrossEndpointsTest.aRouteOnlyTsoGetsNoDefaultDashboardUntilRouteKeyedAggregates` (all routes of Z-SIB plus one of Z-MIR, and one route of Z-MIR alone: 403 on every dashboard default and node call; probe clean). Queued (db + analytics): route-keyed dashboard aggregates, then route-only dashboards.

The page: an unknown `status` is shown as given (the three known ones keep their labels), and the error banner has no Retry on 400 as well as 403. - **D-DASH-TSO-HOUSE-SCOPE-02 (lead, 2026-10-24 17:33 UTC; refines D-DASH-TSO-HOUSE-SCOPE-01 on backend-core session 17's question).** Node-scoped dashboards aggregate by zone (`dw.agg_daily_zone` and kin carry no route), while a route-only TSO's reach covers only their routes (`GET /v1/memos` shows those routes' memos only). Showing whole zones would be wider than that reach, so constraint 4 (server-side scope, never a leak) decides: the route-only TSO's default node set is the zones whose EVERY `dw.dim_geo` route is assigned to that TSO on the business date (frozen like the memo reach); a partly held zone stays out; when no zone qualifies the default call keeps today's 403 ERR_OUT_OF_SCOPE (no contract change; the web shows its plain error state, no Retry); explicit `node_id` queries keep the existing reach check. The proper fix for partly held zones is route-keyed dashboard aggregates (a db + analytics item, queued for later; not before the device lab unless the owner's data shows route-only TSOs are common). Tests in `ScopeLeakAcrossEndpointsTest`: all routes of zone A and one of two routes of zone B → zone A only; part of one zone only → 403; house-scoped TSO → own house, not the sibling.
- **D-DASH-TSO-HOUSE-SCOPE-03 (lead, 2026-10-24 17:50 UTC; supersedes the route-only part of D-DASH-TSO-HOUSE-SCOPE-02 on backend-core session 17's Opus checker finding).** Dashboard rows carry the zone stamped when they were written (`agg_daily_route`/`agg_daily_zone` at rebuild, `fact_visit`, `risk_signal`, `v_memo_flags` = the route's zone on the date, route-less memos = the outlet's current zone), so even a zone whose every CURRENT `dw.dim_geo` route belongs to the TSO leaks over a past range (a route that moved out, a route not yet in `dim_geo`, route-less memos), and the only exact check is a nine-table all-time scan that the next table silently breaks. Ruling: the route-only TSO's default dashboard call stays 403 ERR_OUT_OF_SCOPE (fail-closed, constraint 4) until route-keyed dashboard aggregates exist (db + analytics item, queued; the web keeps its plain error state). This slice ships the house part only: `house` resolves like the other geo levels with its zones taken from Reach, not from `dim_geo` (which folds duplicate houses into the lowest id); tests pin the house cases, the duplicate-house fold, and the route-only 403.
