- **D-BC-TRACE-DAY-01 (backend-core session 17, 2026-10-24; D-TEST-DATE-PIN-01, lead ruling 19:23 UTC).** DataFlowTrace day follows the seed clock (D-TEST-DATE-PIN-01). The dev seed (db/seed/06) starts sr1001's Gulshan assignments and scopes at its own `app.dhaka_date(now())`; the data-flow traces pinned 2026-10-24..27, so 22 of them failed from Dhaka midnight 2026-10-25 on every sha. Each trace now names its route (`DataFlowTraceSupport(dayRoute)`: GUL-SR-2 for the main, concurrency, parking, money and holiday traces, GUL-SR-1 and BAN-SR-11 for the "Sunday" and "Monday" traces, BAN-SR-11 for the cover day) and `setUp` sets `day` to the first date on or after the seed's Dhaka today on which that route is planned for sr1001, by `DayPlan.isPlanned` over the seeded mask, assignment, calendar entries and weekend config (no weekday or date in the code); the world clock keeps 10:30Z of that day. Day-dependent figures follow: memo numbers (`yyMMdd`), the holiday trace's month and its second (global) holiday, the target months (three months from the seed's today, a month the seed lacks copying its first month's targets, so the doubled-month and no-lighter branches always run; dates on or after today), and the LKX CPR target counts every route the LKX bundle plans that day (the Daily route alone on a Saturday, plus the 3F route on its days), which was the Saturday-only figure before. Checked on start days today, +1 and +4: every weekday a seeded route visits (Saturday to Thursday), one of them across a month boundary. Not changed: db/seed/06 (its start-at-today rule is deliberate). Open (Opus checker notes, not built): `traceDay` reads `app.route.visit_days_mask` and the global/wing/division weekend, while the bundle prefers the effective `app.route_planned` row and resolves the weekend through every config scope by the clock; a differing `route_planned` row or a role/zone/user weekend override would make the trace pick a day the bundle does not plan (a loud failure, never a silent pass); the trace's working-day SQL hard-codes Friday like before. The seed's own targets stay fixed months (2026-10 to 2026-12; db).
- **D-TEST-DATE-PIN-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 19:23 UTC; backend-core session 17's finding).** Twenty-two `DataFlowTrace*` tests (`backend/app`) started failing identically on INT 39ed8bc5 and on every lane at 18:00 UTC, when the Dhaka business date rolled to 2026-10-25: `db/seed/06_dev_gulshan_banani.sql` starts its route assignments and zone scopes at `app.dhaka_date(now())`, while `DataFlowTraceSupport` pinned the trace day to the constant `2026-10-24`, so sr1001 had no planned Gulshan route on the pinned day (`prepareSrDay` threw). INT CI stayed green only because trains 32 and 33 ran no backend job after 18:00. Ruling: a test that runs over the date-relative dev seed derives its day from the seed's clock (the first day on or after `app.dhaka_date(now())` with a planned route for the SR, from the seeded calendar), never from a constant; the time-of-day pin stays; the seed is not changed (its start-at-today behaviour is deliberate and the slice smoke follows it, PASSED tonight on the rolled date). Fixed by backend-core session 17 in the same push as its house-scope slice (a fix that makes its own run green is not widening), after a grep for other constant 2026-10 dates in tests that depend on seeded or today data. Follow-up for the infra/qa lane: a repository gate that rejects a constant business date in a test file that touches the dev seed.
- **D-WEB-DASH-MAPS-01 (web-dashboard session 9, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-MAPS-01).** The Maps key reaches the web container app only as the Container Apps secret `maps-web-key` + secret-backed env `MAPS_WEB_KEY`, set by `infra/deploy.sh` after each apps deployment (skipped with "MAPS_WEB_KEY absent" when empty, value never echoed); the `NEXT_PUBLIC_MAPS_WEB_KEY` build-arg is removed from deploy.sh, web.Dockerfile, ci.yml and web-snapshots.yml. Every Maps load goes through the capped BFF `/api/bff/maps/load`; the "no map key" state stays the honest empty state.
- **D-TSO-SYNC-NOW-01 (android-tso session 6, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-SYNC-01).** A save's debounced upload runs under its own unique name `aron-sync-pending-u<id>` (KEEP, network-constrained, 5 s `cfg.sync.debounce_s`, never expedited), and the reconnect, network-validated, app-start and app-in-front nudges ask for that same job without the debounce, so while rows wait exactly one pending job waits only on the network and WorkManager starts it the moment one returns (process alive or not). Before, the save used the main name, where the failure backoff (up to 300 s) waits, and KEEP swallowed the save's request, so a sale made after a flaky spell waited out the backoff while online; `ConnectivityFlush` reacted only to `onAvailable` (its health check fails on a mobile link still attaching, with no second chance) and nothing nudged on app start or foreground. Now `ConnectivityFlush` also flushes once on the step to `NET_CAPABILITY_VALIDATED` per network, nudges at registration (app start) and on the first activity started (`ActivityLifecycleCallbacks`, no new dependency), and with nothing to upload a reconnect runs the signed-in user's bundle delta (`refreshIfServerNewer`, else `refreshOnForeground` on its 30 min gate; SR and AMO wiring). Unchanged: Sales Submit (expedited, main name) and check-out (now name, or the 17:00 gate jitter) at once; server holds delay every automatic trigger including the pending job; the failure backoff and the 15 min battery-not-low periodic safety net; no polling, no receiver, no foreground service. Opus checker fixes in the same push: a run the server answered in full (DRAINED, RUN_LIMIT) replaces a stale backoff on the follow-up name instead of keeping it, and the reconnect pull runs only with an app activity started and after the health check (a background process start or a captive portal never spends the delta gate). Proof is Robolectric (`SyncAtOnceTest`) on the test account's CI, not a device; the owner checks on the next signed build.
- **D-NIGHT-PACKAGE-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 22:25 UTC; owner's bedtime message, blanket permission for the night).** Eight owner items assigned in `docs/requests/owner-2026-10-24-night.md`: sync-at-once and dashboard-at-once (android-tso #6, web-dashboard #9, backend-core #18), no check-out without Sales Submit, the Sales Submit screen and digest, print footer and logo (android-print #5), the web map key (web-dashboard #9), three test outlets in the dev seed (db #11), the working-hours app allow-list under MDM (mdm #3), a data-architecture review and a board deck (lead). The adb-connected Honor cannot be reached: no laptop session is alive (its environment was deleted 2026-10-07); the owner is asked to start one in the morning.
- **D-NIGHT-SYNC-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 22:25 UTC; owner item 1).** Whenever the phone's outbox has unsent rows exactly one network-constrained WorkManager job stays enqueued, so the upload starts the moment connectivity returns (no polling, no receiver loop; a NetworkCallback may nudge it); online, a committed row keeps the 5 s debounce, Sales Submit and check-out go at once, the periodic job stays as the safety net, docs/04 budgets hold; pulls run on the same trigger when their generation is stale. Server: the 5 s outbox projector and dirty-key rebuild (docs/33 C3) are verified on dev (a synced memo reaches `dw` within 15 s). Web: dashboard tiles and tables refetch every 30 s while the tab is visible and on window focus.
- **D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 22:25 UTC; owner item 2).** SR and AMO check-out is disabled until the day's Sales Submit (`day_submit`) is committed locally (offline-capable), Bangla-first text "Sales Submit first" with a one-tap link, no bypass. Server: a check-out without an effective `day_submit` for the business date raises risk signal `CHECKOUT_WITHOUT_SUBMIT` (contract 1.9 queue row 5; `RiskSignalCode` is x-extensible); until that batch lands the server stores the check-out unchanged.
- **D-NIGHT-DAYCLOSE-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 22:25 UTC; owner item 3, two screenshots).** Sales Submit compare rule: the day is in sync when every committed device row is acknowledged (device ⊆ server); a server count above the device count is information (other devices or earlier installs of the login today), a device count above the server count or an unacknowledged device row is the mismatch. Rejected rows are listed with type, memo number or outlet, the `ReasonTexts` reason (bn/en) and the consequence; a final rejection never blocks Sales Submit, a retryable one does until re-synced. The header fits at 360 px ("Phone"/"Server"), states are consistent, Submit sales enabled only under the compare rule. Server (1.9 queue row 4): the digest returns per-type counts for this login+date and for this device, plus the rejected list (client_uuid, type, code, final).
- **D-PRINT-08 (lead, 2026-10-24 22:25 UTC; owner device result).** The owner confirms printing works on signed build 1546 (stock slip and sale memo): the P0 of 05:50 UTC is closed (root cause D-PRINT-07). Follow-ups for android-print: 1 inch of blank paper after the last line of every printed document (8 lines of feed at 203 dpi, 58 mm) so the tear-off never cuts text; the Aron logo as a raster above the "AKTCL" header when the printer accepts `GS v 0`, text-only fallback kept (optional per the owner). The same result is recorded on the lead docs branch and referenced from android-print's notes: the print P0 (D-PRINT-02/03/07) is closed on the device.
- **D-NIGHT-MAPS-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 22:25 UTC; owner item 5, permission given).** The Maps browser key (GitHub secret `MAPS_WEB_KEY`, never printed) serves the dev web portal through `infra/deploy.sh` as a secret-backed container env `MAPS_WEB_KEY` read server-side by `web/src/lib/maps/guard.ts` and the capped BFF loader; the `NEXT_PUBLIC_MAPS_WEB_KEY` build-arg is removed so the key is never inlined (docs/requests/web-dashboard-radius-map-maps-key.md); dashboard and radius maps load through the BFF. Owner-only: restrict the key to the dev host's referrer in the Google console.
- **D-NIGHT-SEED-OUTLETS-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 22:25 UTC; owner item 6b).** `db/seed/06_dev_gulshan_banani.sql` adds three retail outlets on GUL-SR-1 with the owner's names and exact coordinates (Asef Home 23.788347, 90.402357; Asef Office 23.791886, 90.420650; Asef Head Office 23.795805, 90.418034), priced like the other seeded outlets, visible to sr1001 every seeded day. The repository is public, so these coordinates are public; the lead offers a GitHub-secret-fed seed step on request.
- **D-NIGHT-MDM-ALLOWLIST-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 22:25 UTC; owner item 7).** On a device-owner-enrolled phone Android allows a working-hours app allow-list: the policy gains `work_hours_allowlist` {enabled, packages[], schedule from the working-day calendar or an explicit window, grace_min}; the DPC suspends every launcher-visible package outside the list during the window (Aron apps, dialler and system UI always allowed) and lifts it after, applied on receipt, boot and app start on trusted time; the portal MDM section gets the editor (typed package list until the packages-catalogue endpoint of contract 1.9 queue row 6); enrolment of the Honor as device owner is a runbook step needing the laptop session or the owner's hands (`dpm set-device-owner` on an account-free phone, else factory reset + QR); kiosk (lock task) stays the stricter alternative.
- **D-NIGHT-SEED-OUTLETS-02 (lead, 2026-10-24 22:45 UTC; db #11 question).** The three owner test outlets move the GUL-SR-1 outlet count that the backend DataFlowTraceTest pins (`GUL-SR-1:18:21` → `GUL-SR-1:18:24`, line 72). db edits that one literal in the same commit as its seed change (lead-approved cross-lane exception so the head is self-consistent; the Backend app tests job must RUN green on that exact sha); backend-core #18 is told not to touch that line. Rule restated: a seed change that moves a count another lane's test pins is fixed in the same head, never in a later train; if the other lane also edits that file, the db head merges INT after that lane's train and stays LAST.
- **D-DATA-ARCH-REVIEW-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 23:10 UTC; owner item 6; `docs/status/data-architecture-review-2026-10-25.md`).** Three Opus reviewers (schema, pipelines, reuse and lake), three Opus adversarial verifiers and one synthesis, on INT c427d842 (migrations to V0113). Verdict: the capture foundation is right (client-UUID keys with four duplicate layers, integer milli-taka and `qty_base` CHECKs, append-only history with a hash-chained audit log, UTC plus Dhaka business date on every row, generated data dictionary gated in CI, outside reading only through `dw.v_*`); the reuse layer is not yet right: `dw` stamps rebuilt old days with the route's current zone (SCD2 request open since 2026-10-07), there is no rebuild, reconcile or restatement tool, no `data_origin` tag separates seed, test, pilot and production rows, the reading surface stops at route x SKU x day (no memo-line views), 7 of 11 event types have no producer, and there is no lake. No blocker; two time-sensitive items: data origin before pilot and real sales mix, and as-was attribution. Routing in `docs/requests/data-architecture-actions-2026-10-25.md`: db "data origin" first, backend-reports "as-was attribution" then rebuild/reconcile, db "reuse surface", infra "lake and blobs" at pilot size, backend-core nightly Parquet export after those; the two small fixes (a void or edit on a later day dirties both dates; the worker reads the admin suspicious-visit threshold) go to backend-core. Refuted findings in the report's s5 are not re-raised.
- **D-DATA-OUTBOX-ROLE-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 23:10 UTC; review finding 5).** Domain events are thin change notices for the projector and integrations (type, keys, business date), never the reuse feed: the lake and every outside consumer read the `dw.v_*` views and the capture partitions. Catalogue types without a producer are marked `planned`; the four live types enforce their keys; `memo.superseded` is added so an edit is never summed twice. docs/31 s3.5, docs/data-events.md and docs/33 C3.4 are amended to say this in the lead's next docs pass; backend-admin and backend-core implement the catalogue and key changes as queued rows.
- **D-DATA-LAKE-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 23:10 UTC; review s4; supersedes the Fabric-mirroring alternative offered in docs/16 (the lake part of D-560 where it relies on mirroring)).** The data lake is a nightly Parquet export of `dw.v_*`: in the test account one Standard LRS storage account with hierarchical namespace, Hot tier, no zone redundancy, container `lake`, paths `lake/v1/<view>/business_date=YYYY-MM-DD/` with a manifest (row counts, SHA-256, migration and view-contract versions), run by a read-only `lake_export` login, re-exporting the dates the projector rebuilt, with a test matching Parquet totals to `v_daily_route` and one restore drill; in the final account the same files plus archived partitions past the 13-month hot window and a query engine (Fabric shortcut or Synapse serverless) sized with the sponsor; log-based CDC only if a consumer needs minute-level feeds. Fabric mirroring is removed from docs/16 (it cannot work with partitioned tables, views and weekly column adds). Pilot cost is well under US$1 a month; nothing fleet-sized is created in the test account (docs/28).
- **D-TSO-CHECKOUT-SUBMIT-01 (android-tso session 6, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-01).** SR and AMO check-out needs the business date's Sales Submit committed on this phone: `AttendanceFlow(salesSubmitted = …)` reads `RoomDaySource.alreadySubmitted` (SR, `submitStands`: a `day_submit` of the date whose cycle the server has not voided) or `AmoDay.daySubmitted` (AMO supervisor day) on screen open, on resume and again at the press (never a stale flag); until the first read answers, after a failed read and after a voided cycle check-out stays off; offline the local record is enough. The screen shows the warning banner "আগে Sales Submit করুন। আজকের বিক্রি সাবমিট করার পর চেক আউট চালু হবে।" / "Sales Submit first. Check-out opens once today's sales are submitted." with the one-tap link "Sales Submit খুলুন" / "Open Sales Submit" (SR: `SrScreen.SUBMIT`; AMO: `LocalAmoNavigate` to `AmoScreen.SALES_SUBMIT`, a CompositionLocal in `AmoApp` so an app-module screen can open another). No bypass in the app; TSO passes no gate (its day closes with Final Submit). Opus checker fixes in the same push: check-out presses run one at a time (a Mutex: the gate read suspends, so a double hold-complete could otherwise write two check-outs); the state carries `checkOutTimeReached`, so the SR Home keeps its 17:00 Ready-to-submit and Ready-to-check-out states and the 17:00 caption gives way to the banner. Open for the lead: with several routes a day, `submitStands` is true once any route is submitted (`RouteDayLock` uses any), kept as is; and an SR who stays offline to the end of the day cannot check out, because Sales Submit itself waits for every row to be acknowledged (Q-UI-08, D-NIGHT-DAYCLOSE-01 (a)); the offline-queue submit (D-383) is not built here. The server-side risk signal `CHECKOUT_WITHOUT_SUBMIT` stays with backend-core (contract 1.9 row 5).
- **D-TSO-DAYCLOSE-SCREEN-01 (android-tso session 6, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-DAYCLOSE-01).** Sales Submit on the phone: (a) a row is in sync when the server count is at least the phone's (`CountRow.matches = server >= device`); a server count above the phone's is shown as information ("Server has N more … from other phones or earlier installs of this login today" / "সার্ভারে আজ এই লগইনের অন্য ফোন বা আগের ইনস্টল থেকে আরও N টি … আছে।"), never "Not matching"; the phone above the server stays the mismatch, and an unacknowledged row stays the Unsynced block; the table lists every type either side counts (a server-only type shows phone 0). ADAPTER for contract 1.9 queue row 4: `ServerCounts.thisDeviceCounts` / `CountRow.serverThisDevice`, null until backend-core ships the per-device digest; once set the rule compares with this phone's own server count. Wired in the same push to the lead's fixed 1.9 shape (`SalesSubmitDigest` on `GET /v1/sync/totals`, docs/requests/contract-v1.9-shapes-backend-core.md on lane/backend-core) through a LANE-LOCAL parser `core-sync SubmitDigest` (swap to the generated DTO when 1.9 lands): `SubmitDigestSource` reads it when the screen asks (open, Sync data, Submit; one read per 30 s, never offline, never before a token), `DigestServerCounts` feeds `device.received` as this phone's count and the server's `rejected[]` rows this install does not hold (other phones, earlier installs: a non-final parked row of another install shows "waiting on the phone that sent it" and never blocks). A server without the member answers nothing new (1.9.0 keeps `digest` optional: absent means the login-total compare and, before any totals, "Nothing to compare yet"). A read is capped at 5 s and its answer, failure included, kept 30 s, so Submit never waits on a weak link longer than that; a type absent from the digest counts as zero for this phone; a parked row of this phone shows "will be sent again" only while this install still holds it. Until the server answers it the residual risk is a row this phone believes acknowledged but the server lost while other installs of the login pushed the total higher; the server re-checks with the digest resend (F-SYS-080). (b) The rejected, quarantined and retry rows are listed from the phone's own outbox (`RoomDaySource.rejectedRows`: type, memo number from the stored record or the outlet name, the bundle's `reason_texts` bn/en or the bare code, and the outcome: stored and flagged, not stored, or will be sent again); final outcomes never block, a retry row blocks through the existing Unsynced rule. (c) The column header reads "Phone"/"Server" ("ফোন"/"সার্ভার") with count columns weighted 1.5:1:1, proven on one line at 360 dp and font scale 1.3 with native text measurement (the old "Device" wrapped). (d) The list shows with or without a digest; "Nothing to compare yet" only before the first digest, "No records on this phone today" after it. Goldens: `submit_bn_*` re-recorded (header), new `submit_owner_case_bn_light`. Opus checker fixes in the same push: the notes and the list name every record type in words (sale line, sales submit, print, outlet request, task, else "Other record (code)"), never the wire name; a retry row is listed only for a code the contract marks retryable (`RecordOutcomeCode.retryable`), not the phone's own resync markers; a memo line shows its parent memo's number and outlet; a row of another date shows its date. The header test was run against the old "Device" label and old weights as a negative control and failed there.
- **D-TSO-CHECKOUT-SUBMIT-02 (android-tso session 6, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-02/03, lead rulings 23:31 UTC).** (02) Offline the check-out notice reads "অফলাইন — Sales Submit হয়নি। নেটওয়ার্ক ফিরলেই ডেটা নিজে থেকে যাবে; তারপর সাবমিট করে চেক আউট করুন।" / "Offline — Sales Submit not done yet. Your data goes out as soon as the network returns; then submit and check out." (English "not done" rather than "not acknowledged": the gate reads the local submit, and a submit queued offline after every row was acknowledged does open check-out); check-out waits for the network (no bypass), the pending upload of D-TSO-SYNC-NOW-01 goes when one returns, and the check-out stamps the real press time. The late-check-out reason `OFFLINE_WAIT` is server-side: `AttendanceEventPayload` has no reason field, so the phone sends nothing new. (03) NOT applied tonight, returned to the lead: an "every touched route" gate strands the SR, because the Sales Submit screen submits only the chosen route (`RoomDaySubmitWriter` with `meta(0L)`) and refuses a second submit once any submit stands (`AlreadySubmitted`), while visits on off-plan outlets carry routes the picker never offers; the gate stays "a standing submit of the date" (D-TSO-CHECKOUT-SUBMIT-01) until the screen can submit each touched route. Known: the offline flag is read when the screen composes (a resume or a revisit refreshes it).
- **D-PRINT-09 (android-print session 5, 2026-10-24; owner follow-ups after D-PRINT-08).** (1) Every document ends with at least one inch of blank paper after its last row, so tearing never cuts it: every job (raster, bit image and the ASCII test page) ends with `ESC 3 26` + `ESC d 9` (234 dots, about 29 mm: margin over the inch in case a firmware measures `ESC 3` in a coarser unit), and the pacing model counts that feed as print time. Before, the raster job's `ESC 3 0` (line spacing 0, so bands touch) made the closing `ESC d 3` feed next to nothing. The spacing is set explicitly rather than using `ESC J`, which this lane has not tried on the MP-58N; `ESC d` is already proven there. (2) The Aron mark prints above the "AKTCL" header. It is a 74 x 112 dot 1-bit image made once from `assets/brand/aron-logo-original.png` (cropped, Floyd-Steinberg dithered) and bundled as `core-printing/src/main/resources/aron/print/logo.pbm` (P4, about 1 KB), so there is no image decoding on the phone and the JVM gets the same bits. It is placed on top of the rendered paper in `MemoPrinting`, not in the templates, so template versions, `print_event`, reprint markers and the document goldens are unchanged. It adds about 120 rows (about 5.8 KB of raster). It is on by default and can be switched off per printer in Settings → Printer diagnostics (`SavedPrinter.printLogo`, kept when the same printer is picked again). When it is missing or invalid, or switched off, the paper starts with the text header as before. "When the printer accepts it" is decided by the person: the mark is part of the same raster image as the rest of the paper, so a printer that prints the memo prints the mark too.
- **D-MDM-ALLOWLIST-01 (mdm session 3, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-MDM-ALLOWLIST-01).** The work-hours allow-list is an optional member `work_hours_allowlist` {enabled, packages[≤50], schedule {mode calendar|window, days, start, end}, grace_min 0..30 (default 5)} of `MdmPolicyWrite`/`MdmPolicy`, stored in the existing `app.mdm_policy.doc` jsonb (no migration) and delivered inside the signed `apply_policy` params. It is served ahead of contract 1.9 queue row 6 (exact shape in `docs/requests/contract-v1.9-shapes-mdm.md`): the member is left out when absent (`@EncodeDefault(NEVER)`), so a policy without it is the 1.8 shape and conformance is unaffected; the web uses local types until the generated ones carry it. Working hours are an end-after-start span on one day; overnight is refused.
- **D-MDM-ALLOWLIST-02 (mdm session 3, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-MDM-ALLOWLIST-01).** On the phone the allow-list suspends every launcher-visible package, system apps included (YouTube, Chrome, OEM preloads), outside its list, the policy's `allowed_packages`, the kiosk packages and the protected set: the ARON apps (`com.aktcl.aron.*`), the phone's resolved diallers/call handlers, default SMS, launchers, enabled keyboards and Settings screens, plus a fixed list (System UI, Settings, telephony, the common diallers, emergency, cell broadcast, installer, permission controller). It fails open: when the protected set cannot be resolved (or a telephony phone shows no dialler) or, in `calendar` mode, the bundle calendar does not know the date, nothing is blocked and `protected_unresolved` is reported. The grace delays the start of blocking; the end is exact on the schedule. The protected set now also applies to block windows (an explicitly listed dialler or ARON app is never suspended), and the server refuses block-window lists naming an ARON app or a known dialler/telephony/Settings/System UI package.
- **D-MDM-ALLOWLIST-03 (mdm session 3, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-MDM-ALLOWLIST-01).** Window edges use one non-repeating `AlarmManager.setWindow` alarm with a 10-minute window instead of the exact alarm the brief named: `NoPollingLintTest` forbids `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`/`USE_EXACT_ALARM` (docs/04 battery budget), and a plain inexact alarm may slip by most of its lead time. While the phone is in use an edge is at most about 10 minutes late; a dozing phone gets it when the screen comes on; every app start and sync re-evaluate. The runbook and the portal preview say so.
- **D-MDM-ALLOWLIST-04 (mdm session 3, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-MDM-ALLOWLIST-01).** `calendar` mode takes its hours from the policy (`start`/`end`), not from `cfg.day`: the config registry has no working-hours keys (only `checkin_earliest_time`, `checkout_earliest_time` and the device policy's `hard_end_time`), and a per-policy span lets the owner set it in the same card. Working days come from the bundle calendar the app already configures (`DeviceOwnerPolicy.configure`).
- **D-MDM-ALLOWLIST-05 (mdm session 3, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-MDM-ALLOWLIST-01; Opus checker findings).** (1) A check-in or check-out commit now runs the MDM pass right after the attendance engine, so a check-out inside working hours cannot open apps the allow-list or a block window still wants suspended. (2) The MDM edge alarm is set on `ELAPSED_REALTIME_WAKEUP` as a delay from trusted now (at least 60 s), not on the wall clock, so a skewed system clock can neither loop it nor delay a release. (3) The protected set also holds the clock apps (alarm and timer handlers), the SIM toolkit and the WebView provider. (4) Tightened block-window validation (no ARON, dialler or system package) is documented in the 1.9 request as a change to 1.8 behaviour.
- **D-BC-DIGEST-01 (backend-core session 18, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-DAYCLOSE-01).** Contract 1.9 queue row 4 goes on `GET /v1/sync/totals` (the Sales Submit Server column, docs/24 s4.12), not on `POST /v1/sync/digest` (bucket re-send compare, unchanged) and not on `ServerTotals` (repeated in every batch response): `SyncTotalsResponse.digest` = per record type `{device: {received, acknowledged} | null, login: {acknowledged}}` plus `rejected[]` (client_uuid, type, business_date, status rejected/parked/quarantined, code, final, from_this_device, memo_no, outlet_id, last_received_at; at most 200, newest first, `rejected_truncated`). Counts come from `app.ingest_registry` (stored = `accepted`/`voided`; received = any outcome); the device is the token's, the user the token's, never a parameter; memo numbers are looked up only among the caller's own rows. The lead's "device.committed" is named `received` (the server cannot know what the phone committed). Built as `SalesSubmitDigestService` behind local DTOs; the 1.9 batch adds the member and fills it from the principal. Shapes: `docs/requests/contract-v1.9-shapes-backend-core.md`.
- **D-BC-CHECKOUT-RISK-01 (backend-core session 18, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-01).** CHECKOUT_WITHOUT_SUBMIT is judged after the whole batch for each SR/AMO `attendance_event` check-out accepted in it (never a duplicate): no `app.route_day_event` day_submit of the user on the stored row's business date that is not data-voided and whose route-day/supervisor-day is not submit-voided → one `risk_signal` (subject user, severity 2, weight `cfg.geo.integrity_weight` or 20, evidence checkout uuid and time), ON CONFLICT per user-day. The check-out is stored unchanged; the insert runs in its own transaction after the batch and a failure is logged only. Switch: on when `ContractCatalogues.RiskSignalCode` names the code and the database's `risk_signal_code_check` accepts it (needs a db row: the constraint value and the weight default); until both, nothing is raised. Known gap: a Sales Submit uploaded in a later batch than the check-out leaves the signal open for a reviewer.
- **D-BC-FRESH-01 (backend-core session 18, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-SYNC-01 server part).** Accepted memo → dashboard on dev: the memo's `memo.created` outbox row commits with the memo (DomainEventProducer, same savepoint); the worker (`AggregationWorker.start`, fixed delay 5 s after each pass) projects it, rebuilds the route-day and then the zone-day in the same pass (`drain` order route, device, zone) and stamps `dw.summary_freshness`, so `dw` is current ≤ 5 s + one pass after the commit (≤ 10 s + passes for an outbox id that commits out of order, re-read next pass); dev has no read replica (`postgresReadReplica = false`); `/v1/app/home` (the slice smoke tile) and the national dashboard read `dw` uncached. The one step over 15 s was the API's own `DashboardService` cache (summary, geo-validation): 30 s → 5 s (docs/25 F-API-014 said 30 s; lead to update the backlog line). The slice smoke now polls the tile every 2 s and reports the seconds from the accepted sale to the tile. Outside backend: the phone's 5 s debounce and the web's 30 s refetch (D-NIGHT-SYNC-01) add to what a person sees; tiles join `dw.dim_geo`, which the worker refreshes every 10 minutes, so a sale on a route created in the last 10 minutes waits for that refresh (rare: routes change by admin action, not in the field).
- **D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-02 (lead, 2026-10-24 23:32 UTC; android-tso #6 slice B item 1).** Pilot rule for an SR who is offline at day end: check-out WAITS for connectivity. The owner's rule is "no check-out without Sales Submit" and Sales Submit needs the server's acknowledgement (D-NIGHT-DAYCLOSE-01 (a)), so the gate stays shown with the reason ("অফলাইন — Sales Submit হয়নি" / "Offline — Sales Submit not yet acknowledged"); the pending network-constrained job of slice A uploads the moment a network returns; the check-out stamps the real press time; attendance accepts a late check-out with reason OFFLINE_WAIT so it is visible, not punished; no emergency bypass. The D-383 offline-queued submit (queue the submit and let the check-out follow automatically when acknowledged) is a pre-pilot follow-up slice for the SR app lane, not tonight's; the owner chooses between the two readings in the morning report.
- **D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-03 (lead, 2026-10-24 23:32 UTC; android-tso #6 slice B item 2).** (withdrawn by D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-04) With several routes on one business date the check-out gate requires Sales Submit for EVERY route that has a visit, sale, dues collection or stock movement on this phone that day; routes untouched that day are not required. RouteDayLock keeps its "any route submitted" meaning for its own purpose; the gate computes "all touched routes submitted". android-tso folds this into the slice C head with D-TSO-CHECKOUT-SUBMIT-02 and a two-route test (one submitted, one not → gate closed).
- **D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-04 (lead, 2026-10-24 23:55 UTC; android-tso #6 slice C checker finding; supersedes D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-03).** D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-03 ("every touched route submitted") is withdrawn: the Sales Submit screen submits the business date once (one chosen route label; a second submit is refused as AlreadySubmitted), so "every touched route" would strand an SR who touched two routes, and off-plan outlet visits carry routes the picker never offers. The check-out gate stays "a standing Sales Submit of the business date" (D-TSO-CHECKOUT-SUBMIT-01). Completeness is still guaranteed, because the submit's compare is login-level (every row of this login and date acknowledged by the server, D-NIGHT-DAYCLOSE-01 (a)), not per route. Per-route submits for multi-route days are a product question for the owner, not a night slice. Wording: the offline notice says "Sales Submit not done yet" in English (the gate reads the local submit); OFFLINE_WAIT is derived server-side from the timestamps (no payload field; a payload reason is a 1.10 queue row if wanted).
- **D-DB-SEED-OUTLETS-01 (db session 11, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-SEED-OUTLETS-01).** Seed 06 (dev only) adds the owner's three test outlets on GUL-SR-1 (sr1001's Sunday and Wednesday route): `GUL1-T01` Asef Home 23.788347, 90.402357; `GUL1-T02` Asef Office 23.791886, 90.420650; `GUL1-T03` Asef Head Office 23.795805, 90.418034. Retail GT (`GT-GROCERY`), price type `outlet`, cluster Gulshan 1 Circle, `location_basis` master (confirmed, 8 m), visit order 21-23 after GUL1-001..020, placement and location history through the seed's existing guarded inserts; no outlet-scope `cfg.geo.radius_m` override, so the geofence radius resolves as for every other outlet. Codes `GUL1-T0n` cannot collide with the generated `GUL1-0nn`; owner name "Asef (test outlet)", phone numbers synthetic. Idempotent (NOT EXISTS on the code). The repository is public, so the pins are public (lead's privacy note to the owner). GUL-SR-1 now has 23 seeded outlets (24 on dev with SMOKE-SR-002); the one literal `DataFlowTraceTest.kt:72` (GUL-SR-1:18:21 -> 24) is edited by db in the same commit (lead ruling D-NIGHT-SEED-OUTLETS-02).

Durga Puja stays the single named row on 2026-10-21 that the owner still confirms (owner row O); no later year's lunar date is guessed. D-NIGHT-SEED-OUTLETS-01 is the lead's ruling in `docs/requests/owner-2026-10-24-night.md` item 6 (lane/lead-docs-1022); D-NIGHT-SEED-OUTLETS-02 is the lead's message to db #11 at 22:45 UTC (db edits the one literal).
- **D-CONTRACT-1-9-01 (lead, 2026-10-24 23:25 UTC; contract 1.9.0 batch).** Contract 1.9.0 applied as one lead head per D-CONTRACT-BATCH-01: rows 1 to 5 of `docs/requests/contract-v1.9-queue.md`. (1) `MemoView.memo_no` is `$ref MemoNo` (was an inline string of at most 40 characters). (2) `MemoView.route_id` is `[integer, null]`, so `GET /v1/memos` can list route-less memos with a left join. (3) `getFlaggedMemos` names the national-level roles (NATIONAL, TOP, ANALYST) among its callers, matching the server's `DASHBOARD_ROLES`; description only, and no other dashboard operation names a caller list. (4) `SyncTotalsResponse.digest` (optional in 1.9.0 because the server does not send it yet and the conformance suite would otherwise go red; a later batch makes it required; `SalesSubmitDigest`, `DigestTypeCounts`, `DigestDeviceCounts`, `DigestLoginCounts`, `RejectedRecordView`) exactly as backend-core #18 wrote it (`docs/requests/contract-v1.9-shapes-backend-core.md`, `lane/backend-core` 9ce9270): per-device and per-login counts and the login's rows not stored, on the GET totals answer only (not `ServerTotals`; no `GET /v1/sync/digest`) (D-NIGHT-DAYCLOSE-01). (5) `RiskSignalCode` gains `CHECKOUT_WITHOUT_SUBMIT` (x-extensible-enum; s11.4 row, weight 20) (D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-01). Breaking: the widened `MemoView.memo_no` and `route_id` response members are breaking for an old reader under oasdiff; approved by the version bump plus the changed queue file (CI gate rule). Row 6 (mdm packages catalogue, `work_hours_allowlist`) is row 1 of `docs/requests/contract-v1.10-queue.md` (shape file from mdm #3 pending; the lead shipped 1.9.0 without waiting for it). Wiring: backend-core (left join in `MemoApi`, the digest, the check-out signal; not yet on `lane/backend-core`, which holds the shapes file only at 9ce9270; `SyncTotalsResponse` is a hand-written DTO in `BatchDtos.kt`), db (`CHECKOUT_WITHOUT_SUBMIT` in `app.risk_signal_code_check` and `cfg.geo.integrity_weight`), android (Sales Submit digest and rejected list; a label for the new code where risk codes are labelled), web-dashboard and web-admin (nullable `route_id` where `MemoView` is read). Server before apps (R18).
- **D-DB-RISK-CHECKOUT-NO-SUBMIT-01 (db session 11, 2026-10-24; D-NIGHT-CHECKOUT-01, lead row 22:45 UTC, backend-core #18 shapes row 5).** V0114 replaces `app.risk_signal`'s `risk_signal_code_check` (same name, the V0077 list unchanged + `CHECKOUT_WITHOUT_SUBMIT`) NOT VALID and adds `{"CHECKOUT_WITHOUT_SUBMIT": 20}` to the `cfg.geo.integrity_weight` registry default (guarded, as V0077); V0115 validates. Severity 2 is the row's own column, written by the server; nothing in db stores it. A saved `cfg_value` of the weight map (none on dev today) would not gain the key: the server's lookup falls back. The server's switch can find the code with `pg_get_constraintdef` on `conrelid = 'app.risk_signal'::regclass` (the name is not a relation). Next free migration V0116.
- **D-BC-DIGEST-WIRE-01 (backend-core session 19, 2026-10-25; contract 1.9.0, D-NIGHT-DAYCLOSE-01, D-BC-DIGEST-01).** `GET /v1/sync/totals` always carries `digest` (`SalesSubmitDigest`, optional in 1.9.0; v1.10 row 3 makes it required): `IngestService.totals(userId, deviceId, deviceUuid, date)` fills it from `SalesSubmitDigestService` in the same read handle, the device half from the principal's device proof only (`AronPrincipal.deviceId`/`deviceUuid`; a web token or a device not bound to the user gets `device_uuid` null and every `device` null). The DTOs stay hand-written in backend/sync (as `BatchDtos.kt`); `ContractConformanceTest` validates the served shape. `SalesSubmitDigestTest` reads the digest over HTTP and checks it equals the service's answer for the token's device.
- **D-BC-MEMO-ROUTELESS-01 (backend-core session 19, 2026-10-25; contract 1.9.0 rows 1–2).** `GET /v1/memos` lists memos stored without a route (left join, `MemoView.route_id` null). Reach for such a memo: the writer, or the outlet's current route in the caller's routes, or that route's zone (else the outlet's zone) in the caller's zones; national roles see all. A routed memo's reach is unchanged (its own route and that route's current zone). Current outlet placement, not the placement on the business date as `dw.v_memo_flags` uses: the memo history is "memos of this outlet in my scope" (F-SR-054), so the outlet's present owner sees its past memos.
- **D-BC-CHECKOUT-ON-01 (backend-core session 19, 2026-10-25; D-BC-CHECKOUT-RISK-01, D-DB-RISK-CHECKOUT-NO-SUBMIT-01).** CHECKOUT_WITHOUT_SUBMIT is always on: the catalogue (contract 1.9.0) and the constraint (db V0114/V0115, on dev with train 37) both carry it, so the switch that waited for both (catalogue check, `pg_constraint` probe re-asked every 60 s, test hook `checkoutRiskOverride`) is removed. Each check-out's evaluation still runs in its own transaction with its failure logged, so a database without V0114 logs a constraint error per check-out and never fails the batch; the weight falls back to 20 when a saved weight map lacks the key.
- **D-BC-DW-BOTHDATES-01 (backend-core session 19, 2026-10-25; data-architecture review findings 6 and 18, actions row 5; built by session 18, `docs/requests/backend-core-19-bothdates-threshold.patch`).** (a) The dw projector (`AggregationWorker.project`, `earlierMemoDays`) also dirties the route-day of the memo a void or edit ends, whatever the event's date and route: `memo.voided` reads the voided memo named by `aggregate_id`, an editing memo's `memo.created` reaches the edited memo through `supersedes_client_uuid`; both are lookups on `UNIQUE (client_uuid, business_date)` (no scan; other event types cost nothing), each statement binds only the names it uses (the session 18 checker's blocking item: `:d` was bound on the void query that has none); a route-less memo has no route-day and is skipped. Tests `AggregationTest.aVoidOnTheNextBusinessDateRebuildsTheSalesDayToo` and `anEditOnTheNextBusinessDateRebuildsTheEditedMemosDay` (both fail without the change). (b) The worker role builds `AggregationWorker` with `DbServerConfig` over the registry defaults (`Main.kt`), so the rebuild reads the admin's `cfg.geo.suspicious_score_threshold` as dashboards and reports do (was the constant 50); test `AggregationTest.theWorkerReadsTheAdminsSuspiciousThreshold` builds the worker the same way and shows a saved threshold of 20 turns a score-20 user-day suspicious (the `Main.kt` line itself is wiring, not unit-tested).
- **D-QA-CI-HANG-01 (qa session 3, 2026-10-25; lead hand-off 2026-10-25 00:39/00:45, ruled by the lead).** The two lane runs that sat in "Shared, db and backend" (31 and 50 minutes) were in `:backend:analytics:test`, not at a database wait. (1) That task runs in its own parallel job `jvm-analytics` ("Backend analytics tests", 45 minutes, its own PostgreSQL service); the `jvm` job excludes it (`-x :backend:analytics:test`), so every test still runs exactly once. Chosen over `maxParallelForks`: measured locally on 4 cores, 3 forks took 36 min 43 s against 38 min 35 s for 1 (the suite is CPU-bound on the per-class database migrations, forks only share the same cores); a second runner is the real parallelism. (2) Step `timeout-minutes: 45` and job `timeout-minutes: 60` on `jvm` (was 75), 35 on the analytics step, 40 on the app step. (3) Every JVM test task prints `TEST-CLASS START/END <class> (<ms>, <result>, <n> tests)` (shared convention in the root `build.gradle.kts`, lead-approved; class level, not per method), so an overrun is attributed from the log. (4) `junit.jupiter.execution.timeout.default=5m` on `:backend:analytics:test` (per test and per `@BeforeAll`; the slowest class takes 4.4 minutes in all). `Backend analytics tests` joins the required checks in `tools/github-governance.ps1` (the lead re-applies the protection). No test skipped, disabled or loosened. Finding for the db/platform owners: a class with one test takes 11-13 s, all of it the Flyway migration of its fresh database (`FreshDb.create`); a migrated template database (`CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE`) would cut most of the 38 minutes of the analytics suite (and the app and db suites the same way): not done here (shared fixture, not in the slice).
- **D-QA-DATE-PIN-GATE-01 (qa session 3, 2026-10-25; D-TEST-DATE-PIN-01 follow-up, lead hand-off 2026-10-24 19:25).** `tools/ci/date-pin-scan.py` (a step of Repository gates, self-tested in `tools/ci/test_gates.py`) fails when a test under `backend/`, `db/` or `android/` that uses the dev seed (a non-comment line mentions `06_dev_gulshan_banani`, `SeedLoader`, `DataFlowTrace`, `devProfile`, `GUL-SR-` or `BAN-SR-`) holds a `2026-MM-DD` literal in code. Passes: a derived day, `// date-pin-ok: <reason>` on the line or above, `tools/ci/date-pin-allow.txt` (the holidays the seed names: 2026-10-21, 2026-12-16, 2026-12-25, each with a reason), or a row of the shrink-only `tools/ci/date-pin-baseline.txt`. `sr1001` and `SMOKE-SR-` are not markers (they come from the static seeds 01/05). On the current tree: 106 literals in 11 files, all baselined (27 path/literal rows; `T..Z` timestamps count, `//` inside a string is code), none fixed by qa; per-lane list in `docs/requests/date-pin-offenders-2026-10-25.md`; a stale baseline row is a notice, a new literal an error.
- **D-LEAD-STALL-01 (lead, 2026-10-27 19:10 UTC; the fleet stalled when the account's usage credits ran out).** From about 08:06 UTC 2026-10-25 the lead session could not run a turn ("out of usage credits"); the lanes stopped by 12:50 UTC the same day; the seven-day allowance reset at 18:00 UTC 2026-10-27 and the lead resumed at 18:52 UTC. No work was lost (every candidate sat on its lane branch) but no train landed for 58 hours and the evening report of 2026-10-25 and the morning report of 2026-10-26 were never written. Pacing from now, until the owner says otherwise (owner row Y): the fleet spends at most one seventh of the weekly allowance per day; at most two Opus lanes and one Sonnet lane build at a time plus the Sonnet integrator; checkers are Sonnet except for T1 code (money, sync, security, geofence, migrations, infra, Bangla, print), which keeps the Opus checker (docs/29); the lead does the lead-docs pass itself; the two-hourly lane check reads and ends when nothing is queued; every evening report states the usage position. If the owner wants the earlier pace, the remedy is extra usage on the account, which is the owner's billing call.
- **D-DB-DATA-ORIGIN-01 (db session 12, 2026-10-25; D-DATA-ARCH-REVIEW-01, lead action row 1, review High 3).** V0116 adds `app.data_origin` (enum `production` < `pilot` < `test` < `seed`; GREATEST/max gives the most synthetic contributor). `app.outlet`, `app.route`, `app.app_user` gain `data_origin NOT NULL DEFAULT 'production'` (real rows are production unless tagged; the existing `app_user.pilot` flag is NOT mapped to `pilot`, so no real account changes class silently). Every app table of retention class `transaction` plus `geo_fix` and `geo_breadcrumb` (47 tables, registry `app.data_origin_capture`; exempt only `entry_unlock` and `media_upload`, which have no row owner) gains `data_origin`, stamped by a BEFORE INSERT trigger that overwrites any value the writer sent: the most synthetic of the row's users (`user_id`, `acting_for_user_id`, `entered_by_user_id`, `assessed_user_id`, `assigned_user_id`, `acting_user_id`, `assignee_user_id`, `submitted_by`, `entered_by`, `chosen_by_user_id`; approvers such as `decided_by` do not count), its outlet, its route and, for the 12 child tables (memo line, discount, void, print, QC line, check/answer/redemption/web-entry/QC-summary lines, task event, feedback status), its parent row; none known -> production. Frozen at capture; only `app.restamp_data_origin(from date)` re-derives it after a master is re-tagged. The function runs with the caller's rights and `app.apply_db_role_grants()` lets the API, worker and job roles execute it like every app function, so the protection is the guard: `app.guard_data_origin` refuses any change of a set value (42501) unless the role owns the table or is superuser (migrations, the dev seed, the move runbook). That protection is real only where the apps sign in with their own logins (`dbPerAppLogins`, today only the dev test account); in the dev-lite, stage and prod templates the apps sign in as the owning admin login and the guard cannot tell them from a migration (review finding 12, infra row 7). The owner-but-not-superuser path runs on the dev deploy itself (the migration's closing restamp and the seed); local tests run as superuser. the write-once guards list the column and the three append-only guards (`due_ledger`, `loyalty_ledger`, `qc_summary_entry_line`) were replaced by `app.deny_mutation_except_origin`, which lets only that column move. A re-tag does not restamp by itself (run the function). A child that arrives before its parent takes only its own keys until the next restamp. No NOT NULL constraint on the capture columns (partitioned parents cannot take NOT VALID constraints); the trigger never writes NULL and the migration's closing restamp fills old rows. **dw, no backend change:** the 9 facts, the 5 route aggregates, `route_last_position`, the 2 outlet aggregates and `dim_geo`/`dim_outlet`/`dim_user` gain `data_origin`, set by BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE triggers (the Aggregator's explicit column lists never name it): a fact copies its source row's frozen origin (`fact_memo` from `app.memo`, `fact_visit` from `app.visit`; keys only when the source is missing) or takes its user/route keys; a route-day or outlet-day aggregate takes the most synthetic of its route (and outlet) and of the memos, visits, stock movements and collections behind it, so `production` means nothing non-production went in (a real route-day with one test sale reads `test`; exact splits come from the fact-level views of row 4). Zone, hourly-zone and screen-use aggregates carry none (no view reads them; a zone has no origin). Every `dw.v_*` over captured or master rows ends with `data_origin` (CREATE OR REPLACE, additive, grants kept); per-user views and `v_final_submit_log` take max() over their rows, `v_device_command` the most synthetic user actively bound to the device (not frozen), `v_route_monthly_target` its route; the calendar views are reference data and unchanged. **Seed:** 01 tags the seven test accounts `test` and the Mirpur fixture routes `seed`, 03 the Mirpur outlets `seed`, 06 (dev) the Gulshan-Banani routes and every outlet in Z-GUL/Z-BAN `test` (GUL1-T01..T03 included; outlets added later on those routes, such as the smoke outlets, on the next seed run), new 08 runs the restamp. On dev the deploy migrates first (restamp: every row production) and the seed job then tags and restamps. Tests: `DataOriginTest` (stamp, parent, forged payload, guard as `api_rw`, restamp, registry coverage, dw facts/aggregates/dims, counts by origin app = dw, every view, backfill of pre-V0116 rows), `SeedGulshanBananiTest` (dev tags). `dw.scd2_track` (V0043) is replaced so that only columns the `_version` table keeps open a version: without it the backfill and every re-tag would have opened content-identical versions of `dim_geo`/`dim_outlet`/`dim_user` and moved the facts' version keys (Opus checker blocker). Costs, honest: the restamp rewrites every dw row of the range (no change filter) (`app.touch_master` is replaced so that a write changing only `data_origin` keeps `version` and `updated_at`: a seed or runbook re-tag never breaks an admin If-Match, never re-sends outlets or routes to phones as a bundle delta, never moves a restamped `route_day`/`supervisor_day`/`gift_assignment` version; found by `ContractConformanceTest`, whose harness PATCHes the seeded outlets and routes with If-Match "1"); each route or outlet aggregate write runs up to four indexed max() lookups. Fine at pilot size; at fleet size compute once per route-day and filter the restamp (follow-up before staging). Not tagged: `dues_adjustment`, `outlet_request_event`, `submit_void_event`, `user_consent`, `device_status_report`, `route_assignment` (audit, telemetry and master links, no row owner of their own). The move runbook change (drop test and seed rows, reconcile by origin, handle references from kept rows to dropped ones) is infra's: `docs/requests/infra-move-runbook-data-origin.md`.
- **D-DB-EVENT-STATUS-01 (db session 12, 2026-10-25; lead row 3 06:37 UTC, D-DATA-OUTBOX-ROLE-01, review Medium 5).** V0117 adds enum `app.domain_event_status` (`live`, `planned`) and `app.domain_event_type.status NOT NULL DEFAULT 'planned'` (a type catalogued later is planned until its producer ships). Live: `memo.created`, `memo.voided`, `visit.closed`, `stock.moved` (the lead's docs-pass grep, confirmed in `DomainEventProducer.kt` and `DataVoidApi.kt`); planned: `day_exception.decided`, `due.collected`, `outlet.changed`, `risk_signal.changed`, `route_day.state_changed`, `target.revised`, `tracking_action.created` and the new `memo.superseded` v1 (aggregate `memo`, required `memo_uuid`, `superseded_by_uuid`, `route_id` (integer or null: a memo may have no route), `business_date`; optional `superseding_business_date`; required keys not enforced, V0018 rule). The V0017 catalogue guard now lets `status` change (a producer ships or is withdrawn); the published schema stays fixed. `docs/data-events.md` renders `live`/`planned` in its Status column (`deprecated` still wins) plus the thin-notice rule; regenerated with `tools/data-dictionary/render.sh`. A backend lane that ships a producer asks db for the one-row `UPDATE ... SET status = 'live'` migration in the same train.
- **D-DB-REUSE-SURFACE-01 (db session 12, 2026-10-25; D-DATA-ARCH-REVIEW-01, lead action row 4, review High 4 and Low 13, 14, 15).** V0118. (1) `dw.fact_memo_line` (one row per line, partitioned by `business_date`, `app.partition_policy` event_fact) is kept in step with `dw.fact_memo` by an AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE row trigger on the memo fact (`dw.sync_fact_memo_lines`): every write of a memo fact rewrites its lines from `app.memo_line` (scope keys and status from the memo fact, `data_origin` from the line), so the projector needs no change and a route-day rebuild rebuilds its lines; an AFTER TRUNCATE statement trigger empties the line facts with the memo facts (a wipe and rebuild, as `AggregationTest` does; Opus checker blocker), and the rewrite also deletes by the new key first; backfilled by touching every memo fact. Cost, honest: every projector upsert of a memo fact rewrites its lines even when nothing changed (dead rows); fine at pilot size, skip unchanged memos before the fleet move (row 9 batch). (2) New stable views, no names of people, no phone numbers, no coordinates, no photos, each with `data_origin` and `is_effective`: `v_memo` (memo UUID, not the printed number), `v_memo_line`, `v_visit`, `v_due_ledger` (no memo number, no free-text note), `v_stock_movement`, `v_outlet_sku_daily`, `v_dim_geo`, `v_dim_outlet` (shop name and code, no owner), `v_dim_user` (no names or logins), `v_dim_product` (`data_origin` constant production: products carry none). Column comments are copied from the source columns. (3) `is_effective` appended last on every `dw.v_*`: true for aggregate and calendar rows; `v_memo` active with at least one line (the dashboards' memo count); `v_memo_line` memo active and line not voided; `v_visit`/`v_stock_movement` not voided; `v_memo_flags` active, not data-voided and with a line (as `v_memo`); `v_outlet_masked` and the dimensions active; `v_device_command` not rejected, expired or failed; `v_final_submit_log` some submit still standing. (4) `dw.view_contract_version()` = `1.0`; `docs/dw-view-contract.md` pins every view's columns with their types, rendered by `DwViewContractTest` (in `tools/data-dictionary/render.sh`), whose regeneration fails unless an added column comes with a higher minor version and a removed, renamed or retyped one with a higher major version; the test also keeps a version -> SHA-256 history of the column table, so a change without a new version fails even if the file is edited by hand. (5) The printed memo number (contains the login) is `[pii:personal]` on `app.memo`, `app.memo_void`, `app.sale_abort`, `app.due_ledger`, `app.due_collection.against_memo_no`, `dw.fact_memo`, `dw.v_memo_flags`, whose table classes follow; `v_memo_flags` keeps the column for the flagged-memos endpoint (API role) and for `web_ro`; `bi_reader` no longer reads it: `app.apply_db_role_grants()` is replaced so a `v_*` map row honours `except_tables` (V0020's generator plus that one condition), bi_reader's `v_*` row skips `v_memo_flags` and a new row grants that view without `memo_no` (column grants), every other view stays a whole-table grant. (6) `dw.agg_reconcile` (grain route/zone, metric, source vs aggregate value, generated diff, consecutive runs, re-enqueued, resolved_at; one open finding per date, grain, key and metric by a unique partial index) and `dw.agg_restatement_log` (grain, keys, reason of docs/16 s8.7, old/new/delta jsonb, rebuild_id; append-only) for backend-reports' rebuild and reconcile slice; worker writes both, outside readers see neither; unpartitioned at pilot size (row 9 batch). db tests: `ReuseSurfaceTest`, `DwViewContractTest`; `RetentionPolicyTest`, `DataOriginTest`, `EntrySourceV0077Test` follow the additions.
- **D-DB-MASTER-HISTORY-01 (db session 13, 2026-10-27 21:40 UTC; D-DATA-ARCH-REVIEW-01, lead action row 9 item 2, review Medium 9).** V0119 gives the master columns that are overwritten in place a past: ten app-side history tables in the `app.route_zone_history` pattern (period per Asia/Dhaka business date, `valid_from` null = since ever, `valid_to` exclusive, gist exclusion, one open row, `data_origin`): `division_history` (wing_id, status), `territory_history` (division_id, status), `house_history` (territory_id, status), `zone_history` (territory_id, house_id, status), `cluster_history` (zone_id, cluster_type, status), `route_history` (kind, visit_kind, route_class, status; the zone stays in `route_zone_history`), `outlet_class_history` (zone_id, channel, sub_channel_id, geo_class, outlet_kind, price_type, status; route and cluster stay in `outlet_placement_history`), `product_node_history` (parent_id, sales_enable, status), `sku_history` (variant_id, category_code, base_unit, base_per_pack, pack_type, entry_unit_default, report_unit, report_factor, sales_enable, status), `user_history` (role, designation, home_zone_id, status). Registry `app.master_history_map` pins master, key, tracked columns and audit entities (owner-guarded, it steers the trigger). `app.master_history_track()` (SECURITY DEFINER, search_path pg_temp last; the update trigger fires only WHEN a tracked column or data_origin differs) opens a period on the date of a real change; a no-op write, an untracked column or a data_origin re-tag opens none (a re-tag copies the master's origin onto all its periods); a second change the same date rewrites that period, a same-date revert reopens the previous one. History tables refuse direct writes from every role but the owner (`app.guard_master_history`, V0116's owner check), since `apply_db_role_grants` gives api_rw INSERT/UPDATE on every app table. Seeding: `app.rebuild_master_history()` walks `app.audit_log` before-images newest first from the current row. **Recovered:** every audited change of a tracked column (admin API geography/cluster/route/outlet/user updates, outlet import updates, outlet-kind batches, house merges, product node and SKU edits), dated by the audit row's business date. **Not recoverable:** changes the audit log never saw (migrations such as V0104's lighter pack 1 -> 25, seed SQL, direct SQL, ingest-side writes): such a change is attributed to the date of the nearest older audited change of the row that records the column (since ever when none) and an audited value it replaced can be lost (for SKU and product node, whose audit rows are full snapshots, any SKU edit before V0104 would date the pack change to that edit); an unreadable before-image stops the walk for that row. The rebuild locks the master and replaces the whole table (a re-run drops trigger-made periods): for V0119 and an empty account only. dw: `v_dim_geo_history` (route -> zone -> house/territory -> division -> wing by range intersection), `v_dim_outlet_history`, `v_dim_product_history` (SKU -> variant -> brand -> segment -> category), `v_dim_user_history`, each with valid_from/valid_to/is_current, data_origin and is_effective (status active in the period); neighbours can carry equal values when an unshown column changed. View contract 1.1 (minor bump: views added; the brief's "keeps 1.0" conflicts with DwViewContractTest, which requires a minor bump for an added view). Not done here: rekeying `dw.dim_*_version` and the Aggregator onto these tables (review High 1, backend-reports). db test `MasterHistoryTest`.
- **D-DB-REGISTRY-PRUNE-01 (db session 13, 2026-10-27 23:30 UTC; lead action row 9 item 3, review Medium 10, docs/16 s6.1).** V0120 `app.prune_ingest_registry(p_now, p_limit)` deletes at most p_limit settled (`accepted`, `rejected`) registry rows last seen before `p_now - app.ingest_registry_days(p_now)`, oldest first, through a partial index on `last_seen_at`; candidates are taken as an array (InitPlan + per-partition primary-key lookups, never a full scan) and the delete re-checks status and `last_seen_at` on the locked row, so a replay that touches the row while the prune waits keeps it. Kept for ever: `voided` tombstones (an admin void keeps refusing the uuid), `quarantined` and `parked` rows (released by update), and accepted memo uuids with no `app.memo` row of their own (matched to a paper memo; `memoAlias` follows their `server_id`). `app.ingest_registry_days()` = greatest of the global `cfg.retention.ingest_registry_days` in force (else its default 45), the key's minimum 30, and the largest `cfg.sync.max_backdate_days` in force (or its default) + 31: the key's rule "must exceed max_backdate_days + 30" is not enforced by the config editor, so the database enforces it. The worker's `RetentionJob` (backend/sync, step 5) runs 1,000-row chunks, each its own transaction under a 60 s statement timeout, until a chunk comes back short, one fails (the committed count is kept) or 10 minutes pass; idempotent. jobs_rw reaches DELETE through worker_rw. Not done: the archive export job (D-DB-PART-01 window). Tests: `RetentionJobTest` (chunks, kept rows, floor, race, jobs_rw).
- **D-DB-MEMO-LINE-DIFF-01 (db session 13, 2026-10-28 00:50 UTC; lead 00:28 item 1, fleet follow-up (a) of the session 12 handover).** V0121 replaces `dw.sync_fact_memo_lines()`: instead of deleting and re-inserting every line of a memo on each `dw.fact_memo` write (the projector upserts every memo of a rebuilt route-day, changed or not, so at fleet volume millions of dead line rows a day), it deletes only lines `app.memo_line` no longer has under the memo's key, and upserts each source line that differs from its fact row (identical lines are filtered out before the upsert, so they are neither rewritten nor row-locked; `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... WHERE` differs stays as a guard; updated_at stamped only on a change). An unchanged memo writes no new line version and takes no line lock; `app.restamp_data_origin` now skips dw tables without an origin trigger (`dw.fact_memo_line` follows its memo fact through the diff); a memo delete or key move still removes the old key's lines; the TRUNCATE trigger is unchanged. Same contents as before (ReuseSurfaceTest's existing cases unchanged); new case proves unchanged rebuilds keep every line's ctid, a voided line or a restamped line is the only one rewritten, and a stale row under another memo is moved back, not duplicated. Opus checker PASS (should-fixes taken: no row locks, restamp pass, wider test). Fleet follow-up (b) (V0116 aggregate origin lookups, restamp range) stays open.

- **D-INFRA-LAKE-01 (infra session 7, 2026-10-28 09:23 UTC; action row 7, review 2026-10-25 s4 and findings 11, 12).** Lake and blobs at pilot size. (1) A separate data-lake account `infra/modules/lake.bicep` (name `st<prefix><env>lake<suffix>`, StorageV2, hierarchical namespace, Hot, TLS 1.2, no shared key, no public blob access, no SFTP or local users, soft delete 14 days, no versioning (not available with HNS), no lifecycle delete, tag `component=lake`) with container `lake`; `lakeStorageSku` is `Standard_LRS` in dev and dev-lite (no new zone redundancy under the docs/28 dev exception) and set to `Standard_ZRS` in stage and prod because the final lake will also hold archived partitions past the 13-month hot window (only copy), to be confirmed with the sponsor before the move; only the worker identity holds a data role there (Storage Blob Data Contributor); the worker gets `ARON_LAKE_ACCOUNT` and `ARON_LAKE_CONTAINER`. (2) `exports` container in the photo account with a 7-day delete rule and `ARON_BLOB_CONTAINER_EXPORTS=exports` in the api and worker common environment (the variable `ExportWiring.kt` already reads). (3) Photo delete rule at 730 days, the figure of `cfg.retention.media_days` in docs/21 s4.8 and docs/16 s13.1 (docs/28 and docs/31 name none); it is scoped to `media/photos/` because the media container also holds admin `assets/` and `support/` bundles; the docs' exemptions (an active outlet's reference photo, `app.retention_hold`) cannot be expressed by a prefix rule and are asked of backend-core in `docs/requests/backend-core-photo-retention-exemptions.md` (latent until the first photo is 730 days old). (4) Database logins `app_bi` and `lake_export`, both members of the existing V0014 privilege role `bi_reader` (SELECT on `dw.v_*`, no `memo_no`, plus EXECUTE on the `dw` functions, all invoker rights; a login cannot reuse the role's name, so the BI login is `app_bi`), passwords generated once into Key Vault `aron-db-pw-app-bi` and `aron-db-pw-lake-export` by `db-login-secrets.sh` exactly like the other logins, read URLs `aron-db-bi-read-url` and `aron-db-lake-read-url`; `runtime-logins.sql` now fails the dblogins job if either login reaches anything beyond SELECT on `dw.v_*` in app, dw, stg or public, may CREATE there, may execute a SECURITY DEFINER function there, the same in any schema a non-superuser owns (extension objects excluded), or reads no `dw.v_*` view; the worker gets `ARON_LAKE_DB_URL` (lake_export) only when per-app logins are on, never an admin URL, so the export (row 8) must refuse to run without it. (5) `reset-to-profile.sh` compares the lake account with `lakeStorageSku`, not `storageSku`, so a ZRS photo profile never dooms the LRS lake. Proven locally: validate.sh green, runtime-logins.sql twice on PostgreSQL 16 with every migration in db/migrations applied and every negative grant tried caught (including by the independent checker); Azure apply happens on the next INT deploy (test account).
- **D-REPORTS-REBUILD-01 (backend-reports session 6, 2026-10-28 10:00 UTC; review High 2, Medium 6 and 8, action row 3).** (1) `RebuildService.rebuild(from, to, scope)`: ADMIN and SUPERADMIN only, scope named (national, zones or routes) and inside the caller's reach, at most 93 days, never the future; it marks the route-days dirty with reason `rebuild:<uuid>` and writes one `dw_rebuild` audit row in the same transaction; no HTTP surface (the contract has no admin path for it: v1.10 queue row 4). (2) `dw.agg_restatement_log` (append-only) gets a row when a rebuild changes a route-day or zone-day of a CLOSED Dhaka date (before today), with old/new/delta of counts and milli-taka only and the reason mapped from the dirty key (`rebuild`, `reconcile`, `memo_voided`, else `late_batch`); other grains are not written yet; a stale-scan mark no longer overwrites a queued `rebuild:` or `reconcile` reason. (3) `Reconciler` (nightly from 01:00 Dhaka, once per date via the `dw-reconcile` consumer row) compares the last 7 closed days: route-days against `app.memo`/`app.visit`, zone-days against their route-days, metrics `memo_count, gross_mtk, net_mtk, visit_count` (`qty_base` is not compared, qa-1); one open finding per date/grain/key/metric, re-enqueued with reason `reconcile`, an alert (ERROR log) when it survives a second run, closed when the difference is gone; keys already queued are skipped. (4) A failing key backs off 5 s doubling to 15 min and goes dead (`dead_at`) after 8 attempts; a dead key is not claimed; `redrive(kind, subject, date)` or new data (`mark_dirty`) revives it; `app.v_dirty_key_dead` shows them. The memo-void both-dates dirtying is D-BC-DW-BOTHDATES-01 (reused). Not done: phone-save-to-tile lag gauges and month snapshots (own slices), an alert channel beyond the log.
